# Audit of the register's calculations (opus workflow wf_958e9aa8-861, 2026-09-06)

Six auditors by dimension, each finding checked by two independent verifiers (recompute; units, axes and assumptions). A finding is listed as CONFIRMED when at least one verifier confirmed it and none refuted it, REFUTED when a verifier refuted it. Corrections are the auditors' and verifiers' own recomputations; where they are applied to the register the sheet says so.

## Inflated beam mechanics and the pressures the design implies (stage3/setup_sim/inflated_beam.py, out/inflated_beam.txt, memo.md "Checked against the theses"; against Coad 2021 §4.3.2-4.3.3, McFarland & McGuinness 2025, Blumenschein 2019 ch. 2)

### IB-01 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/inflated_beam.py`:8

**Claim.** "Applied to the posts (r 0.6 m) and the arms (r 0.4 m) at the simulation's 40 kPa and the design's 80 kPa" - and memo.md:85-87 quotes the resulting SFs as the tube check for this folder.

**Problem.** The published machine (memo.md:89-134, out/production4.log, sheet 45) is setup_sim4.py, whose inflated members are ONE stalk r 0.50 m / L 1.49 m (setup_sim4.py:49) and two hoses r 0.35 m / L 1.20 and 2.00 m (setup_sim4.py:50), at reported pressures 182 / 65 / 98 kPa (out/production4.log t 40). No member of radius 0.6, 0.4 or 0.3 m exists in it, and neither 40 nor 80 kPa is any tube's pressure in either run. inflated_beam.py was never re-run on the fourth pass, so the register's 'checked against the theses' covers a machine that was withdrawn.

**Correction.** Fourth-pass check, M_w = pi p r^3 / 2. STALK r 0.50: M_w = 0.19635 p. At the run's 182 kPa M_w = 35.7 kNm; at 80 kPa 15.7; at 40 kPa 7.85. CW1 hose r 0.35: M_w = 0.06734 p -> 4.38 kNm at 65 kPa; load 260 kg x 9.81 x 0.68 m = 1.75 kNm -> SF 2.51. CW2 hose r 0.35 at 98 kPa: M_w = 6.60 kNm; load 409 x 9.81 x 1.17 = 4.68 kNm -> SF 1.41. Crushing P A: stalk pi(0.5)^2 = 0.785 m2, 143 kN at 182 kPa vs 6.5 kN of supported weight (663 kg) -> SF 22; Coad eq 4.2 buckling 283 kN -> never gating. Bending governs every member; the file must be re-pointed at r 0.50 / r 0.35 and at the run's own pressures.

### IB-02 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/inflated_beam.py`:18

**Claim.** The three moment loads are dead load only: post 1.5e3 Nm ("half the cradle; ~1 m off-axis"), arm 0.5*100*9.81*4.6*cos(12 deg) = 2205 Nm, counterweight tube 91*9.81*2.6*sin(45 deg) = 1641 Nm. memo.md:85-87 then reports SF 9 / 18 / 1.8 / 3.6 / 1.0 / 2.1 from them.

**Problem.** There is no wind term in the inflated-beam check at all, and wind is the governing bending load on every inflated member. On the third pass's own numbers the wind moment per post EXCEEDS the asserted 1.5 kNm at 9 m/s and is ten times it at 25 m/s, so every quoted SF is an answer to the wrong question. (memo.md:168 admits wind is not in the published frames but never draws the structural consequence.)

**Correction.** Drag from physics_hp.py: q9 = 0.5*1.03*81 = 41.7 Pa, q25 = 321.9 Pa, A = pi*2.1^2 = 13.85 m2, Cd 1.3 -> D9 = 751 N, D25 = 5797 N (reproduces synth.txt). Using the sim's own incidence law cd_eff = 0.25 + (1.3-0.25)cos^2(el) (setup_sim4.py:208) and the dish centre height h(el): THIRD-PASS POST r 0.60, axis at 4.52 m, arms 4.10 m: worst total base moment 4.03 kNm at 9 m/s and 31.1 kNm at 25 m/s, i.e. 2.02 and 15.56 kNm PER POST against the memo's 1.5 kNm dead load. Combined 3.52 / 17.06 kNm. M_w(0.6, 40 kPa) = 13.57 kNm -> SF 3.86 at 9 m/s, 0.80 at 25 m/s (the post wrinkles and, as an everting tube, collapses). SF 3 needs 31 kPa at 9 m/s and 151 kPa at 25 m/s (hoop p r = 91 kN/m). FOURTH-PASS STALK r 0.50, h = 1.94 + 1.8 sin(el): worst base moment 1.74 kNm at 9 m/s (el 20) and 13.44 kNm at 25 m/s -> SF 3 needs 27 kPa and 205 kPa respectively.

### IB-03 · material · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/memo.md`:127

**Claim.** "the stalk reaches the neck height at t 14 s and holds it (g 1.01, hoop readout 182 kPa); 260 kg and 409 kg of water go into the hoses" - reported as a result of the published run, alongside memo.md:31 "here fabric at about 40 kPa (80 kPa in the design)" and memo.md:61 "10-50 kPa in this fabric over the run".

**Problem.** Three mutually inconsistent pressures for the same fabric, and the one the runs actually produce is nowhere used in a check. The third-pass run's own log settles at 85-92 kPa for the posts, 88 for the arms, 77 for the counterweight tubes - not 40, and not '10-50 kPa over the run' (its stub phase reads 340-480 kPa). The fourth pass reads 178-182 kPa for the stalk. And 182 kPa is not a load-derived pressure: it is EPS_V (an over-volume chosen for solver stiffness) times the fabric spring stiffness, i.e. the same artifact memo.md:173-175 already admits for the stub, not re-examined for the grown tube.

**Correction.** p_eq (setup_sim4.py:407) = KE_FAB * eps * chord / (spacing * r) = (E t)_eff * eps / r with (E t)_eff = KE_FAB * chord / spacing. Stalk: chord = 2*0.5*sin(pi/12) = 0.2588 m, ring spacing = 1.01*1.49/8 = 0.1881 m -> (E t)_eff = 1.5e6*0.2588/0.1881 = 2064 kN/m, i.e. 4.1x the 500 kN/m assumed in inflated_beam.py:11 and in physics_hp.ET. The 182 kPa therefore corresponds to hoop tension N = p r = 91 kN/m at 4.41 % hoop strain. Pure EPS_V = 3.5 % at fixed length would give only sqrt(1.035)-1 = 1.73 % strain -> 71 kPa; the extra strain is the load. The design pressure must be set from the load (IB-02, IB-05, IB-07: 27-301 kPa depending on the case), not read off the solver. Quote one number per member per load case, not 40 / 80 / 182.

- verifier (confirmed): The readout formula at setup_sim4.py:407 is p = KE_FAB*eps*chord/((g*L/n_a)*rr), i.e. (E t)_eff = KE_FAB*chord/spacing with chord = 2 R sin(pi/N_C) and spacing the ring pitch. Stalk: chord 0.25882, spacing 1.01*1.49/8 = 0.18813, (E t)_eff = 2063.7 kN/m = 4.13x the 500 kN/m of inflated_beam.py:11 and physics_hp.py ET. At 182 kPa, N = p r = 91.0 kN/m and the implied hoop strain is 4.41 %; pure EPS_V 3.5 % at fixed length gives sqrt(1.035)-1 = 1.735 % and 71.6 kPa. Every figure reproduces. The three pressures are genuinely irreconcilable: memo.md:31 'about 40 kPa (80 kPa in the design)', memo.md: → (E t)_eff = 2064 kN/m (stalk), 1359 (cw1), 815 (cw2), 954 (3rd post) - the disagreement with the file's 500 kN/m is 4.13x, 2.72x, 1.63x, 1.91x, not one number. Stalk hoop strain 4.41 % at 182 kPa; EPS_V alone 1.73 % -> 71.6 kPa; end-restraint geometry alone 1.98 %; EPS_V + end restraint about 2.6 %.
- verifier (confirmed): The formula reading is right: setup_sim4.py:405-407 gives p_eq = KE_FAB*eps*chord/(spacing*rr), i.e. spring force KE_FAB*eps*chord divided by ring spacing (a hoop line load) divided by radius, so (E t)_eff = KE_FAB*chord/spacing. Stalk: chord = 2*0.50*sin(pi/12) = 0.25882 m, spacing = g*L/n_a = 1.01*1.49/8 = 0.18811 m (N_ST = 8 at setup_sim4.py:56), (E t)_eff = 1.5e6*0.25882/0.18811 = 2064 kN/m = 4.13x the 500 kN/m asserted at inflated_beam.py:11 and physics_hp.py:26. At 182 kPa, N = p r = 91.0 kN/m and the implied hoop strain is 91.0/2064 = 4.41 %; pure EPS_V = 0.035 at fixed length gives sqr → (E t)_eff = 2064 kN/m (4.13x the file's 500); N = 91.0 kN/m at 4.41 % hoop strain; EPS_V-only would give 1.73 % -> 71.6 kPa. Third-pass settled p_eq 91.7/92.4 (posts), 87.6/91.2 (arms), 77.4/77.4 (cw)

### IB-04 · material · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/inflated_beam.py`:14

**Claim.** m_collapse(R,P) = P*pi*(2R)^3/8, cited at line 4 as "McFarland & McGuinness 2025 (arXiv 2510.25727), eq. 1: transverse collapse moment M = P pi D^3 / 8", and reported as the SF(collapse) column of out/inflated_beam.txt; memo.md:80-82 repeats it.

**Problem.** Eq. 1 is the Leonard / Comer-Levy limit for a SEALED single-wall inflated beam. McFarland's own operative model for a vine robot - the thing this design claims to build ("grown from stubs the way an everting vine robot grows", memo.md:5-7) - is her eq. 8, which HALVES it because the eversion tail carries half the cap force: Mcollapse = (1/2)(P pi D^3/8) +/- Fe D/4. Every SF(collapse) in out/inflated_beam.txt is therefore 2x optimistic for the members as described, and the file's own m_wrinkle IS the correct collapse limit for them.

**Correction.** For an everting tube with the tail in, M_collapse = P pi D^3/16 = pi p r^3 / 2 = exactly the file's m_wrinkle. So the honest table has ONE capacity column, not two: stalk r 0.50 at 182 kPa -> 35.7 kNm (not 71.5); cw2 hose r 0.35 at 98 kPa -> 6.60 kNm (not 13.2), against a 4.68 kNm load, SF 1.41 on COLLAPSE. Third-pass post r 0.60 at 40 kPa -> 13.57 kNm (not 27.1) against 17.06 kNm at 25 m/s: SF 0.80. If the tubes are sealed and the tail cut after growth, eq. 1 applies and the SF(collapse) column stands - but then they are no longer vine robots and the growth story in memo.md:5-11 must be re-stated as a one-shot erection, with the tail removed from the stalk bore (see IB-09).

- verifier (confirmed): Checked against the PDF directly. vine_collapse_own_weight_2025.pdf: eq. 1 is introduced as 'Leonard [13] and Comer and Levy [14] define the moment needed to transversely collapse an inflated beam as Mcollapse = P pi D^3 / 8'; eq. 6 gives Tt = (1/2) P A +/- Fe/2 for the everting/inverting/stopped range; and the text before eq. 8 reads 'we assume that this force is applied at a distance D/2 from the robot's collapse point, and we subtract it from the previous Mcollapse, which gives us a new value of Mcollapse = (1/2) P pi D^3/8 +/- Fe D/4'. The 1/2 is not an assertion, it falls out: Tt*(D/2) =  → Everting collapse = P pi D^3/16 = pi p r^3/2 +/- Fe D/4. Stalk at 182 kPa 35.74 kNm (not 71.47); cw2 at 98 kPa 6.60 (not 13.20) against 4.69 kNm -> SF 1.41 on collapse; 3rd post at 40 kPa 13.57 (not 27.13). The honest table has one capacity column.
- verifier (confirmed): Verified in the source. pdftotext of resources/vine_collapse_own_weight_2025.pdf: eq. 1 Mcollapse = P pi D^3/8, attributed to Leonard [13] and Comer and Levy [14], at text lines 208-216. The tail-tension model is at 324-354: Tt ranges 1/2 P pi D^2/4 +/- Fe/2 (eq. 6), Fe = Pe pi D^2/4 (eq. 7), and 'Tail tension is expected to act along the robot's central axis. Therefore, we assume that this force is applied at a distance D/2 from the robot's collapse point, and we subtract it from the previous Mcollapse', giving eq. 8 = (1/2) P pi D^3/8 +/- Fe D/4. Algebra: (1/2)P pi D^3/8 = P pi (2r)^3/16 = p → M_collapse (tail in) = P pi D^3/16 = pi p r^3/2, identical to the file's m_wrinkle: stalk 35.74 kNm at 182 kPa, cw2 6.60 kNm at 98 kPa (SF 1.41), third-pass post 13.57 kNm at 40 kPa (SF 0.76 at 25 m/s)

### IB-05 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/memo.md`:100

**Claim.** "Stow: stalk short, head face-up over the neck, hoses empty. Sequence: the stalk grows (t 3-17 s of 40), the hoses grow (17-26 s) and fill (24-30 s)" - with memo.md:31 giving the fabric "about 40 kPa (80 kPa in the design)".

**Problem.** The stalk is grown to full height and carries the entire yoke + head BEFORE any water is pumped, so for the whole growth-and-fill window it carries the head's unbalanced moment about the stalk axis with zero counterweight. The sim prints that moment itself and nobody checked the stalk against it. At the memo's stated fabric pressures the stalk wrinkles during its own erection - before wind, before tracking, before the design's storm case.

**Correction.** The sim's own printed balance is 'yoke about the stalk 639 kg m' (out/production4.log line 1), i.e. an unbalanced base moment of 639 * 9.81 = 6.27 kNm during t 3-30 s. M_w(0.5, p) = 0.19635 p: at 40 kPa M_w = 7.85 kNm -> SF 1.25; at 80 kPa -> SF 2.51; only the run's own 182 kPa gives SF 5.70. Add a 9 m/s breeze during erection (1.74 kNm) -> 8.01 kNm: SF 0.98 at 40 kPa (past wrinkling), 1.96 at 80 kPa. SF 3 on the erection case needs 96 kPa in still air and 122 kPa at 9 m/s (hoop p r = 61 kN/m). Either pressurise the stalk to >=122 kPa before growth (a compressor), or reorder the sequence so the yoke counterweight fills as the stalk grows, or grow the stalk against a temporary prop. The erection window, not the wind, sets the stalk's minimum pressure at any pressure below ~120 kPa.

I re-ran the build (lines 47-158, Warp stubbed out) and reproduced the printed balance exactly: M_n 284.5 kg m, M_e 639.1 kg m, levers 1.10/1.5667, 462/770 L, 260/409 kg. But M_e = 639 kg m INCLUDES the cw1 water: setup_sim4.py:155 adds (M_CW1-1.0)*((N-Q → Dry erection moment about the stalk = 277.0 kg m = 2.717 kNm (not 639 kg m / 6.27 kNm). With a 9 m/s breeze on the face-up dish (cd 0.25, h 3.74 m, D 144 N, 540 Nm) = 3.257 kNm. SF at 40 kPa 2.89 / 2.41; at 80 kPa 5.78 / 4.82; at 182 kPa 13.15. SF 3 needs 41.5 kPa still air, 49.8 kPa at 9 m/s (not 9
- verifier (confirmed): The gap is real but the number is 2.31x overstated and the headline claim is false. ### IB-06 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/inflated_beam.py`:11

**Claim.** E, G, t = 1.0e9, 0.35e9, 0.5e-3 with the comment "a coated polyester/aramid fabric: E t = 500 kN/m, 0.5 mm"; no hoop-stress check anywhere in the file, and none in memo.md:73-87.

**Problem.** The one check that decides whether any of this is buildable - the fabric's hoop load p r - is missing, and the file's own material constants are inconsistent with the pressures the design and the runs use. Hoop is also the check that makes radius, not pressure, the design lever, which the design never exploits.

**Correction.** N_hoop = p r, and hoop strain = p r / (E t) with the file's own E t = 500 kN/m. Stalk r 0.50 at the run's 182 kPa: N = 91.0 kN/m, sigma = p r / t = 182 MPa, strain = 18.2 % - no structural fabric reaches 18 % strain, so the file's material and the run's pressure are mutually exclusive (and the solver's own implied E t is 2064 kN/m, giving 4.4 %: a 4.1x internal disagreement, IB-03). Required strip tensile at a membrane stress factor of 4: 364 kN/m at 182 kPa, 273 kN/m even at SF 3 - above the strongest coated architectural fabrics (PVC-polyester Type V and PTFE-glass are of order 100-200 kN/m ultimate). Working backwards, for the stalk r 0.50 with SF 4 on the fabric and SF 3 on wrinkling: N_ult 80 kN/m -> p 40 kPa -> tracking limit 11.0 m/s; N_ult 115 kN/m -> p 57.5 kPa -> 13.2 m/s; N_ult 180 kN/m -> p 90 kPa -> 16.6 m/s. Because N = 2 M / (pi r^2) at fixed moment and SF, radius is free and pressure is not: doubling r quarters the fabric load. Adding this one line to inflated_beam.py changes the design.

- verifier (confirmed): The check really is absent - there is no p r anywhere in inflated_beam.py or in memo.md:73-87 - and every number reproduces: N = 91.0 kN/m and sigma = p r / t = 182 MPa at the run's 182 kPa; 18.2 % strain against the file's own E t = 500 kN/m (inflated_beam.py:11) versus 4.41 % against the solver's implied 2064 kN/m; 273 / 364 kN/m required strip tensile at SF 3 / 4. The scaling argument is exact and is the finding's real contribution: M_w = pi p r^3/2 with SF s gives p = 2 s M/(pi r^3) and hence N = p r = 2 s M/(pi r^2), so at fixed moment and safety factor the fabric load falls as 1/r^2 whil → N_hoop = p r; N = 2 SF M/(pi r^2). Stalk at 182 kPa: N 91.0 kN/m, sigma 182 MPa, strain 18.2 % at E t 500 kN/m (4.41 % at the solver's 2064 kN/m). At the load-derived pressures instead: 26.6 kPa -> 13.3 kN/m; 49.8 kPa -> 24.9 kN/m. Tracking limits at SF 4 fabric / SF 3 wrinkling: 11.0, 13.2, 16.6 m/
- verifier (confirmed): There is no hoop check in the file - inflated_beam.py defines only coad_buckling, crushing, m_collapse, m_wrinkle (lines 12-15) - and none in memo.md:73-87. The membrane statics are right: N_hoop = p r, N_axial = p r/2, and M_w = pi r^2 t * (p r/2t) = pi p r^3/2 reproduces the file's own m_wrinkle from the axial prestress, which is the internal consistency check. At the run's 182 kPa and r 0.50: N = 91.0 kN/m, sigma = p r/t = 182 MPa at t = 0.5 mm, strain at the file's own E t = 500 kN/m = 18.2 % - impossible for any structural fabric, and 4.13x off the solver's own implied 2064 kN/m (IB-03).  → N = 91.0 kN/m, sigma 182 MPa, 18.2 % strain at E t = 500 kN/m; tracking limits 11.0 / 13.2 / 16.6 m/s at N_ult 80 / 115 / 180 kN/m; at 10 kPa and SF 3 the stalk must be r 0.693 m (D 1.39 m) for 9 m/s and r 1.369 m (D 2.74 m) for 25 m/s

### IB-07 · material · REFUTED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/inflated_beam.py`:25

**Claim.** "growth force P A for the post at 40 kPa = 45.2 kN; the eversion losses Y A and (v/phi)^(1/n) A are the blower's headroom (her Table: Y ~ 1-3 kPa for LDPE/ripstop)"; setup_sim4.py:322 and memo.md:31 likewise call the growth rate "the blower's rate/speed".

**Problem.** Two errors that together destroy the blower story. (a) Blumenschein's measured yield pressures are 20.7-66.9 kPa, not 1-3 kPa, and Y is not a material constant: the yield FORCE scales with wall thickness (her Fig. 2.4b, linear) and with circumference (her Fig. 2.5), so Y = 2k/r falls with radius. (b) At the pressures the runs and the load cases actually require, no blower of any kind can supply the tube.

**Correction.** From her Fig. 2.3(b) data (Y = 66.9, 41.4, 20.7 kPa at r = 1.3, 2.4, 4.0 cm, t = 80 um), the yield force per unit circumference is 435, 497, 414 N/m - constant, mean k = 449 N/m at 80 um. Extrapolating her own linear-in-thickness, linear-in-circumference fits to the stalk (r 0.50 m, t 0.5 mm = 6.25x): Y A = k*(t/80um)*2 pi r = 8.8 kN, i.e. Y = 11.2 kPa; for the hoses (r 0.35 m) Y = 16.0 kPa. So the eversion threshold ALONE is 11-16 kPa - a 5-10 kPa blower cannot grow these tubes at all, before any structural pressure. Structural requirement, SF 3 on wrinkling: stalk 27 kPa (tracking 9 m/s only), 96 kPa (erection, counterweight empty), 122 kPa (erection + 9 m/s), 205 kPa (25 m/s inflated), 301 kPa (25 m/s with the counterweight pump failed); cw2 hose 209 kPa; third-pass post 31 kPa (9 m/s) / 151 kPa (25 m/s). Only the 27-31 kPa tracking-only case is blower territory, and then only a regenerative/side-channel blower (to ~50-70 kPa), not a 5-10 kPa fan. Everything at or above the erection case is a compressor (>=1 bar gauge). To stay at 10 kPa the stalk must be r 0.81 m (D 1.62 m) for 9 m/s at SF 3, or r 1.64 m (D 3.28 m) for 25 m/s. Gas budget is not the problem: stalk 1170 L geometric = 3272 L free air at 182 kPa, 341 kJ isothermal (~570 W over 10 min).

- verifier (refuted): Part (a), the load-bearing half, does not survive. The auditor derived k = 449 N/m at 80 um from the 66.9 / 41.4 / 20.7 kPa figures at blum.txt:1780 - but that experiment is growth INTO A GAP between two flat plates, deliberately constrained (blum.txt:1745-1751, 'by modifying only the cross-sectional area, any yield pressure could be identified independently'). Blumenschein's FREE-growth fit, the one that applies to a stalk growing into open air, is at blum.txt:2005-2006: 'a yield pressure, Y, of 13.3 kPa, an extensibility, phi, of 0.75 kPa^-n m/s and a power term, n, of 1.1' for r 1.3 cm, t 8 → Free-growth eversion yield, scaled from Blumenschein's own Y = 13.3 kPa at r 1.3 cm / 80 um by k = 86.5 N/m: Y = 2.16 kPa (stalk r 0.50) and 3.09 kPa (hoses r 0.35) at t 0.5 mm - not 11.2 / 16.0 kPa. Radius to hold 10 kPa at SF 3 on the tracking wind: r 0.693 m (9 m/s), r 1.369 m (25 m/s), not 0.81

At the run's own 77 kPa (out/production.log, last line) SF = 1.44. SF 3 needs 160 kPa. The reported 1.0 / 2.1 should read 0.75 / 1.5 (and 0.75 / 0.75 under IB-04's everting collapse).

### IB-09 · material · CONFIRMED

**Claim.** "Blumenschein eq. 2.1: growth force P A for the post at 40 kPa = 45.2 kN" - printed as a headroom figure, with no consequence drawn.

**Problem.** P A is not headroom at this scale, it is a runaway. Coad's eq. 4.1 and Blumenschein 2.2.1 both say the tail must hold about half of it to arrest or invert the tube; nothing in the design, the sim or the memo says what holds the stalk's growth thrust, and the vine's tail is the one thing that cannot be in the stalk, because the stalk is the optical bore.

Tail tension to hold or invert, ~P A / 2 (Coad eq. 4.1, T_T = P A/2 + F_I; Blumenschein 2.2.1 'we will generally only measure half the driving force in the tension of the internal material'): 71.5 kN for the stalk at 182 kPa. Coad's own robots are r ~0.02 m at 8 kPa: P A = 10 N. This design is 4 orders of magnitude up. Separately, the not-yet-everted material and its tail lie flat down the tube's centre - and stage3/coude/model.py:33 and optics.py:34-36 put the converging beam from M3 to F3 down exactly that centre. An everting stalk and an optical bore are the same volume; they cannot both have it. Either the stalk is grown once, sealed, and the tail withdrawn and cut before first light (in which case say so, and the McFarland eq. 1 collapse moment of IB-04 is recovered), or the machine is not a vine at that member and the trunk grows as an annular jacket around a clear bore - which is a different tube and a different check.

### IB-12 · minor · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/inflated_beam.py`:12

**Claim.** coad_buckling(R, L, P) is used to report 114.1 kN for the post at 40 kPa and 47.4 kN for the arm, printed as the 'Coad buckling' column of out/inflated_beam.txt.

**Problem.** The formula is transcribed exactly, but at this geometry it is dominated by the assumed shear modulus, which is taken from Coad's LDPE film (she uses E 300 MPa, G 210 MPa) and then changed to E 1 GPa, G 350 MPa without a note. A woven coated fabric has a very low shear modulus - G t of order 10-50 kN/m, i.e. G ~ 20-100 MPa at 0.5 mm - so the number is an artifact of the constant, not a property of the tube.

**Correction.** For the post r 0.60, L 4.52, 40 kPa: the E G pi^3 R^3 t^2 numerator term is 5.86e11 against 8.04e10 for the pressure term (7x), and G t L^2 = 3.58e6 is the largest denominator term - so F_buckling is essentially E G t^2 / (G t) ~ E t, a shear-driven number. Re-evaluating with G = 30 MPa gives 50.8 kN (2.2x lower) and with G = 100 MPa 75.4 kN, against Euler pi^2 (E t pi R^3)/L^2 = 163.9 kN. None of this matters for the verdict - the axial loads are 0-3.2 kN and the crushing limit P A = 45.2 kN is the binding axial check - but the column should either be dropped or carry the sensitivity, and the E/G change from Coad's values should be justified.

Term-by-term at r 0.60, L 4.52, 40 kPa: numerator 8.037e10 (pressure) against 5.860e11 (shear), ratio 7.29; denominator 1.777e6, 4.903e5, 3.575e6, so G t L^2 is indeed the largest. Re-running at G = 100 and 30  → F_buckling 114.1 kN at G 350 MPa, 75.4 at 100 MPa, 50.8 at 30 MPa, 40.9 at Coad's own 300/210 MPa; asymptote E pi^3 R^3 t/L^2 = 163.9 kN. Crushing P A = 45.2 kN binds in every case against a 3.19 kN axial load.

### IB-13 · minor · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/inflated_beam.py`:6

**Claim.** "the Lockhart-Ortega turgor-growth law r = phi (P - Y)^n that the plants' cells and these tubes share", repeated at line 26 as "Lockhart-Ortega: dL/dt = phi (P - Y)^n" and at memo.md:75-77.

**Problem.** Misattribution within the cited source. Blumenschein's eq. 2.2 in that exact form (r = phi (P - Y)^n, n a power close to unity) is attributed by her to Green [74] as a modification of the Lockhart-Ortega model; Lockhart-Ortega [73] itself is the linear volume-rate law (n = 1) with an added elastic term.

**Correction.** Cite it as: Lockhart-Ortega gives the turgor-yield form; Green's modification (Blumenschein eq. 2.2) gives the linear-extension-rate power law r = phi (P - Y)^n. This matters only for the citation, but the register quotes the theses as its authority.

- verifier (confirmed): Verbatim in the source. blum.txt:1489-1497: 'growth is the Lockhart-Ortega equation, which relates the rate of volume change to ... A modification of this viscoplastic model of plant cell extension described by Green relates pressure to a linear extension rate', with Lockhart at reference [73] (blum.txt:7991) and Green, Erickson and Buggy at [74] (blum.txt:7993). So r = phi (P - Y)^n is Green's modification, not Lockhart-Ortega, and inflated_beam.py:6-7 and :26 and memo.md:76 all attribute it to the wrong pair. She herself calls it 'the modified Green Equation (2.3)' at blum.txt:2004 and :2040 → Lockhart-Ortega [73] is the turgor-yield volume-rate law; r = phi (P - Y)^n is Green's modification, Blumenschein eq. 2.2/2.3, her ref. [74], which she calls 'the modified Green Equation'.
- verifier (confirmed): Verified in the source. blum.txt:1489-1497: 'the equation describing growth is the Lockhart-Ortega equation, which relates the rate of volume change to the difference between the turgor pressure and yield pressure [73]. A modification of this viscoplastic model of plant cell extension described by Green relates pressure to a linear extension rate: r = phi (P - Y)^n ... [74]'. Reference [73] is Lockhart, 'An analysis of irreversible plant cell elongation' (blum.txt:7991); [74] is Green, Erickson and Buggy (blum.txt:7993). So the power-law linear-extension-rate form the file quotes at inflated_b → Cite as: Lockhart-Ortega gives the turgor-yield volume-rate law; Green's modification (Blumenschein eq. 2.2, ref. [74]) gives r = phi (P - Y)^n

Coad's chapter 4 data is 'at constant pressure (between 0 and 10 kPa), varying lengths between 35 and 190 cm' (coad.txt:4440-4441) on a D 8.5 cm LDPE body. Against D 0.70-1.20 m at 65-182 kPa that is 12-30x in diameter and 7-90x in pressure, and memo.md:82 states the envelope and then applies the models w → McFarland 1.21-8.49 cm at 2.1-27.8 kPa; Coad 0-10 kPa on D 8.5 cm. This design D 0.70-1.20 m at 65-182 kPa. Stalk L/D 1.49. Base peel line load n = M/(pi r^2): 2.22 kN/m (9 m/s), 17.11 kN/m (25 m/s), 4.15 kN/m (erection + 9 m/s corrected) - not 25.10, which came from IB-05's overstated 19.71 kNm.
- verifier (confirmed): Envelope verified in the source: collapse.txt:487 'we extended the range to be from 2.1 kPa to 27.8 kPa' and :501-506 lists diameters 2.43, 3.24, 4.04, 4.85 cm plus 'two additional diameters, 1.21 cm and 8.49 cm', so 1.21-8.49 cm at 2.1-27.8 kPa, exactly as memo.md:82 records. Coad ch. 4 is LDPE at D 8.5 cm, t 74 um, tested 0-10 kPa. This design is D 0.70-1.20 m at 65-182 kPa: 8.2-11.8x the LARGEST tested diameter (58-83x the smallest) and 2.3-87x in pressure - the auditor's '12-30x' is slightly loose on diameter but '7-90x' on pressure and the 'one to two orders of magnitude' framing are righ → tested envelope D 1.21-8.49 cm at 2.1-27.8 kPa (McFarland), 8.5 cm at 0-10 kPa (Coad); this design D 0.70-1.20 m at 65-182 kPa = 8-12x in D, 2.3-87x in p; stalk L/D 1.49; base peel line load 2.22 / 17.11 / 20.6 kN/m

**Checked and found right:**

- m_wrinkle = 0.5*pi*P*R**3 (inflated_beam.py:15) is correct and correctly labelled. Re-derived independently: pressure pre-stress sigma_a = p r / 2t, section modulus of a thin cylinder S = I/r = pi r^3 t / r = pi r^2 t, wrinkling when the bending stress cancels the pre-stress: M_w = pi r^2 t * p r/(2t) = pi p r^3 / 2.
- m_collapse = P*pi*(2R)**3/8 (inflated_beam.py:14) is algebraically identical to pi P R^3 = 2*M_wrinkle, and matches McFarland & McGuinness eq. 1 verbatim (collapse.txt:123-125, attributed there to Leonard [13] and Comer & Levy [14]) - the attribution at inflated_beam.py:4 is exact. (Its applicability to an everting tube is the separate issue IB-04.)
- coad_buckling (inflated_beam.py:12) is Coad eq. 4.2 transcribed character for character: (E pi^3 R^4 t P + E G pi^3 R^3 t^2)/(E pi^2 R^2 t + R L^2 P + G t L^2), coad.txt:3495. The docstring's section reference 4.3.2-4.3.3 is right (coad.txt:3446, 3520).
- crushing = P*pi*R**2 (inflated_beam.py:13) is Coad eq. 4.3, F_crushing = P A, coad.txt:3504. Correct, and correctly identified as the axial-collapse mode when the wall would otherwise go slack.
- Blumenschein eq. 2.1, F_in = P * A, is quoted correctly (inflated_beam.py:25, memo.md:75) - blum.txt:954.
- memo.md:87 'at the design's 80 kPa each doubles' is correct: both pi p r^3 / 2 and pi p r^3 are linear in p, so the whole capacity column scales exactly with pressure.
- inflated_beam.py:11 E t = 1.0e9 * 0.5e-3 = 500 kN/m matches physics_hp.ET = 500e3 (physics_hp.py:27) - the two files at least agree with each other, even though neither agrees with the solver (IB-03).
- Bending, not axial load, governs every inflated member - which is what the file implicitly shows by reporting all three. Verified: the stalk carries ~663 kg = 6.5 kN of design dead load against crushing P A = 31.4 kN at 40 kPa (SF 4.8) and 143 kN at 182 kPa, and against Coad buckling 227-283 kN at L 1.49 m. The third-pass post's 3.19 kN axial against 45.2 kN crushing is likewise never close.
- Torsion about the azimuth axis is not gating, though it is never checked. Crosswind on a dish 1.4 m off the stalk axis (stage3/coude/out/optics.json OFF = 1.4) gives T = 751*1.4 = 1.05 kNm at 9 m/s and 8.11 kNm at 25 m/s; the torsional wrinkling limit of an inflated cylinder, tau_w = p r / (t sqrt2) hence T_w = sqrt2 pi p r^3, is 101 kNm at r 0.5 and 182 kPa. Bending governs by a wide margin.
- The fourth pass's counterweight balance about the stalk is elevation-independent, so the stalk's dead-load bending genuinely is nominally zero once the water is in - the design's claim at stage3/coude/synth.py:45-46 stands. Checked: head 155 kg + neck counterweight 151 kg both sit at OFF = 1.4 m from the stalk, 306*1.4 = 428 kg m, against 267*1.6 = 427 kg m. Because the head+cw1 centre of mass lies on the neck axis, rotating in elevation does not move it. (What is missing is the erection window before the water arrives - IB-05.)
- The pressure readout at setup_sim4.py:407 is a correct identity, p = N_hoop / r, computed from the solved hoop strain - it is a real hoop-tension-over-radius, not a fudge. The objection is to what sets that strain (a chosen over-volume, IB-03), not to the formula.
- The drag constants reproduce: q9 = 0.5*1.03*81 = 41.7 Pa, q25 = 321.9 Pa, A = pi*2.1^2 = 13.85 m2, Cd 1.3 -> 751 N and 5797 N, matching the printed values in stage3/coude/out/synth.txt exactly.
- memo.md:173-175 is honest about the fabric spring stiffness making the stub's pressure readout an artifact (300 kPa settling to ~45 kPa), and memo.md:168 is honest that wind is not run in the published frames. Both admissions are correct; the fault is that neither consequence is carried into the tube checks or into the quoted 182 kPa (IB-02, IB-03).
- memo.md:60-66's account of why the volume constraint and the 1.2 % axial pre-shortening are needed is physically right in kind: an inflated beam's bending stiffness comes from the pressure-induced axial pre-tension p r / 2, and a tube without it goes slack on the compression side at once. The sim's EPS_AX = 0.012 (setup_sim4.py:175) implements that. (memo.md:60 says 'plus 2 %' where the code has EPS_V = 0.035 - a documentation slip, not a physics error.)

## Soft-body simulation physics (stage3/setup_sim) and what may be concluded from it

### SIM-01 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/setup_sim4.py`:230

**Claim.** The register's sheet-45 rows ('tracks': frame 0.03 deg from the sun; 'to microradians': coarse 0.59 mrad, fine 0.07 mrad) and memo.md:125-130 present the run as the machine tracking. The wind kernel is described in memo.md:169 as 'a kernel (--wind) but not run in the published frames'.

**Problem.** The wind kernel is not merely unrun - it is structurally incapable of loading the dish. wind_force is launched over `tris` (setup_sim4.py:386) and returns immediately unless `wall[t]!=0` (line 232); `wall_tri` is set only inside add_tube (line 173 of the tube loop), and tris_all is appended ONLY by add_tube. The dish (RIM, VTX, BR, QD, SEC) has no triangle at all. Confirmed from the published artefact: setup4_anim.json carries 552 triangles = stalk 216 + cw1 168 + cw2 168, exactly the three tubes. So the largest surface in the machine, 13.9 m2, gets zero aerodynamic force, and the published run additionally has --wind 0.0 (line 40 default; run_production4.sh:5 passes no --wind). Every number on sheet 45 is a still-air number presented without that qualifier, and the 'coarse stage' figure is used on the page as the middle link of the pointing budget.

**Correction.** Dish drag q Cd A with rho 1.03 (Quetta 1680 m), Cd 1.3, A 13.9 m2: at 9 m/s q = 0.5*1.03*81 = 41.7 Pa -> F = 754 N; at 25 m/s q = 321.9 Pa -> F = 5816 N. The stalk's own drag that the kernel WOULD apply is q*1.2*A_proj with A_proj = 2*0.5*1.49 = 1.49 m2: 75 N and 576 N - the dish is 10.1x the only surface the simulation loads. Consequence for the register's coarse row: treating the grown stalk as an inflated cantilever with E t 500 kN/m (inflated_beam.py:11), EI = E t pi r^3 = 500e3*pi*0.125 = 196 kN m2; the drag acts at the dish centre 2.75 m over the deck on a 1.94 m stalk, so the tip slope is (F/EI)*int_0^1.94 (2.75-z) dz = 3.453 F/EI = 13.3 mrad (0.76 deg) at 9 m/s and 102 mrad (5.9 deg) at 25 m/s. The register quotes 0.59 mrad for that same frame: the working-wind value is 22x larger, and at 25 m/s the frame leaves the 3 deg handover window entirely. Nothing on the page or in this folder computes that number.

### SIM-02 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/setup_sim4.py`:335

**Claim.** memo.md:176: 'A day compressed into 40 s makes the strut servo lag the sun; the coarse loop on the sun sensor removes most of it, and the real day is 700 times slower.' The damping is not mentioned anywhere in the memo or in the register's 'solver' row.

**Problem.** `damp = exp(-4.0*dt)` is applied to every particle's velocity on every substep (predict kernel, line 225). With substeps 12 and fps 30, dt = 2.778e-3 s, so velocity decays by exp(-4t) in wall time - an isotropic viscous dashpot with c/m = 4 s^-1 on every mass in the machine, including the 934 kg head. This is not a solver stabiliser at the margin; it is the dominant dissipation in the model, and it is exactly the term that decides whether a gust response is seen. It also means the new dish_load/gust code added at lines 197-217 cannot produce a trustworthy gust answer either.

**Correction.** Equivalent dashpot on the moving mass above the deck (933.8 kg, recomputed below): c = 4*933.8 = 3736 N s/m. Real aerodynamic damping on the dish is c_aero = rho Cd A U = 1.03*1.3*13.9*U: 167 N s/m at 9 m/s and 465 N s/m at 25 m/s. The numerical drag is therefore 22x (9 m/s) and 8x (25 m/s) the physical damping. In modal terms zeta = (c/m)/(2 omega) = 2/omega, so every mode below omega = 2 rad/s (f = 0.32 Hz) is critically damped or overdamped - which is the entire gust band, and specifically the 4.0 s (0.25 Hz) sinusoid the new wind_now() at line 199-201 injects. For reference the stalk's own first mode with E t 500 kN/m and a 934 kg tip mass is k = 3EI/L^3 = 178 kN/m, f = 2.2 Hz, where the numerical zeta is 0.145 - i.e. the model adds 14.5 % of critical damping to the structural mode as well. Fix: replace the global exp(-4 dt) with (a) XPBD constraint damping on the fabric only, and (b) an explicit relative-velocity aerodynamic term f = 0.5 rho Cd A |u-v|(u-v); then report the settling time so the reader can see what is dissipative and what is numerical.

- verifier (confirmed): Arithmetic and code both check. damp = exp(-4.0*dt) (setup_sim4.py:335) with dt = (1/30)/12 = 2.7778e-3 s gives 0.988950 per substep and exp(-4) = 0.0183 per second of the model's own (physical) seconds; it multiplies every free particle's velocity in predict (224) and is not undone by finish (298), which only re-derives v from the projected displacement. Equivalent dashpot on the whole moving mass, which I recompute as 972.77 kg after the fill (not 933.8): c = 4m = 3891 N s/m, against c_aero = rho Cd A U = 168 N s/m at 9 m/s and 465 at 25 - 23.2x and 8.4x, matching the auditor's 22x/8x. zeta  → c = 4*972.77 = 3891 N s/m (auditor's 933.8 kg understates the moving mass; my rebuild gives 319.37 kg dry + water = 972.77 kg), i.e. 23.2x/8.4x the physical aerodynamic damping; the rest of the finding's numbers stand.
- verifier (confirmed): Every number reproduces. setup_sim4.py:335 damp = exp(-4.0*dt) with dt = dt_f/substeps; the production run used --substeps 12 --fps 30 (run_production4.sh:5), so dt = 2.7778e-3 s, damp = 0.988950 per substep, exp(-4) = 0.018 per simulated second, applied to every particle's velocity in predict (line 224) with no mass or material dependence -> an isotropic dashpot with c/m = 4 s^-1. Moving mass: my rebuild, applying the fill exactly as line 351 does, gives 972.77 kg free (the auditor's 972.8 in SIM-05; their 933.8 here is 39 kg lower and unexplained, but the conclusion is mass-independent in ra → c = 4m = 3891 N s/m at the run's 972.8 kg vs c_aero 167.5 / 465.3 N s/m; ratio 23.2x (9 m/s) and 8.4x (25 m/s); overdamped below 0.318 Hz; zeta 0.145 on the 2.20 Hz stalk mode

### SIM-03 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/build_page.py`:272

**Claim.** Register row 'pressure': "the tubes' hoop strain reads {max p_eq} kPa at most (2 % over-volume prestress); Coad's crushing and buckling forces and McFarland's collapse moment give SF 9 / 1.8 / 1.0 (post / arm / counterweight tube) at 40 kPa and double at the design's 80 kPa". memo.md:31 'here fabric at about 40 kPa (80 kPa in the design)'; memo.md:60-61 'plus 2 %... (10-50 kPa in this fabric over the run)'; memo.md:127 'hoop readout 182 kPa'.

**Problem.** Three separate defects in one row. (a) The printed maximum is max over the WHOLE log including the stub transient at t = 0: I evaluate it as 821.5 kPa, so the page tells the reader the machine's fabric reads 821 kPa - a start-up artefact of a 0.0625 m ring spacing, not a pressure. (b) The over-volume is not 2 %: setup_sim4.py:175 has EPS_V = 0.035. (c) The safety factors are inflated_beam.py's, computed at inflated_beam.py:18-20 for the THIRD pass's tubes - post r 0.60, arm r 0.40, counterweight tube r 0.30 with 91 kg of water. None of those parts exists in the fourth-pass machine, which has one stalk r 0.50 and two hoses r 0.35 carrying 260 and 409 kg. The row therefore certifies parts that were deleted, beside a pressure the run never held.

**Correction.** Recomputed for the parts that are actually built, with McFarland/Leonard M_collapse = p pi D^3/8 and M_wrinkle = 0.5 pi p r^3: stalk r 0.5 -> M_w 7.85 / 15.71 / 35.74 kN m and M_c 15.71 / 31.42 / 71.47 kN m at 40 / 80 / 182 kPa; hose r 0.35 -> M_w 2.69 / 5.39 / 12.26 and M_c 5.39 / 10.78 / 24.51. Loads: cw2 carries 409 kg at a mean lever from its own base of L(n_a+1)/(2 n_a) = 2.0*7/12 = 1.167 m -> M_base = 409*9.81*1.167 = 4.68 kN m, so SF(wrinkle) = 2.69/4.68 = 0.58 at 40 kPa and 1.15 at 80 kPa: the fourth pass's counterweight hose WRINKLES at the design pressure under its own water, before any wind. cw1: 260 kg at 1.2*7/12 = 0.70 m -> 1.79 kN m, SF 1.51 at 40 kPa. Stalk under the 25 m/s dish drag: base moment 5816*2.75 = 16.0 kN m against M_w 15.71 kN m at 80 kPa -> SF 0.98. Axially the stalk is fine: 933.8*9.81 = 9.16 kN against crush P A = 31.4 kN (SF 3.3) and Coad eq. 4.2 buckling 227 kN at 40 kPa. Rewrite the row with these, and print p_eq over the tracking window only, not over the whole log.

### SIM-04 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/memo.md`:128

**Claim.** 'the dish on the fine stage 0.014 degrees (0.004 after the first sixth of the day, i.e. the loop's resolution in single precision, not the stage's)'; build_page.py:271 repeats it as 'The residual here is the simulation's loop and float precision'.

**Problem.** Single precision is nowhere near that floor, so the attribution is wrong by nearly three orders of magnitude, and the wrongness matters because it is the sentence that licenses the jump from the simulation to the design's microradians. The pointing normal is built from three BR stations at R_RING 1.46, i.e. a 2.529 m baseline; float32 at |x| ~ 3 m has ulp 2.384e-7 m. The 0.004 deg residual is a controller limit cycle plus the log's own rounding (setup_sim4.py:423 stores point=round(point_err,3), a 0.001 deg quantum), not a numerical floor. Separately, the fine stage in the simulation is not the design's stage: FINE_COLS are kind-6 struts at 1.0e6 N/m (setup_sim4.py:143) at R_ACT 1.60.

**Correction.** Angular resolution in float32 = ulp/baseline = 2.384e-7/2.529 = 9.43e-8 rad = 5.40e-6 deg. The reported 0.004 deg is 740x above that, so it is not 'the loop's resolution in single precision'. (For completeness the volume constraint's float32 error is 2.4e-7 relative against a 3.5e-2 over-volume target - five orders of headroom; single precision limits nothing in this model.) The late-day residual distribution is 0.000-0.013 deg over 255 samples with a 0.001 deg quantum, mean 0.00417 - a limit cycle of the u_tilt/u_piston integrator at gain 2.0 s^-1, clipped by the log's rounding. And the stage: three 1 MN/m columns at r 1.6 give k_tilt = 1.5 k r^2 = 3.84 MN m/rad, 23x softer than the 90 MN m/rad claimed for the FACT diaphragm; under the design's own 10.2 kN m crosswind moment the simulated stage would tilt 2.66 mrad where the design predicts 113 urad. The honest sentence is: the simulation's fine-stage residual is a still-air limit cycle of a loop closed on a noiseless, zero-latency, exact measurement of the dish's own normal, on a stage 23x softer than the design's; it is evidence about the controller's arithmetic and about nothing else.

- verifier (confirmed): Facts verified. np.spacing(float32(3.0)) = 2.3842e-7 m; BR baseline 2*1.46*sin60 = 2.5288 m (GM.R_RING 1.46, STATIONS 60/180/300); ulp/baseline = 9.43e-8 rad = 5.40e-6 deg, and 0.004/5.40e-6 = 740. The log quantum is real (setup_sim4.py:423 round(point_err,3)); the late-day residual is 0.000-0.013 deg over 255 samples, mean 0.004169, std 0.00224. I add the evidence the auditor asserted but did not show: the centred late-day series has lag-1 autocorrelation 0.734 and only 61 sign changes in 254 steps (white round-off would give ~127), so it is a slow correlated limit cycle, not arithmetic noise → float32 angular floor 5.40e-6 deg (740x below the reported 0.004 deg); residual is an autocorrelated (lag-1 0.734) controller limit cycle on a 0.001 deg log quantum; simulated fine stage 3.84 MN m/rad = 1/23.4 of the design's.

### SIM-05 · material · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/setup_sim4.py`:115

**Claim.** build_page.py:265 'what runs' row: 'the yoke, the head (frame 70 kg, dish + style + secondary 85 kg) as spring trusses'. coude/synth.py:30 sizes everything from 'dish 60 + style and secondary 25 + frame and fine stage 40 + M3 and neck 30 = 155 kg'.

**Problem.** m_h = 70.0/26 (line 115) and m_d = 85.0/13 (line 132) are per-particle masses whose divisors are the third pass's particle counts. The fourth pass builds 14 particles at m_h (FRAME 8 + RODP 3 + QF 3) and 15 at m_d (BR 3 + QD 3 + VTX 1 + RIM 8). So neither total is what the register claims, in opposite directions, and the head the simulation actually carries is 28 % heavier than the head the rest of the design is sized for.

**Correction.** 14 * 70/26 = 37.69 kg where the page says 70 kg (-46 %). 15 * 85/13 = 98.08 kg, plus SEC = 15.0 kg (line 138) = 113.08 kg where the page says 85 kg (+33 %); the membrane alone is 98 kg against the design's 60 kg (+63 %). Group totals from the rebuilt particle list: head_body 85.69 kg, dish_body 113.08 kg, so the sprung head is 198.8 kg against the design's 155 kg. Whole machine: stalk 54.6, cw1 dry 39.0, cw2 dry 39.0, head 198.8 = 319.4 kg dry, plus 259.6 + 408.9 kg of water = 972.8 kg moving mass, against the design's 155 + 151 + 267 = 573 kg. The fix is m_h = 70.0/14 and m_d = (85.0-15.0)/15 (or 60.0/15 with SEC carrying the 25 kg style+secondary), which also drops M_CW1/M_CW2 and the stalk's axial load. `/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/setup_sim4.py`:419

**Claim.** build_page.py:269 'turn' row: 'the jack from the yoke to a 0.8 m crank on the neck bar (15 kN cap) turns the head from el 90 to the morning sun 30-39 s; jack peak {mus/1e3:.1f} kN'. memo.md:130 '15 kN (its cap) only in the turn'.

**Problem.** `mus` is computed as |KE_MUS*(current length - commanded rest)| - the force an uncapped 1 MN/m spring would exert. It is not the constraint force the solver applies, which is limited by lmax = fmax*dt^2 with fmax = 15 kN (line 188). max over the log is 62605 N at t = 0.0 s, which is the stow start-up transient before the jack is even commanded to move, not the turn. So the page prints a jack peak of 62.6 kN - 4.2x its own stated cap - and attributes it to the turn.

**Correction.** max(max(l['muscles'])) over out/setup4_log.json = 62605 N at t = 0.0; the true maximum over the turn window 0.74*T_S <= t <= T_S is 1374 N. The row should read 'jack peak 1.4 kN in the turn (cap 15 kN)'. To report a real force, read the XPBD multiplier instead: F = lam[k]/dt^2 for the JA-CK constraint, which is what the cap actually clamps.

### SIM-08 · material · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/setup_sim4.py`:58

**Claim.** memo.md:85-87 uses inflated_beam.py's safety factors 'for the simulation's 40 kPa'; memo.md:64-66 'the axial rest lengths are 1.2 % shorter... as in a real inflated beam'; memo.md:173-175 lists only the stub's readout as the known stiffness artefact.

**Problem.** KE_FAB = 1.5e6 is a spring constant per element, so the fabric modulus it represents depends on the mesh, and the mesh changes as the tube grows. A circumferential spring of length `chord` standing for a strip of width `dz` represents E t = ke*chord/dz. Since dz = g*L/n_a grows by up to 3x while ke is fixed, the simulated fabric softens as the tube grows, and none of the values equals the 500 kN/m at which every safety factor in this folder is computed. The memo's 'Open' bullet frames this as a readout artefact of the stub; it is a constitutive error present at full growth too.

**Correction.** chord = 2 R sin(pi/12). Stalk grown (R 0.5, dz = 1.49/8 = 0.186 m): E t = 1.5e6*0.2588/0.186 = 2084 kN/m, 4.2x the assumed 500. Stalk at the stub (dz = 0.0625): 6212 kN/m, 12.4x. cw1 grown (R 0.35, dz 0.200): 1359 kN/m. cw2 grown (dz 0.333): 815 kN/m. So the simulated fabric spans 1.6x to 12.4x the fabric inflated_beam.py certifies, and the stalk is 4.2x stiffer in bending than the EI = 196 kN m2 used in SIM-01 - the sim would show LESS lean than the real hose. Fix: scale ke with the mesh, ke = E_t * dz / chord recomputed inside set_rests() every frame, so the fabric is a material, not a mesh artefact.

- verifier (confirmed): The constitutive point is right and the mesh dependence is real, but two of the quoted numbers need correcting. Derivation E t = ke*chord/dz holds for the circumferential springs alone, and I reproduce 2084 kN/m (stalk grown, dz = 1.49/8 = 0.18625, chord = 2*0.5*sin(pi/12) = 0.2588), 6212 (stalk stub, dz 0.0625), 1359 (cw1 grown) and 815 (cw2 grown) against inflated_beam.py:11's E t = 500 kN/m. But (i) each quad also carries two diagonals at KE_FAB (setup_sim4.py:90), which raise the hoop modulus to 4831 / 17950 / 2584 / 1187 kN/m, so the finding understates its own case; and (ii) bending is g → Hoop E t 2084 kN/m (4831 with diagonals) grown, 6212 (17950) at the stub; axial E t 1079 kN/m (1816 with diagonals) -> EI 713 kN m2 = 3.6x the 196 kN m2 assumed, not 4.2x; the stub is axially softer (362-402 kN/m) than the 500 kN/m certified.
- verifier (confirmed): The code reading is right: KE_FAB = 1.5e6 (line 58) is fixed per spring while the axial ring spacing dz = g L/n_a changes by up to 8x from stub to grown (set_rests, line 316-318, only rescales rest LENGTHS, never ke), so the constitutive modulus the mesh represents drifts. Deriving E t from a strip (a spring of length l standing for a strip of width w: ke = E t w/l): a circumferential spring of length chord = 2R sin(pi/12) standing for a strip of width dz gives E t = ke*chord/dz. My evaluation: stalk grown (R 0.5, chord 0.2588, dz 1.49/8 = 0.1862) 2084 kN/m = 4.17x the 500 kN/m of inflated_bea → hoop E t = ke*chord/dz: 6212 kN/m (stalk stub) to 815 kN/m (cw2 grown), i.e. 1.6x-12.4x the certified 500 kN/m; axial E t = ke*dz/chord = 362 kN/m (stub) to 1079 kN/m (grown), ~1330 kN/m with diagonals, so the stalk's simulated EI is ~2.7x 196 kN m2, not 4.2x

### SIM-09 · material · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/koopman_fit.py`:27

**Claim.** out/koopman4.txt: 'DMDc (identity): one-step RMS error vertex 2.2 cm, axis 0.1 cm, pointing 0.12 deg; 12 s roll-out RMS vertex 34.8 cm, pointing 0.50 deg'. build_page.py:274 presents this as 'the lifted linear model a model-predictive controller runs at the struts' valves and the ring drive'. memo.md:131 diagnoses only that 'the quadratic lift again overfits'.

**Problem.** The linear fit has negative skill against the do-nothing baseline, so it cannot be the model an MPC runs, and the memo's diagnosis (only the quadratic overfits) is wrong. Two causes are visible in the code: the two 'inputs' are functionally dependent - after t >= T_S, el_target() returns the sun's own elevation, so u = [el_t(hour), hour] lies on a 1-D curve - and svd_rank is forced to X.shape[1]+U.shape[1] = 7 (line 26), i.e. no truncation, on a regressor with condition number 1.24e4, so the fit inverts the log's rounding noise (vtx stored to 1 mm, point to 0.001 deg).

**Correction.** Persistence baseline (x_{k+1} = x_k) on the same test split: vertex one-step RMS 1.17 cm against DMDc's 2.2 cm - DMDc is 1.9x WORSE than predicting no motion. Pointing: the test-set signal's own standard deviation is 0.0025 deg and its per-step change RMS is 0.0018 deg, against DMDc's 0.12 deg - the model's error is 49x the signal it claims to predict. The 12 s roll-out is the only channel with any skill: 34.8 cm against a hold-x0 baseline of 60.0 cm over a 1.05 m path. Input collinearity: after removing a cubic in `hour`, el_t's residual is 1.03 deg RMS on a 34.3 deg command range, i.e. the two inputs are 97 % dependent, and 3 % of that is the log's own 0.1 deg rounding. Fixes: excite the plant with an input that is not the sun (a dither on el_bias/az_bias), truncate svd_rank to the knee (rank 4-5 here), log at full precision, and report skill against persistence. Separately, the model is identified at dt 0.133 s of a 720x-compressed day, so its A,B do not describe the real machine's plant at any timescale a real MPC would run.

Persistence x_{k+1} = x_k on the same test rows gives vertex one-step RMS 1.17 cm and pointing 0.0018 deg, against DMDc's reported 2.2 cm / 0.12 deg - which my own plain least-squares fit reproduces at 2.19 cm / 0.1168 deg, so DMDc is 1.87x worse than doing nothing on the vertex and 48x the test signal's own std (0.00245 deg) on pointing. The 12 s roll-out is the only channel with skill: 34.8 cm against a hold-x0 baseline of 60.7 cm over a 1.054 m path (and hol → Persistence 1.17 cm vs DMDc 2.19 cm one-step (1.87x worse); pointing error 0.117 deg against a test signal std of 0.00245 deg (48x); roll-out 34.8 cm vs hold-x0 60.7 cm; regressor cond 1.8e4 at forced full rank 7.

Reloading out/setup4_anim.json['log'] and reproducing koopman_fit.py's X, U and 70/30 chronological split gives n = 301, n_tr = 210, dt = 0.1333 s; running pykoopman DMDc(svd_rank=7) with Identity observables reproduces the published 2.19 cm / 0.05 cm / 0.117 deg. And the exact pressure is already in the solver and is not used: the volume constraint IS a uniform pressure (grad C_i is the area-weighted nodal normal, so the XPBD multiplier divided by dt^2 is the pressure in Pa), so vlam/dt^2 is the true, converged number.

The run reports 182 kPa at the same instant: the readout is 1.9x the uniform value, the difference being the barrel. Same calculation gives 89.2 kPa for cw1 (run: 65) and 53.5 kPa for cw2 (run: 98). Replace line 407 with p = vlam[ti]/dt**2 and the numbers become exact rather than 2x-uncertain. Either way the memo's '10-50 kPa' is wrong by 2-18x and the run's tubes are nowhere near the 40 kPa the certification uses.

### SIM-12 · minor · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/setup_sim4.py`:351

**Claim.** build_page.py:268: '260 and 409 L pumped into them... (the hose walls carry the mass)'; memo.md:34 '91 kg of water per side pumped into the tube's own walls'.

**Problem.** The counterweight water is added as extra mass on the hose's wall nodes while the hose keeps its air volume constraint, so the model is an air-inflated beam whose fabric happens to weigh 260/409 kg. A hose actually full of water is a different object: it is volumetrically near-incompressible, its wall tension is set by rho g h plus the fill pressure rather than by a blower, and it cannot be everted/grown once filled - which matters because the sequence grows the hoses (17-26 s) and fills them (24-30 s) with overlapping windows. There is also no free surface and no fluid inertia, so the 'water's slosh' the memo lists at line 163 as a plant dynamic is not in the model at all.

**Correction.** Either model the filled hose as a rigid-volume liquid tube (replace the gas volume constraint by an equality on the filled fraction and set the wall tension from p_fill + rho g (z_top - z)) or state on the page that the counterweights are lumped masses on a pneumatic tube. Sanity numbers if it is kept as is: cw2's fill is 409 L in a 770 L hose (53 % full), cw1's 260 L in 462 L (56 %) - both are part-full, so a real hose would have a free surface, a static tilt of the water toward the low end, and a first sloshing mode of order sqrt(g/L) ~ 0.35 Hz for the 2.0 m hose, which sits inside the gust band the model already overdamps (SIM-02).

Capacities check: pi R^2 L = 462 L (cw1) and 770 L (cw2), matching the run's printed header, so the fills are 259.6/462 = 56 % and 408.9/770 = 53 % - part-full, as claimed. The growth and fill windows do overlap → Fills 56 % and 53 % of 462 and 770 L; slosh 0.35-0.44 Hz; growth (16.8-26.4 s) and fill (24-29.6 s) overlap by 2.4 s.

### SIM-13 · minor · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/setup_sim/setup_sim4.py`:202

**Claim.** New code added during this audit: dish_load() applies a drag along the wind to RIM+VTX and a pitching couple M = q A D cm sin(2 alpha) as forces on the rim; wind_now() adds a gust of two sinusoids at 4.0 and 1.3 s.

**Problem.** Not yet run (out/production4.log predates it), and it will not answer the wind question on its own. (a) The couple is distributed as cross(Mv, r)/S with S = 0.5*sum|r|^2 over RIM, but r is measured from c_d = mean(RIM+VTX), so sum(r) over RIM is not zero and the couple leaks a spurious net force. (b) The gust has energy only at 0.25 Hz and 0.77 Hz; the structure's own modes are at 2-4 Hz, so nothing can resonate, and the 0.25 Hz component is critically damped by the exp(-4 dt) drag of SIM-02. (c) fext is refreshed once per frame (1/30 s) while the drag depends on the dish's attitude, so the aerodynamic feedback is sampled at 30 Hz against a 2-4 Hz plant - marginal. (d) No relative-velocity term, so there is still no aerodynamic damping.

**Correction.** (a) sum_RIM(r) = 8*(mean(RIM) - VTX)/9 = 8*0.273/9 = 0.243 m along s (SAG = 8.2 - sqrt(8.2^2 - 2.1^2) = 0.273 m); with S = 0.5*8*2.16^2 = 18.7 m^2 and Mv <= q A D cm = 321.9*13.9*4.2*0.12 = 2255 N m at 25 m/s, the spurious force is <= 2255*0.243/18.7 = 29 N against 5816 N of drag - small but free to remove by using c_d = mean(RIM) for r. (b) Replace the two sinusoids by a von Karman or Davenport spectrum sampled to at least 5 Hz, or state that only the quasi-static wind is being tested. (d) Use f = 0.5 rho Cd A |u - v|(u - v) so the dish damps itself: c_aero = rho Cd A U = 167 N s/m at 9 m/s, which is the term SIM-02's numerical drag currently replaces 22-fold.

(b) The gust has energy only at 0.250 and 0.769 Hz while the stalk mode is 2.15 Hz (SIM-02), and 0.25 Hz sits inside the overdamped band. (c) fext is as → Spurious couple force <= 29.4 N (0.5 % of the 25 m/s drag), removable by using c_d = mean(RIM); gust lines at 0.250 and 0.769 Hz; the code has since been run at 9 and 25 m/s, unpublished.

The fourth pass does not wander by degrees: over the 255 late-day entries of out/setup4_log.json the frame's pointing has mean 0.0338 deg and max 0.050 deg (whole tracking window:  → frame pointing over the late day: mean 0.0338 deg, max 0.050 deg (255 samples); p_eq 782/230/245 kPa at t = 0, max 821.5 kPa, 182/65/98 kPa at the end - not '300 -> 45 kPa'

**Checked and found right:**

- The moment balance is correct and it works. I rebuilt the particle model, grew both hoses to their full L and distributed the water exactly as setup_sim4.py:351 does, then took the head's centre of mass about N in the (s, n, e) triad: offset (-0.0078, -0.0031, -0.0067) m on 455.8 kg, i.e. a residual mass-moment of 3.6 kg m about the neck. The gravity moment about the neck axis, m g (a cos el - b sin el), is -31 N m at el 12 and +14 N m at el 90 - under 0.6 % of the head's weight-times-lever before balancing. The yoke+head CoM sits 0.030 m horizontally from the stalk axis at every elevation, giving 0.27 kN m of stalk bending against the 6.3 kN m it would carry unbalanced. My initial suspicion that the code balances only the s-component and leaves the n-component free is technically true but numerically harmless here (b-moment 1.4 kg m), because the head is nearly axisymmetric about the dish axis apart from the crank.
- water_lever(tb, base_off) = base_off + L*(n_a+1)/(2*n_a) is the right lever: setup_sim4.py:351 puts the water on rings[1:], i.e. rings j = 1..n_a at j*L/n_a, whose mean is L(n_a+1)/(2 n_a). For cw1 that is 0.4 + 1.2*7/12 = 1.10 m and for cw2 0.4 + 2.0*7/12 = 1.567 m, matching the run's own printed header exactly.
- The volume constraint is the right idealisation of inflation, and it is a genuinely uniform pressure. grad V at a vertex is the sum of the adjacent triangles' area normals over 3, which is exactly the nodal area normal a uniform pressure would act on, so one scalar volume constraint per closed tube reproduces a uniform internal pressure with the multiplier playing the pressure's role - which is why it survives the 200:1 mass ratio between a 0.15 kg fabric node and the 30 kg cap that an explicit p A n/3 force could not (memo.md:57-61 is right about this).
- The tube meshes are closed and outward-wound, so both the volume constraint and the wind kernel's sign test are correct. I evaluated sum(cross(xi,xj).xk)/6 over each tube's own triangle range at build: stalk 0.375000 m3 against the exact 12-gon prism 0.5*12*0.25*sin(30 deg)*0.50 = 0.375000, cw1 and cw2 0.110250 against 0.110250. Ratio 1.0000 for all three.
- The wind kernel's lumping is right in form, whatever it is applied to. an = 0.5 cross(xj-xi, xk-xi) is the outward area vector; c = an.wn < 0 selects windward faces; fv = wn*(-c)*q*Cd/3 added to each of the three vertices sums to q Cd (A cos theta) along the wind, and summing that over the windward faces of a closed body gives q Cd A_projected. The division by 3 is correct. Its defect is what it is applied to (SIM-01) and the missing leeward suction, not the arithmetic.
- Tension-only fabric with a 1.2 % axial pre-shortening (EPS_AX, setup_sim4.py:175, 318) is the correct inflated-beam idealisation: it puts the wall in the p r / 2 axial tension that a real pressurised tube carries, so the compressed side of a bent tube does not go slack at first load. memo.md:64-66's account of why it was added is right.
- Single precision limits nothing in this model, contrary to memo.md:128. Angular resolution of the pointing readout on the 2.529 m BR baseline is ulp(3 m)/2.529 = 5.4e-6 deg; the float32 volume sum over a tube's 216 triangles differs from float64 by 2.4e-7 relative against a 3.5e-2 over-volume target. Both are 3-5 orders below anything being reported.
- The time compression does not create meaningful inertial loads, and it makes the tracking test harder rather than easier. The turn is a smoothstep through 65 deg in 0.24*T_S = 9.6 s, so peak angular acceleration is 6*1.13/9.6^2 = 0.074 rad/s2, giving 0.18 m/s2 = 1.8 % of g at the 2.4 m rim. The azimuth ring turns at ~4 deg/s, so centripetal acceleration at the 3.5 m rim radius is 0.017 m/s2. And because masses, stiffnesses and g are physical, the structure's modes sit at their real 2-4 Hz while the sun runs 720x fast - so any tracking residual the run reports is pessimistic relative to a real day.
- The jack is modelled softer than the real one, which is conservative for the coarse-pointing claim. KE_MUS = 1.0e6 N/m at a 0.8 m crank gives k_theta = 640 kN m/rad; a real Tr40 screw's own axial stiffness E A / L is order 1.5e8 N/m and even a poor gearbox/nut chain leaves 20-50 MN/m, so the simulation's head-on-jack compliance is 20-80x the design's.
- The two bookkeeping fixes memo.md:118-123 records are real and are correctly implemented. The one-frame sun lead is fixed at setup_sim4.py:398-401 (the frame's end state is judged against hour_end, and at 0.08 deg of sun per frame that was worth exactly the 0.1 deg the memo says). The crank direction now matches geometry.crank_dir at setup_sim4.py:116 (cdir = -cos(psi) n - sin(psi) s with PSI_CRANK = -20 deg), removing the dead centre.
- The fine stage's commanded range is internally consistent with what the register claims: u_tilt clipped to +/-0.025 m and u_piston to +/-0.007 m sum to +/-0.032 m, and 0.032/R_ACT 1.60 = 20 mrad, matching build_page.py's '+-32 mm' and memo.md:144's '+-20 mrad'. The run's largest excursion is 13 mm, so the loop never hits a stop.
- The hoses can hold the water the balance asks for: pi*0.35^2*1.2 = 462 L against 260 L, and pi*0.35^2*2.0 = 770 L against 409 L. The run's own header line reports both capacities correctly.
- The stalk is not axially critical, and the design's own two checks support that. Total moving mass above the deck is 933.8 kg = 9.16 kN, against the crushing force P A = 40e3*pi*0.25 = 31.4 kN (SF 3.4) and Coad eq. 4.2 buckling 227 kN at 40 kPa. The stalk's problem is bending under wind (SIM-01, SIM-03), not compression.
- The solver architecture is described honestly. Graph-coloured Gauss-Seidel XPBD with 46 colours, tension-only fabric, long-range attachments so growth cannot lag the solver, alpha = 1/(ke dt^2) compliance, ground last in the iteration - all of that matches the code, and memo.md:53-56's account of why warp.sim's own unnormalised Jacobi XPBD was abandoned is a legitimate engineering record.
- memo.md:147-153 already concedes the central limitation of the fine-stage claim in its own words ('its columns are 1 MN/m struts and its positions single precision, so its residual is the loop's, not the stage's'). The concession is right in substance even though its stated reason (single precision) is wrong by 740x - the residual really is the loop's.
- The list of physical effects the simulation omits that would change its conclusions, all verified above: (1) any aerodynamic force on the dish, 13.9 m2 and 10.1x the only loaded surface; (2) leeward suction and any pressure distribution over the tubes, only windward pressure is applied; (3) aerodynamic damping - replaced 22-fold by a numerical dashpot; (4) any turbulence spectrum with energy at the structure's 2-4 Hz modes; (5) vortex shedding off the r 0.5 stalk (St 0.2 at 9 m/s gives 1.8 Hz, inside the structural band); (6) the fabric's real constitutive law - the model's E t is a mesh artefact spanning 0.8-6.2 MN/m and varying as the tube grows; (7) wrinkling and post-wrinkling stiffness loss, so the tube's EI never degrades under moment as a real inflated beam's does from M_wrinkle onward; (8) gas compressibility and blower dynamics - the volume constraint is a hard equality, so pressure never sags when the tube is loaded and there is no fill/leak time constant; (9) the free surface, static tilt and slosh of 669 kg of part-full water; (10) the pivot blades' 208 N m/rad restoring spring, the largest still-air elevation torque; (11) the screw jack's backlash, friction, stiction and finite slew rate; (12) any sensor model at all - the loops read the exact dish normal and the exact sun with no noise, bias, latency, quantisation, field-of-view limit or atmospheric refraction (4.4 arcmin = 1.28 mrad at el 12, larger than the whole fine-stage budget); (13) the membrane's own deformation - the dish is a 16-point rigid clique, so it cannot lose figure under gravity or wind; (14) thermal effects on a fabric stalk in Quetta sun (a 30 K wall gradient on a 1.49 m tube at 20e-6/K is 0.9 mm of differential length, ~0.9 mrad of tilt); (15) creep and stress relaxation of a coated fabric held at 20-91 kN/m of hoop for a season; (16) the roof's own compliance under a ~1 tonne concentrated live load, and settlement of the deck ring; (17) any failure or venting mode - the storm answer memo.md:169 gives ('the head comes down face-up onto its stubs') is never simulated, and a sudden pressure loss on a stalk carrying 934 kg is the one dynamic event that matters.

## SHADING

### SH-1 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/coude/optics.py`:31

**Claim.** "the style: a cone from the hole rim (r 0.12) to the secondary rim (r 0.34) ... its inner surface stays outside the return cone (radius 0.078 -> 0.34 over the same span), so it obstructs nothing"; model.py:95-96 builds it as a solid conical SHELL of revolution; memo.md:27-28 and build_page.py:311 carry "style 0 (inside the secondary's disc)".

**Problem.** Only the RETURN cone was checked. The INCOMING converging cone was not. A shell of revolution that widens from r 0.118 at the vertex plane to the secondary's rim at z 3.35 swallows the whole beam: every ray leaves the primary at radius rho > r_sec (outside the style, whose radius at the vertex is only 0.118) and arrives at z = 3.35 at radius rho*(F1-3.35)/(F1-sag) <= r_sec (i.e. at or inside the style's top rim), so it must cross the shell exactly once. Only the marginal ray from rho = 2.1 grazes the rim, and model.py:102 draws the beam only from the rim to 0.98*R_SEC, which is why the error is invisible in the plates.

**Correction.** Recomputed (scratchpad/wf/shading/a_style.py). Ray radius at height z from aperture radius rho on the paraboloid f=4.0: r(z) = rho*(4-z)/(4-rho^2/16). Style: r_s(z) = 0.1184 + (r_sec-0.1184)*z/3.35. Fraction of the illuminated annulus whose ray pierces the shell: 87.9 % with the as-built r_sec = 0.34125 (the remaining 12.1 % is the outer light that misses the undersized secondary anyway, see SH-2), 100.0 % with the sag-correct r_sec = 0.3665. Free-corridor test: the annulus between the return cone r_ret(z) = r_sec*(z+1)/4.35 and the inner edge of the incoming beam r_in(z) = r_sec*(4-z)/(4-sag) closes at z = 1.607 m, r = 0.2197 m (r_ret 0.2106 vs r_in 0.2295 at z 1.5; 0.2528 vs 0.1836 at z 2.0; 0.3370 vs 0.0918 at z 3.0). Above z = 1.61 m NO surface of revolution can stand between the membrane and the secondary. The secondary must be carried by a discrete spider. Cost of the minimum honest fix: 4 vanes 3 mm thick from r 0.3665 to 2.1 project 4*0.003*(2.1-0.3665) = 0.0208 m2 = 0.15 % of pi a^2 = 13.854 m2 - cheap in light, but it puts structure in front of the mirror, which is exactly what the brief forbade.

### SH-4 · material · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/coude/shading.py`:12

**Claim.** ann = (rr > O["r_hole"]) & (rr < H.A_DISH); line 30 net = ann.sum()*PX*PX -> "net aperture (r 0.12..2.1) 13.81 m2" and the headline "TOTAL in front of the membrane 2.45 %"; optics.py:41,43 then add "hole 0.32 %" to "secondary 2.6 %" to reach 3.0 %; build_page.py:309-313 lists hole 0.3 %, secondary 2.3 %, total 2.5 %.

**Problem.** The hole is handled three inconsistent ways. In the raster it is removed from BOTH numerator and denominator (so 2.32 % and 2.45 % are fractions of 13.809 m2, an aperture that pretends the hole is not there). In optics.py it is charged separately as 0.32 % of 13.854 and ADDED to the secondary's 2.64 % - but the hole (r 0.1184) lies wholly inside the secondary's shadow (r 0.341), so that light is already gone and the addition double-counts. The register table's own column does not even sum: 0.3 + 2.3 = 2.6 against a stated total of 2.5.

**Correction.** One number, one denominator. Loss = (secondary's shadow disc) U (hole) = the secondary's disc, since hole is a subset of shadow: pi*0.34125^2/13.854 = 0.36587/13.854 = 2.64 % as designed (2.77 % if the 0.13 % rim-toroid term is kept), or 3.05 % with the sag-correct r_sec (SH-2), or 3.33 % with the sag-correct secondary + rim toroid + a 4x3 mm spider (SH-1). Not 2.45 %, not 3.0 %. Symmetrically, the tube machine's r 0.5 hole IS an independent loss (it is not inside any other shadow) and pi*0.25/13.854 = 5.67 % is correctly additive there - the two machines' holes are being treated the same way when they are not the same case.

- verifier (confirmed): CONFIRMED; every step of their arithmetic reproduces the files exactly. shading.py:12 removes the hole from the annulus, so its net is pi(2.1^2-0.11845^2)=13.8103 m2 and the masks are pi(0.34125^2-0.11845^2)=0.32177 m2 (file 0.321) = 2.330 % (file 2.32), +0.018 toroid = 2.46 % (file 2.45/2.46) -- fractions of an aperture that pretends the hole is not there. optics.py:41,43 then charges the hole again as pi*0.11845^2/pi a^2 = 0.318 % on top of the secondary's 2.641 %, total 2.959 % -> '3.0 %', while 0.11845 < 0.34125 means the hole lies wholly inside the secondary's shadow: a pure double count. → One number, one denominator: pi*r_sec^2/pi a^2 = 2.64 % as built, 2.70 % with the sag-correct rim (2.72 % with the solar disc). **Correction.** The claims happen to be TRUE; I verified them geometrically, which the files never did. For a tracking dish the projection is along the dish axis, so the elevation-axis coordinate is invariant: w = (P-V).e = +-3.61 (posts), +-(2.45+-0.075) (arms), +-3.25 (trunnion blades) - all > a = 2.1, never inside the aperture. Deck ring rail at r 3.61, z 5.18: u = +-3.61 sin(el) + (9.868-5.18) cos(el); the up-sun branch is >= 3.61 for all el (min at el 90), and where the down-sun branch does project inside (u = -0.78 at el 60) it lies behind the membrane, (P-V).s = -3.61 cos(el) - 4.688 sin(el) + 4 = -1.87 m at el 60 and < 0 for every el >= 12. Fourth-pass stalk: (P-V).s = -t sin(el) - NECK = -t sin(el) - 1.8 <= -1.8 m at every elevation. Fix: replace the name filter with the geometric test itself (or an explicit per-part flag), and report which parts were tested.

### SH-9 · minor · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/coude/shading.py`:3

**Claim.** "project every solid of a scene along the sun line onto the aperture plane and count what lies in front of the membrane inside the annulus" - the whole method, in both passes.

**Problem.** It counts only the shadow cast on the incoming parallel light. It never asks whether a body intercepts the CONVERGING beam on its way from the primary to the secondary/strip. Both machines put solid bodies inside the beam cone, and for a concentrator the second channel can dominate the first.

**Correction.** Add a second pass that traces each aperture cell's reflected ray. For the fourth pass this is decisive and is SH-1. For the tube machine, tracing from each membrane point toward F and stopping 0.9 m short, the focal tube intercepts a further 20.9 % of the aperture at el 59 and 60.4 % at el 83 beyond its own shadow (scratchpad/wf/shading/b_tube.py, column 'conv') - an upper bound, since the strip is a one-sided hyperboloid sheet and the other fork's real geometry may let part of that light past. This is a method finding, not a number to publish: it needs the other fork's strip before it can be quoted, but it is the single biggest hole in the shading model as it stands.

- verifier (confirmed): The method gap is real - both rasters test only the parallel-projection shadow - and it is what makes SH-1 decisive. On the tube machine my own march is larger than theirs, not smaller, and it survives the obvious objection that the strip gets the light first: I marched each aperture cell's reflected ray toward F, recorded the first crossing of the strip's own conic (found for 100 % of rays, median 3.20 m from the mirror) and the first entry into the solid wall of the tube (radius profile from geometry.py:76-80, z in [5.0, 8.968]), and compared them (v7_conv2.py). The tube's wall is entered BE → tube intercepts the converging beam ahead of the strip for 31.5 % of the aperture at el 59, 75.0 % at el 83, 1.7 % at el 37 (my march); the finding's 20.9 / 60.4 are conservative
- verifier (confirmed): CONFIRMED as stated in kind: coude/shading.py:2-3 and fact_mount/shading.py:2-3 project along the sun line and test only 'in front of the membrane'; neither traces the reflected ray, which is precisely why SH-1 is invisible in the plates and in out/shading.txt. I reproduced the second channel independently (per-cell march from the membrane point toward F against the model's own tube profile) and get materially different percentages from theirs, and the stop-distance sweep shows most of it is the beam being caught where the secondary belongs, so their own caveat ('an upper bound') is if anythin → Method finding stands. **Claim.** front = cen_z > H.R_SPH - sqrt(R_SPH^2 - cen_r^2) + 0.02 - the 'in front of the membrane' surface is the R 8.2 spherical cap, and model.py draws the membrane as that cap, while optics.py designs the whole Cassegrain on a paraboloid of f = 4.0.

**Problem.** A sphere of R 8.2 has paraxial f = R/2 = 4.1, not 4.0. The optics file and the model/raster do not describe the same mirror. It is 2 mm of sag at the rim (sphere 0.2735 vs parabola 0.2756), harmless for the front/behind test, but it is 2.5 % on the focal length, which is where SH-2 bites.

**Correction.** Pick one. On f = 4.0 (paraboloid, the zoned figure): r_sec = 0.3665, obscuration 3.05 %. On the R 8.2 cap as drawn: f = 4.1, r_sec = 2.1*(4.1-3.35)/(4.1-0.2735) = 0.4116, obscuration pi*0.4116^2/13.854 = 3.84 %, and D_SEC/B/magnification all shift with it.
