### SH-12 · minor · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/stage3/coude/out/shading.txt`:4

**Claim.** "inflated rim toroid: 0.018 m2 = 0.13 %" listed as a shading term (same line in fact_mount/out/shading.txt).

**Problem.** It is a mesh artefact. model.py:84 makes the torus with major radius A_DISH + 0.06 = 2.16 m and minor 0.06, so it spans r = 2.10..2.22 and its intersection with the aperture r < 2.1 has measure zero. The 0.018 m2 is the chordal error of the facetted torus resolved on a 1 cm grid.

**Correction.** Drop the term or state it as a discretisation floor. It happens to be identical (0.018 m2) in both rasters so it cancels from the comparison; it should not appear as 0.13 % of the aperture in either budget.

- verifier (confirmed): Read from model.py:84: makeTorus(H.A_DISH*M + 60, 60, ...) = major 2.16 m, minor 0.06 m, so the body spans r = 2.10..2.22 and, since the raster projects along zl with no lateral shift, its footprint meets r < 2.1 in a set of measure zero. The reported 0.018 m2 is therefore facet noise from the tessellated torus resolved on 1 cm cells - and their sanity bound is right: a full one-cell ring at r 2.1 would be 2*pi*2.1*0.01 = 0.132 m2, so 0.018 m2 is a seventh of one cell width. It appears identically in both rasters and cancels from the comparison. → drop the 0.13 % rim-toroid line from both budgets (or label it a 1 cm discretisation floor); it is not aperture loss
- verifier (confirmed): CONFIRMED. model.py:84 builds the torus with major radius H.A_DISH + 0.06 = 2.16 m and minor 0.06 m in the plane z = SAG, so along the axis it spans exactly r 2.10..2.22 and is tangent to the aperture: its intersection with r < 2.1 has measure zero. The reported 0.018 m2 is the facetted inner circle dipping inside r = 2.1 -- at the tol=2.5 mm used for the mesh the inscribed polygon has about 64 sides and leaves pi*2.1^2 - (N/2)*2.1^2*sin(2pi/N) = 0.0222 m2, the right magnitude, and it is identical (0.018) in both rasters so it cancels from the comparison. It should not appear as 0.13 % of the  → Drop the term (true blocked area inside r<2.1 is exactly zero); if kept, label it a discretisation floor. An inscribed 64-gon at r 2.1 leaves 0.0222 m2 and a 96-gon 0.0099 m2, bracketing the reported 0.018 m2.

**Checked and found right:**

- The per-part raster numbers in fact_mount/out/shading.txt are correct. My independent method (march each aperture cell's ray from the membrane surface along +s through the analytic tube profile of geometry.py:tube_radius and the strip-ring torus) gives, as fractions of the same r>0.5 annulus: el 59 tube 9.86 % (file 9.78), ring 1.46 % (file 1.45); el 37 tube 10.01 % (file 10.01), ring 1.58 % (file 1.58); el 83 tube 1.70 % (file 1.74). Agreement to 0.1 pt. The disagreements in this audit are all in the aggregation and the comparison, not in the raster.
- The arithmetic inside coude/out/shading.txt is self-consistent and reproducible by hand: the style's and the secondary's masks intersected with the annulus are identical, pi*(0.34125^2 - 0.11845^2) = 0.32178 m2 (file 0.321 for both), because the cone projects to exactly the annulus 0.1184..0.34125 and the secondary's disc inside r_hole is excluded from `ann`; /13.809 = 2.330 % (file 2.32); union with the toroid 0.3398/13.809 = 2.461 % (file 2.45).
- The claim that the style's projection ALONG THE SUN LINE lies inside the secondary's disc (optics.py:30) is correct: a cone of revolution from r 0.118 to r 0.341 projects, along its own axis, onto the annulus 0.118..0.341, entirely within the disc r 0.341 that the secondary already shadows. It adds nothing to the incoming shadow. It fails only against the converging beam (SH-1).
- The claim that the mount is behind the membrane and shades nothing is true, for the fourth pass and for the third. I verified it myself since the rasters did not: stalk (P-V).s = -t sin(el) - 1.8 <= -1.8 m at every elevation; third-pass posts, side arms and trunnion blades have invariant projected coordinate w = (P-V).e of 3.61, 2.45 and 3.25 m, all beyond a = 2.1; the deck ring rail is either outside the disc (u >= 3.61 up-sun) or behind it (-1.87 m at el 60). M3, M4, the neck bar, the yoke and both counterweights returned zero from the coude raster, which did include them.
- The invariance of the fourth pass's shading over the year is real, for the reason the memo does not give: for a tracking coaxial design the sun line IS the dish axis, so the projection of any fixed-to-the-head body onto the aperture is independent of elevation and azimuth. The 2.45 % therefore does hold from el 12 to 83 and morning to evening - as a theorem, not as three samples.
- The strong elevation dependence of the vertical tube is real and their raster caught it: the tube's own shadow falls from ~10 % at el 37-59 to 1.7 % at el 83, because a vertical body's projected length along the sun line goes as cos(el). My values 10.01 %, 9.86 %, 1.70 %. The mistake was to publish only the el 59 value as the comparison.
- optics.py:40, hole term for the tube machine: 0.5^2/2.1^2 = 5.669 % of pi a^2 is right, and unlike the fourth pass's hole it is a genuinely additive loss (it is not contained in any other shadow).
- optics.py:40, slot gross area: 0.7*(2.1-0.5) = 1.12 m2 = 8.084 % of pi a^2 is right as a GROSS area (my raster on the curved cap gives 8.26 %, slightly larger because the slot is measured on the sag surface). It is only the marginal contribution that is near zero (SH-3).
- optics.py:11, return beam at the vertex: r_return = r_sec*B/s2 = 0.34125*1.0/4.35 = 0.07845 m, and r_hole = r_return + 0.04 = 0.11845 m - arithmetic correct, and the 40 mm margin is a reasonable choice. (It scales with r_sec, so it moves to 0.1242 m once SH-2 is fixed.)
- fact_mount/shading.py's separation of the mount's parts from the cass machine's own parts is the right reporting structure - it is only the name-based selection feeding it that is broken (SH-5).

The survival case does not fit inside the simulated actuator.

### R-02 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/build_page.py`:293

**Claim.** Sheet 46 'size': 'the head sweeps 7.9 m about the neck, the roof a circle of about 8.5 m about the stalk'; repeated at build_page.py:305 (sheet 51) and coude/memo.md:55-56.

**Problem.** Both numbers omit the secondary and its style - the very parts the fourth pass adds. optics.py:55 computes the sweep as 2*(NECK + A + 0.06) = 2*(1.8+2.1+0.06) = 7.92 m, which (a) adds the along-axis and radial distances linearly instead of in quadrature and (b) stops at the rim, when the secondary sits D_SEC = 3.35 m IN FRONT of the vertex, i.e. NECK + D_SEC = 5.15 m from the neck axis. The 8.5 m roof circle is 2*(sqrt(1.8^2+2.16^2) + 1.4) = 8.42 m, the rim-only figure.

**Correction.** Head sweep about the neck: the farthest point is the secondary rim at 5.15 m, so the swept circle is 2*5.15 = 10.3 m, not 7.9 m (the rim alone is 2*sqrt(1.8^2+2.16^2) = 5.6 m). Roof circle about the stalk: r = sqrt((OFF + r_sec)^2 + ((NECK+D_SEC)*cos el)^2) = sqrt(1.74^2 + (5.15*cos12)^2) = 5.33 m at the design minimum el 12 -> a circle of 10.7 m (86-90 m2). The model's own sweep file confirms the trend: over equinox 8-16 h the max horizontal radius from the stalk axis is 4.86 m (part 'neck bar, spine, secondary axis H08') -> 9.7 m already. Against the third pass's 9.2 x 7.8 m = 72 m2 (fact_mount/out/synth.txt:51), the fourth pass needs MORE roof (~+20 % area and a larger maximum dimension), not less. Sheet 51's framing 'the stalk 2 m tall instead of two 4.9 m posts' hides that cost.

- verifier (confirmed): Recomputed from scratch. optics.py:55 forms 2*(NECK + A + 0.06) = 2*(1.8+2.1+0.06) = 7.92 m, a linear sum of an along-axis distance and a radial one — not any swept diameter. Rotating about the neck axis, a point at along-axis distance d and own radius r sweeps a circle of radius hypot(d,r): the rim (d 1.8, r 2.16) gives 2.8117 m -> 5.62 m across, and the secondary (d NECK+D_SEC = 5.15, r 0.34125) gives 5.1613 m -> 10.32 m across. So 7.92 both overstates the rim sweep and understates the true one by 30 %. For the roof I derived the horizontal radius about the vertical stalk axis exactly: for a → Head sweep about the neck: 10.32 m across (secondary rim at 5.161 m); the rim alone is 5.62 m. Roof circle about the stalk: 10.70 m diameter (r 5.349 m, 89.9 m2) at el 12; 9.72 m over the drawn 8-16 h sweep. Not 7.9 m and not 8.5 m.
- verifier (confirmed): optics.py:55 computes 2*(NECK+A+0.06) = 2*(1.8+2.16) = 7.92 m; both defects are real. (a) The head rotates about the neck axis e; a point at N + d_s*s + d_u*u has perpendicular distance sqrt(d_s^2 + d_u_h^2) since s is perpendicular to e - quadrature, not the linear sum. (b) The secondary sits at NECK + D_SEC = 1.8+3.35 = 5.15 m along s from N, so the swept radius is 5.15 (5.161 with r_sec 0.341 out of plane), not 2.16+1.8. For the rim alone the auditor's 2*sqrt(1.8^2+2.16^2)=5.62 m omits the sag: vertex-to-rim along s is 0.276 m (a^2/4f), giving sqrt(2.0756^2+2.16^2)=2.995 -> 5.99 m. Roof cir → Head sweep about the neck 2 x 5.16 = 10.3 m (secondary rim at NECK+D_SEC = 5.15 m); rim alone 2 x 2.995 = 5.99 m. Roof circle about the stalk at el 12: r = sqrt(1.741^2 + (5.15 cos12)^2) = 5.33 m -> 10.66 m diameter, 89.3 m2, against the third pass's 9.2 x 7.8 = 71.8 m2.

### R-03 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/build_page.py`:293

**Claim.** Sheet 46 'size': 'no F to keep, so it scales with the dish' (also optics.txt:26 'no F to keep, so f/D and size scale freely'; memo.md:56 'with no F to keep the whole machine scales with the dish').

**Problem.** There is an F to keep: the cass machine's F2 at env z 6.14, which fixes z_neck = 6.14 + F3 = 6.94 (optics.py:53). With the deck at z 5.0 that leaves only 1.94 m of stalk, and the rim clearance at the minimum elevation el 12 is already only 0.20 m. No computation anywhere supports the free-scaling claim.

**Correction.** Scale the whole machine by k (NECK, a, F3 all -> k x): clearance(k) = (6.14 + 0.8k) + 1.8k*sin12 - (2.1k + 0.06)*cos12 - 5.0 = 1.14 - 0.880k + 0.06... evaluated: k=1.0 -> 0.20 m, k=1.1 -> 0.11 m, k=1.3 -> -0.06 m. The rim strikes the deck at k = 1.26, i.e. a <= 2.65 m. Scaling DOWN is free; scaling up is capped at +26 % by the fixed F2 height, and further by the etendue throw of 4.2 m per relay stage (optics.txt:11). Correct statement: 'F2's height above the deck caps the dish at a ~ 2.65 m; below that the machine scales freely.'

I evaluated the clearance myself: c(k) = (6.14 + 0.8k) + 1.8k sin12 - (2.1k + 0.06) cos12 - 5.0 = 1.081311 - 0.879865k, giving 0.2014 m at k = 1 (matching optics.txt:25's 0.20), 0.1135 at 1.1, 0.0255 at 1.2, -0.0625 at 1.3. The root is k = 1.2289, not 1.26: a_max = 2.1*1.2289 = 2.581 m, not 2.65 m. Two further caveats the auditor missed. Correct statement: 'F2's fixed height 1.14 m above the deck caps a uniformly scaled machine at a ~ 2.58 m; holding the image distance B at 1.0 m instead lifts the cap, but neither case is computed.'
- verifier (confirmed): There is an F to keep, exactly as claimed: optics.py:53 `z_neck = 6.14 + F3` pins M4 0.8 m above the cass machine's fixed F2, and optics.py:54 prints the resulting 0.20 m rim clearance at el 12. Scaling NECK, a and F3 by k: 6.14 + 0.8k + 1.8k sin12 - (2.1k+0.06) cos12 - 5.0 = 1.081311 - 0.879847k. ### R-07 · material · CONFIRMED
`/Users/faezs/ARTIST-compliant/tutorials/design/compliant/build_page.py`:300

**Claim.** Sheet 48 'loads': '3.2 / 10.2 kN m at 9 / 25 m/s -> 1.8 / 5.7 kN per blade in plane, buckling 11.4 kN'; sheet 46's 'neck' row: 'blade SF 6.5 / 2.0 at 9 / 25 m/s'.

**Problem.** coude/synth.py:34 computes 'F_ring = M_v/(2*R_OUT); per = F_ring/2  # two blades in the loaded plane per stage'. With N_BL = 6 radial blades at 60 deg spacing there is exactly ONE blade in each loaded plane (a = 0 and a = 180), so the /2 halves the force without justification. It also takes the couple lever as the outer radius 0.45 m rather than the blade mid-radius 0.39 m. A proper distributed reaction (rotation about a transverse axis reacted by axis-direction blade forces, sum over cos^2 a = N/2 = 3) gives F_max = M/(3R).

**Correction.** F_max = M/(3 * 0.39) = M/1.17. At 9 m/s (M = 3.18 kN m): 2.72 kN, SF = 11.4/2.72 = 4.2, not 6.5. At 25 m/s (M = 10.25 kN m): 8.76 kN, SF = 1.3, not 2.0. Even with the head's weight properly counterbalanced (see R-08) the survival SF is 8.12/1.17 = 6.94 kN -> SF 1.6. The register should print SF 4.2 / 1.3-1.6, i.e. the single trunk's survival margin is roughly a third of the third pass's SF 4.7 (fact_mount/out/synth.txt:26). Separately: P_cr = 4*pi^2*E*(W*T^3/12)/L^2 = 11.4 kN is the RADIAL column-buckling mode over the 120 mm blade length, but the load resolved above is along the neck axis (the blade's 250 mm width direction), so the two are not the same mechanism; the correct check there is in-plane shear buckling of a 120 x 250 x 1 mm plate, tau_cr = k*pi^2*E/(12(1-nu^2))*(t/b)^2, which for k ~ 2-9 and b = 120 mm gives 23-109 MPa against an applied 8.76e3/(0.120*0.001) = 73 MPa - marginal at best.

- verifier (confirmed): The defect is real and I reproduce the mechanism, but the corrected safety factors need R-08 folded in. coude/synth.py:34 computes F_ring = M_v/(2*R_OUT) then per = F_ring/2 = M/(4*0.45) = M/1.8, halving on the comment 'two blades in the loaded plane per stage' — but the two blades in a diametral plane sit on OPPOSITE sides of the axis and both carry the couple, so neither is a load-sharing pair. I re-derived the reaction from the twist: for a rotation theta about a transverse axis, blade k at angle a_k on the mid-radius R = 0.39 m displaces delta_k = theta R cos(a_k) along the neck axis (its  → F_max = M/(3 R_mid) = M/1.17. With the correct vector-summed pivot moments (2.37 / 8.39 kN m): 2.03 / 7.17 kN per blade, SF 5.6 at 9 m/s and 1.6 at 25 m/s against the 11.4 kN radial buckling load — and that load is the wrong mode anyway; the in-plane shear check gives 25-113 MPa capacity against 73 
- verifier (confirmed): I re-derived the blade reaction independently. With axis = e (synth.py:14) and stations p = 0.39*(cos a, 0, sin a) at a = 2 pi k/6 (synth.py:18-19), a rotation theta about any axis perpendicular to e displaces station k by theta*R*cos a along e - which lies IN the blade's plane (nrm = rad x axis), the stiff direction. Hence M = k_ax theta R^2 sum(cos^2 a) = 3 k_ax theta R^2 and F_max = M/(3R). synth.py:34's `F_ring = M_v/(2*R_OUT); per = F_ring/2` gives M/1.8: the /2 is unjustified (with 6 blades at 60 deg there is one blade at a=0 and one at a=180, and the correct distributed sum is 3, not 4) → F_max = M/(3 R_station) = M/1.17. 9 m/s: 2.72 kN, SF 4.2. 25 m/s: 8.76 kN, SF 1.3 (1.64 with the weight removed per R-08); 4.8/1.5 if the outer radius 0.45 m is allowed as the lever. Applied in-plane shear is 35.0 MPa, not 73 MPa.

Wrong: 'cancelled by t → Pivot transverse moment = sqrt(M_z^2 + M_x^2) = 2.37 kN m at 9 m/s and 8.39 kN m at 25 m/s (crosswind about the vertical 1.05 / 8.12 kN m; head-and-neck-counterweight weight about the transverse horizontal ~2.1 kN m, NOT cancelled by the yoke counterweight, which acts about the azimuth axis). Not 3.
- verifier (confirmed): The axis argument is exact and dimensional. A vertical force W at horizontal offset r along e gives r x F = -1.4 W (e x z_hat), a HORIZONTAL moment vector transverse to the neck axis; its component about the vertical is identically zero. The crosswind, horizontal and perpendicular to e, gives a purely vertical moment 1.4 D. synth.py:33 adds the two as scalars (`M_v = m_head*9.81*OFF + D*OFF`) and its own inline comment concedes the weight is 'balanced by the yoke's counterweight in service, taken here unbalanced', while synth.txt:9 specifies exactly that 267 kg counterweight. Arithmetic verifi → In service the pivot sees the crosswind alone: M_z = 751.3*1.4 = 1.05 kN m at 9 m/s, 5797*1.4 = 8.12 kN m at 25. The head's weight is 155*9.81*1.4 = 2.13 kN m about the horizontal axis transverse to the neck. Unbalanced resultant (vector sum) 2.37 and 8.39 kN m, not the scalar 3.18 and 10.25.

Sheet 38's own accuracy claim (build_page.py:201, fact_mount/out/synth.txt:37) is '4 urad' from a 315 N m static drag asymmetry at 9 m/s — a wind deflection, and this run has no wind (R-01), so 90 MN m/rad and 62 urad/mL are a stiffness and a resolution, neither of which this run demonstrates. The float-precision excuse does not survive: 72.8 urad at f 4.0 m is 0.29 mm of spot motion, four orders above float32 → 'In still air the fine stage holds the dish to 73 urad (0.073 mrad) mean; sheet 38's 4 urad is a static wind deflection at 90 MN m/rad and is not exercised by this run.' The 9 m/s run gives 3.66 mrad. No computation on the page earns the word 'microradians'.

## Fourth-pass optics (stage3/coude): Cassegrain conjugates, secondary/hole/style sizing, image and etendue, M3/M4 and the bore, the shading budget and the net-light claim, the printed roof line

### OPT-01 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/optics.py`:13

**Claim.** r_return_vertex = r_sec*B/s2 = 0.078 m, therefore hole r = 0.12 m, '0.32 % of the aperture' (optics.txt:4; memo.md:14 'back through a hole of r 0.12 m').

**Problem.** This is the AXIAL marginal cone only. The sun is not a point: the image has radius r_img, and every image point is fed by the whole secondary, so the beam envelope at the vertex plane is a linear blend of the secondary rim and the image, not the axial cone. The hole as sized clips the outer field (the solar limb and the slope-error skirt), i.e. it throws away real power. This is the single biggest error in the pass and it propagates to the style, the plenum-film hole and the shading budget.

**Correction.** Envelope at z=0: r = (1-t)*r_sec + t*r_img with t = z_sec/(z_sec+B) = 3.3885/4.3885 = 0.7721. With the file's own r_sec 0.3413 and r_img 0.1379: r = 0.2279*0.3448 + 0.7721*0.1379 = 0.1850 m. With the corrected r_img 0.1512: 0.1954 m. Full Monte-Carlo trace through the exact paraboloid (z=r^2/16) and exact hyperboloid ((z-1.5)^2/1.85^2 - r^2/2.8275 = 1), 3e5 rays, sun disc 4.65 mrad, 0.5 mrad rms slope on both mirrors: max footprint at z=0 is 0.2516 m, 99th percentile 0.1799 m. Throughput of the coded hole r 0.11845 = 77.2 % (80.8 % with no slope error): 19-23 % of the light is lost. Required hole r = 0.20 m for 99.85 %, r = 0.235 m for 100 %, i.e. envelope 0.195 + 0.04 margin. This costs nothing in shading (see OPT-11): r 0.235 < r_sec 0.372, so the enlarged hole stays inside the secondary's own shadow.

- verifier (confirmed): Original is wrong and the correction is right. My own closed form: envelope at z=0 is (1-u)*r_sec + u*r_img with u = z_hit/(z_hit+B) = 3.38849/4.38849 = 0.77213, giving 0.1850 m with r_sec 0.3448 / r_img 0.1379 and 0.1954 m with r_img 0.15125 - both auditor values reproduced to 4 decimals. Independent Monte-Carlo conic trace (4e5 rays, hit points sampled ON the paraboloid so the rim bounds the aperture, sun disc 4.65 mrad, 0.5 mrad Gaussian normal perturbation on both mirrors, exact hyperboloid (z-1.5)^2/1.85^2 - r^2/2.8275 = 1): max radius at z=0 0.2498 m (secondary unclipped) / 0.2324 m (sec → vertex-plane envelope 0.185 m (file's own r_img) to 0.195 m (corrected r_img); hole r >= 0.20 m for 99.85 %, r 0.22-0.235 m for 100 %. Coded r 0.11845 passes only 77-79 %.
- verifier (confirmed): Reproduced independently. optics.py:13 r_return_vertex = r_sec*B/s2 = 0.34125*1.0/4.35 = 0.078448 is the axial marginal cone only. The correct footprint at the vertex plane is the linear blend of the secondary rim and the image: with t = D_SEC/(D_SEC+B) = 3.35/4.35 = 0.77011, env(0) = (1-t)*r_sec + t*r_img = 0.22989*0.34125 + 0.77011*0.137862 = 0.18462 m, i.e. 2.35x the coded 0.078 and 1.56x the coded hole 0.11845. ### OPT-02 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/optics.py`:31

**Claim.** 'its inner surface stays outside the return cone (radius 0.078 -> 0.34 over the same span), so it obstructs nothing' (also memo.md:15-17, optics.txt:6).

**Problem.** False on two counts. (a) Even for the axial cone the style is only TANGENT, not clear: both the style and the return cone are r_sec at the secondary, so the clearance is exactly zero there and any wall thickness intrudes. (b) With the field included the style's inner wall lies INSIDE the beam over its whole length. The style is not a shield around the beam; it is a knife inside it.

**Correction.** Style inner radius: style(z) = r_hole + (r_sec - r_hole)*z/D_SEC = 0.1184 + 0.0665*z. Beam envelope: env(z) = (1-t(z))*r_sec + t(z)*r_img with t(z) = (D_SEC - z + sag)/(D_SEC + B + sag) -> env(z) = 0.1850 + 0.0467*z. style(z) - env(z) = -0.0666 + 0.0199*z < 0 for all z < 3.35 m: the style is inside the beam everywhere, cutting 67 mm into it at the vertex plane. Correction: the style must run from r ~0.235 at the vertex plane to r >= 0.39 at the secondary (i.e. it must attach BEHIND the secondary's rim, not to it, because the beam fills the secondary out to its edge), giving a silhouette slightly larger than the secondary's rather than 'shading nothing more'.

### OPT-03 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/optics.py`:12

**Claim.** r_sec = A*s1/F1 = 0.34 m; 'its own obscuration 2.6 % of the aperture' (optics.txt:3).

**Problem.** Paraxial: it ignores both the primary's sag (0.276 m at the rim, 6.9 % of f - the dish is f/0.95, not a slow paraxial system) and the field. The primary's sag alone makes the marginal ray higher (0.3665 at z=3.35) and the secondary's own sag (0.0385) pushes the hit back, which almost exactly cancels it (0.3448 on axis) - so the paraxial number is a lucky 1 % on the AXIAL ray. But the off-axis rays from the solar limb overfill it, and a secondary of 0.3413 vignettes the outer aperture.

**Correction.** Exact axial marginal ray (iterated): h = A*(F1-z)/(F1 - A^2/(4F1)) with z = D_SEC + sag_hyp(h), sag_hyp(h) = h^2/(R(1+sqrt(1-(1+K)h^2/R^2))), R = b^2/a = 2.8275/1.85 = 1.5284, K = -e^2 = -1.8262 -> h = 0.3448 at z = 3.3885. Full trace with the solar disc: max hit radius on the hyperboloid 0.3612 m (sun only) and 0.3725 m (sun + 0.5 mrad slope). Required r_sec >= 0.3725 (+ figure margin), not 0.3413: +9.1 % in radius, obscuration 3.15 % of pi a^2 rather than 2.64 %.

The spine must be moved off the axis (e.g. three struts on a 0.30 m bolt circle outside the envelope, which is 0.170-0.194 m over z -0.6..-1.8) or replaced by a tube around the beam.

It therefore runs along the beam axis through the image at z = -1.0 and through M3's own station at z = -1.8. ### OPT-06 · material · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/optics.py`:55

**Claim.** 'roof: the head's sweep, 7.9 m across about the neck, plus the stalk 1.4 m to the side: about 9 x 4 m against 9.2 x 7.8 m' (optics.txt:26).

**Problem.** Two errors. (a) 2*(NECK + a + 0.06) adds a perpendicular offset to a radius as if they were collinear; the swept radius about the neck is the hypotenuse. (b) '9 x 4 m' treats the machine as a fixed-azimuth strip, but the whole head turns in azimuth on the stalk, so the footprint is a swept region about the stalk, not a 4 m-wide band. The 4 m figure is the dish's diameter, which is only the extent along the neck at ONE azimuth.

**Correction.** (a) sweep diameter about the neck = 2*sqrt(NECK^2 + (a+0.06)^2) = 2*sqrt(1.8^2 + 2.16^2) = 5.62 m, not 7.92 m. (b) Swept over the real Quetta year (7317 sun positions, el >= 12, own solar model from physics_hp.sun), the horizontal envelope of dish rim + counterweights about the stalk at (1.25, 0) is x -2.73..5.19 (7.92 m) by y -3.94..2.97 (6.91 m), max radius about the stalk 3.98 m. The tube machine on the same rim and the same positions gives 7.81 x 9.09 m, which is the memo's '9.2 x 7.8'. So the correct line is 'about 7.9 x 6.9 m (a 8.0 m circle about the stalk) against the tube machine's 9.2 x 7.8' - the conclusion (smaller, 55 m2 vs 71 m2) survives, the numbers do not. Note memo.md:55-56's separate statement, 'the roof is a circle of about 8.5 m about the stalk', is right (conservative by 0.5 m); optics.txt:26 contradicts it.

(b) My sweep, 73 175 sun positions (every day, 0.05 h, el >= 12, physics_hp.sun, rim r 2.16 sampled every 2 deg plus both counterweight spheres): fourth pass x -2.86..5.30 (8.16 m) by y -4.06..3.25 (7.31 m), max radius about the stalk 4.11 m -> an 8.2 m circle, 53 m2. ### OPT-07 · material · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/optics.py`:14

**Claim.** r_img = f_eff*SUN + 2*SLOPE*f_eff*0.5 = 0.138 m, printed as 'sun 0.124 + slope 0.013' (optics.txt:7).

**Problem.** The '*0.5' silently cancels the factor 2 and leaves sigma*f_eff. A surface slope error sigma deflects the reflected ray by 2*sigma, so the smear radius is 2*sigma*f_eff. Separately, only the PRIMARY's slope is in the budget: the fourth pass adds a secondary and two powered folds, each with its own figure error, and none of them appears.

**Correction.** 2*SLOPE*f_eff = 2*0.5e-3*26.769 = 0.0268 m, so a linear sum gives r_img = 0.1245 + 0.0268 = 0.1512 m; a quadrature sum gives sqrt(0.1245^2 + 0.0268^2) = 0.1273 m. The traced image with 0.5 mrad rms on the primary AND the secondary: 90 % radius 0.1361 m, 100 % 0.2300 m, rms 0.0961 m. The coded 0.1379 is therefore defensible only as a ~90 % containment radius; the derivation printed beside it is wrong, and every downstream envelope (hole, style, M3, bore, F2) should be sized on the 99-100 % radius, i.e. 0.15-0.20 m, not 0.138.

A surface slope sigma deflects the ray by 2*sigma, so the smear radius is 2*SLOPE*f_eff = 0.026769. Linear sum 0.15125, quadrature 0.12732 - both auditor values reproduced. My traced image at z = -1.0 with 0.5 mrad rms normal error on BOTH mirrors: 90 % radius 0.1361 m, 99 % 0.1693, 99.9 % 0.1932, max 0.2376, rms 0.0961 - the auditor's 0.1361 and 0.0961 exactly. So 0.1379 is defensib → r_img = 0.1512 m (linear, 2-sigma) or 0.1273 m (quadrature); traced containment 0.1361 m at 90 %, 0.1693 at 99 %, 0.2376 max. The coded 0.1379 is a 90 % radius, not an envelope.

### OPT-10 · material · CONFIRMED

Clearance is fine (envelope 0.275 m at the deck hole r 0.9, 0.587 m at the underground ellipsoid r 1.0, 6.0 m below F2), but the spot at the pot and the underground relay's aberration both scale by ~2.4x and the statement should say so.

### OPT-13 · material · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/optics.py`:13

**Claim.** r_hole = r_return_vertex + 0.04 -- a flat 40 mm margin, with no statement of what it is a margin against.

**Problem.** The hole is the tightest aperture in the whole train and it is also where every alignment error lands, magnified by the Cassegrain. A 40 mm margin is far too little for a machine whose complaint of record is wind. The style is a 3.35 m cantilever carrying the secondary; its tip deflection walks straight into this margin, and so does pointing.

**Correction.** Traced sensitivities (exact conic trace, secondary decentred / whole head mispointed): a secondary decentre of D moves the image by 5.2*D (1 mm -> 5.1 mm, 5 mm -> 26.0 mm, 10 mm -> 52.7 mm) and grows the vertex-plane envelope from 0.1772 to 0.1927 m at 5 mm. A pointing error d grows the vertex-plane envelope at ~20 m/rad (0.5 mrad -> +9 mm, 2 mrad -> +40 mm, 5 mrad -> +104 mm), with the image centre moving f_eff*d as expected (1 mrad -> 26.5 mm vs f_eff*d = 26.8 mm). So a 40 mm margin buys either ~2 mrad (0.11 deg) of pointing OR ~8 mm of style tip deflection, and nothing for both. A hole sized for 0.17 deg (3 mrad) of pointing plus 5 mm of style deflection needs r = 0.195 + 0.062 + 0.026 = 0.283 m; for the 0.7 deg pointing tolerance quoted for the cass machine it would need r ~0.44 m, i.e. back to the tube machine's r 0.5, and then it would no longer hide inside the secondary's shadow. The optics case for the small hole depends on a pointing and stiffness budget that is nowhere written down.

- verifier (confirmed): The complaint stands and the sensitivities reproduce, with two of the auditor's sub-numbers slightly off. Translating the hyperboloid in the trace: image centroid moves 5.23 mm per mm of decentre (5 mm -> 26.4 mm, 10 mm -> 52.7 mm), matching 5.1/26.0/52.7. Vertex-plane envelope with no slope error grows 0.1780 -> 0.1840 -> 0.1940 -> 0.2108 at 0/2/5/10 mm decentre, i.e. 3.2 m/m, so a 40 mm margin buys ~12.5 mm of style tip deflection, not ~8. Whole-bundle mispointing, no slope: max radius at z=0 grows +9.5 mm at 0.5 mrad, +19.2 at 1, +40.1 at 2, +61.0 at 3, +102.7 at 5 - 20.5 m/rad, exactly the → Envelope grows 20.5 m/rad in pointing and 3.2 m/m in secondary decentre. A 40 mm margin on a correct 0.195 m base buys 1.9 mrad of pointing OR 12.5 mm of style deflection. 3 mrad + 5 mm needs r 0.272 m; 0.7 deg needs r 0.44-0.48 m and then no longer hides in the secondary's shadow.
- verifier (confirmed): Confirmed by my own perturbed trace (120k rays each, hyperboloid translated in x, incoming bundle tilted). Secondary decentre: image centroid moves -5.21x the decentre, linear from 1 to 10 mm (1 mm -> 5.21 mm, 5 mm -> 25.8, 10 mm -> 52.1), matching the auditor's 5.2 and the closed form (m-1) = 5.69. Pointing: centroid moves f_eff*d (1 mrad -> 26.3 mm measured against 26.8 predicted), and the vertex-plane envelope grows at the blend factor t*f_eff = 0.770*26.77 = 20.6 m/rad asymptotically (my 99.9th-pct proxy gives +23.5 mm at 2 mrad and +81.7 mm at 5 mrad, i.e. 12-16 m/rad, a lower bound becau → image shift 5.2x secondary decentre and 26.8 m/rad for pointing; vertex envelope grows ~20 m/rad; the 40 mm margin buys ~2 mrad of pointing or ~8 mm of style deflection, and a 3 mrad + 5 mm budget needs r = 0.28 m

`stage3/coude/optics.py`:8

**Claim.** 'primary: a 2.1 m, effective focal length 4.0 m (the zoned membrane, 0.5 mrad slope)'; SUN and SLOPE are declared locally on line 10.

A sphere of R 8.2 has a paraxial focus at 4.10 m, not 4.00, and its marginal ray crosses at 3.959 m (0.14 m of longitudinal spherical aberration, ~19 mm circle of least confusion) unless the zoning really removes it. The optics and the CAD/raster are therefore built on two different surfaces, and the 0.5 mrad figure that makes them agree is asserted in a docstring rather than derived or cited anywhere in this directory.

**Correction.** State the surface once. If f = 4.0 with 0.5 mrad is the zoned figure, physics_hp.R_SPH must not be the shape the model and the raster use; if the membrane is R 8.2, then s1 = F1 - D_SEC = 0.75, m = 5.467, f_eff = 21.87 m, and every downstream number (image 0.113 m, half-cone 5.35 deg, F2 scale) changes by 18-22 %. Either way, r_img must carry the secondary's and the two folds' slope errors as well as the primary's.

The spherical-aberration arithmetic also checks: marginal crossing R - R/(2cos(asin(a/R))) = 8.2 - 8.2/1.93330 = 3.9586 m against the paraxial 4.100, LSA 0.14 → if the surface is the R 8.2 sphere: f = 4.10, s1 = 0.75, m = 5.80, f_eff = 23.78 m, r_sec = 0.384, image 0.111, half-cone 5.05 deg -- an 11-13 % shift, not 18-22 %; sphere SA alone gives LSA 0.141 m and a ~19 mm circle of least confusion at prime focus

**Checked and found right:**

- Cassegrain conjugates and scale (optics.py:11): s1 = F1 - D_SEC = 0.65, s2 = D_SEC + B = 4.35, m = s2/s1 = 6.6923, f_eff = F1*m = 26.769 m. Reproduced exactly; the traced image scale confirms it (a 1 mrad pointing error moves the image 26.5 mm against f_eff*d = 26.8 mm).
- The hyperboloid's parameters (optics.py:12) are exactly right and mutually consistent: e = (s2+s1)/(s2-s1) = 5.0/3.7 = 1.35135, a_h = (s2-s1)/2 = 1.85, c_h = (s2+s1)/2 = 2.50. Check: c - a = 0.65 = s1 (the vertex sits s1 from the near focus) and c + a = 4.35 = s2. Derived independently, the vertex radius R = b^2/a = (2.5^2 - 1.85^2)/1.85 = 1.52838 m satisfies the mirror equation 1/s1 - 1/s2 = 1.30858 = 2/R. Being an exact conic pair, the secondary is stigmatic for the two foci - no aberration is introduced there, as the file implies.
- The image cone half-angle (optics.py:15, optics.txt:7): arctan(r_sec/s2) = 4.486 deg. The exact ray trace, with the secondary's sag included, gives 4.4925 deg - agreement to 0.007 deg.
- The etendue arithmetic (optics.py:22): pi*a^2 * pi*theta_sun^2 = 13.854 * pi*(4.65e-3)^2 = 9.411e-4 m2 sr = 9.41 cm2 sr. Correct, and 4.65 mrad is the right solar angular radius (0.266 deg).
- The claim that both folds sit at 45 deg at every elevation and azimuth, with no el/2 drive and nothing to slave (optics.py:35), is exactly true, and I could not break it. Using the model's own normals n3 = (e - s)/sqrt(2) and n4 = (z_hat - e)/sqrt(2): the beam arrives at M3 along -s, and d_out = -s - 2((-s).n3)n3 = -s - (e - s) = -e, using only s.e = 0 - which holds at every elevation because the neck is horizontal and perpendicular to the sun's vertical plane. At M4, d_in = -e gives d_out = -e - (z_hat - e) = -z_hat. Equivalently: M3 sits at the intersection of the dish and neck axes and rotates rigidly with the head about the neck, and a rotation about an axis leaves a vector along that axis fixed, so the outgoing ray is invariant. This is a genuinely correct coude/Nasmyth arrangement.
- Consequently the relayed image I2, 0.8 m along the neck axis from M3, is stationary in the stalk frame at every elevation, and the beam through the hollow trunnion is elevation-independent by construction. It fits the r 0.30 bore with real margin: envelope = r_img + d*tan(half_cone) = 0.168, 0.177, 0.186, 0.194 m at d = 0.2, 0.3, 0.4, 0.5 m from I2 using the CORRECTED r_img 0.1512 and half-cone 4.894 deg - i.e. at worst 65 % of the bore radius over a 1 m length. The answer to 'does the beam fit the bore at all elevations 12-83' is yes, comfortably.
- The paraxial secondary radius r_sec = A*s1/F1 = 0.34125 is, on the axial marginal ray, right to 1 %: the primary's rim sag (0.2756 m) would raise the exact hit to 0.3665, and the secondary's own sag (0.0385 m) pushes the intersection back to z = 3.3885 and brings it down to 0.3448. The two errors nearly cancel. (The field is what breaks it - see OPT-03.)
- The style's SILHOUETTE claim is right on its own terms (optics.py:30): a cone whose radius grows monotonically from r_hole to r_sec projects, along an on-axis sun line, into a disc of exactly r_sec, so it adds no shading beyond the secondary's. shading.py's raster confirms it - the style and the secondary each report 0.321 m2 and their union is also 0.321 m2 (shading.txt:2-3,5).
- shading.py's raster is internally consistent and its numbers are reproducible: the reported 0.321 m2 is exactly pi*(r_sec^2 - r_hole^2) = pi*(0.34125^2 - 0.11845^2) = 0.3218 m2, the annulus mask having excluded the hole, and the reported net aperture 13.81 m2 = pi*2.1^2 - pi*0.11845^2. Nothing is fudged.
- The reflection count (optics.py:37): primary + secondary + M3 + M4 = 4 above ground against the tube machine's primary + strip = 2, so two extra, and 0.94^2 = 0.8836. The arithmetic is right; only the baseline it multiplies is wrong (OPT-04).
- z_neck and the low-elevation clearance (optics.py:53-54): z_neck = 6.14 + F3 = 6.94 m puts the second relayed image on the cass machine's F2 as intended, and the lowest rim point at el 12 is z_neck + NECK*sin(el) - (a+0.06)*cos(el) = 6.94 + 0.374 - 2.113 = 5.201, i.e. 0.20 m over the deck. Both reproduced exactly.
- 'no F to keep, so f/D and size scale freely' (optics.py:55) is true: the tube machine's dish must orbit a fixed F at 4.0 m, which pins f; the fourth pass has no fixed point above the deck except F2 at the bore mouth, and z_neck simply follows F3. The whole head does scale with the dish.
- The footprint conclusion, though not its numbers, holds: swept over 7317 Quetta sun positions with el >= 12, the fourth pass occupies 7.92 x 6.91 m (55 m2) against the tube machine's 7.81 x 9.09 m (71 m2) computed the same way, and memo.md:56's 'a circle of about 8.5 m about the stalk' is correct and conservative against the computed 7.96 m.
- The beam clears everything below M4: with the corrected F2 image (r 0.202, half-angle 3.675 deg) the envelope is 0.275 m at the deck hole (r 0.9), and 0.587 m at the underground ellipsoid 6.0 m below F2 (disc r 1.0, bore inner r 0.88). So 'the bore below unchanged' is true as a clearance statement, even though the image scale is not (OPT-10).
- memo.md:68-70 is honest about what has not been done: M3/M4 aberration at 45 deg over a 0.14 m field, the style's wind load and the secondary's alignment on it, and the hollow pivot's centre shift are all listed as open rather than claimed. The finding OPT-13 quantifies how expensive the style item actually is, but the memo does not pretend otherwise.

## MOUNT STRUCTURES (stage3/coude hollow cartwheel pivot, counterweights, jack, stalk, slew ring; stage3/fact_mount trunnion + fine stage; fact/fact_core rank checks)

### MS-01 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/synth.py`:26

**Claim.** th = 0.25*SY*2*L_B/(E_S*T_B) -> '+-25.8 deg per stage at 0.25 sigma_y; +-17.8 needed per stage -> 0.17 sigma_y at el 12 and 83' (out/synth.txt:4).

**Problem.** That formula is the CONSTANT-CURVATURE result, valid only for a strip that straddles the rotation centre (the third pass's crossed blades, fact_mount/synth.py:46, do straddle it). The coude blades are an ANNULUS: R_IN 0.33, R_OUT 0.45, so the blade's radial length L=0.12 sits 0.33 m OUT from the axis (synth.py:12,19; model.py:20,49). When the hub turns by theta the blade's inner edge must translate tangentially by R_IN*theta AND rotate by theta, while the outer edge is clamped to the fixed ring. The bending is displacement-dominated, not curvature-dominated. At the required 17.75 deg/stage the inner edge would have to move 0.33*0.3098 = 103 mm sideways across a blade only 120 mm long - physically absurd, and the file never notices because FACT (an infinitesimal-twist theory) says nothing about strain.

**Correction.** Euler-Bernoulli with v(0)=v'(0)=0 (outer clamped), v(L)=delta=R_IN*theta, v'(L)=theta gives v(x)=ax^2+bx^3, a=3delta/L^2-theta/L, b=theta/L^2-2delta/L^3. Curvature v''(0)=6R_IN*theta/L^2-2theta/L = (6*0.33/0.0144 - 2/0.12)theta = 120.83 theta /m; v''(L) = -104.17 theta. The straddling case gives theta/L = 8.33 theta. Amplification 14.5x. sigma = E(t/2)|v''| = 12083 MPa per rad. theta_allow at 0.25 sigma_y = 375e6/12.083e9 = 0.03103 rad = +-1.78 deg per stage (not 25.8). At the required 0.3098 rad/stage sigma = 3743 MPa = 2.50 sigma_y: the blades fail, they do not sit at 0.17 sigma_y. Fixes, all computed from sigma=E(t/2)(6R_IN/L^2-2/L)theta = 0.25sigma_y: keep t=1.0 mm and grow the blade to L=0.330 m (R_OUT 0.660, pivot OD 1.32 m); or t=0.5 mm, L=0.248 m (OD 1.16 m); or t=0.3 mm, L=0.198 m (OD 1.06 m); or keep 120 mm blades and use 20 stages in series. The bore is what forces this: a rotational flexure's strain scales with R_IN/L, so 'hollow' and 'large angle' are in direct conflict at this diameter. Propagates verbatim to out/optics.txt:22, memo.md:40 and the register sheets.

BUT the auditor's correction is itself wrong: the sign of the imposed end rotation is inverted. ### MS-02 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/synth.py`:28

**Claim.** k_bl = 2*E_S*I_b/L_B per blade -> '417 N m/rad per stage, 208 for the two in series' (out/synth.txt:5); memo.md:40 'spring 208 N m/rad for the series pair'.

**Problem.** 2EI/L is the torque of a strip in pure constant curvature (again the straddling geometry). For the annular blade the torque about the axis is the generalized force conjugate to theta including the tangential translation R_IN*theta, which is 31x larger. The consequence is not cosmetic: the jack sizing at synth.py:44 treats the flexure spring as negligible, and it is not - it dominates.

**Correction.** U = (EI/2) integral v''^2 dx with the shape above; T = dU/dtheta = 2U/theta. Numerically (EI = 200e9*0.250*1e-9/12 = 4.1667 N m^2, L 0.12, R_IN 0.33): integral v''^2 dx = 514.6 theta^2/m, U = 1072.1 theta^2, k = 2144 N m/rad PER BLADE. Per stage (6 blades) 12 865 N m/rad; two stages in series 6432 N m/rad - 30.9x their 417/208. Restoring torque at the ends of travel (+-35.5 deg = 0.6196 rad) = 6432*0.6196 = 3985 N m = 3.99 kN m, not the 129 N m their 208 implies. At the worst jack arm 0.392 m that is 10.2 kN of jack force in STILL AIR, larger than the 4.0 kN they quote for 9 m/s wind and comparable to the 31 kN they quote for 25 m/s. (Clapeyron check: their stiffness-matrix form 12v+6L*theta is the wrong sign convention; the correct element sub-block is [[12,-6L],[-6L,4L^2]], which reproduces the energy integral exactly.)

### MS-03 · blocking · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/synth.py`:33

**Claim.** M_v = m_head*9.81*OFF + D*OFF, logged as 'moment about the vertical through the pivot 3.2 kN m (head weight at 1.4 m + drag 751 N)' and 10.2 kN m at 25 m/s (out/synth.txt:6-7, memo.md:41-43).

**Problem.** Three errors compounded. (a) A vertical force can produce no moment about a vertical axis: r x F with r along the neck axis e and F along -z gives a moment about h (the horizontal axis perpendicular to the neck), i.e. a bending moment in the VERTICAL PLANE containing the neck axis - the head-weight moment and the crosswind moment are about two perpendicular axes and cannot be added as scalars. (b) The lever is wrong: PIV_C = 0.7 (model.py:20), so the pivot centre is 0.7 m from the stalk and the head at OFF=1.4 m is 0.7 m from it, 0.9 m from the ground stage at y0=-0.20 (model.py:46) - not 1.4 m. (c) The mass is wrong in the other direction: the 151 kg head counterweight also hangs at e=1.4 (model.py:76), so the weight the pivot carries is (155+151)*9.81 = 2998 N, not 1521 N.

**Correction.** Resolve in the orthonormal triad (h, e, z). Weight: the counterweight puts the head CoM ON the neck axis, so W = 2998 N acts through the point e = 1.4 and the moment on the ground stage (e = 0.5) is M_h = 2998*0.9 = 2.70 kN m about h, constant at every elevation, plus a transverse shear of 2998 N; zero moment about e and zero about z. Wind D along the dish axis s applied at the dish centre (r = 0.9 e + 1.8 s): the 1.8 s part is parallel to the force, so M = 0.9 D (e x s) = 0.9 D (sin(el) h - cos(el) z), magnitude 0.9 D independent of elevation. Resultant transverse moment at the ground stage, worst alignment: 9 m/s -> sqrt((2698+671)^2+82^2) = 3.37 kN m (el 83); 25 m/s -> 7.92 kN m (el 83); at el 12 the wind part is nearly all about the VERTICAL (Mz = -5.10 kN m at 25 m/s) and the weight part all about h, giving 6.35 kN m. So the correct worst case is 7.9 kN m about a horizontal transverse axis, not 10.2 kN m about the vertical. With synth's own stage spacing (+-0.15, line 22) the lever is 0.85 and the weight moment 2.55 kN m.

Triad checked: h x e = z, e x z = h, z x h = e (geometry.py:27). (b) PIV_C=0.7, stages at +-0.20 (model.py:20,46), so the loaded (ground) stage sits at e=0.5 and the lever from the head at e=1.4 is 0.90 m, not 1.4; the two stages are in SERIES so the ground stage carries the lar → D9 = 41.715*1.3*13.8544 = 751.3 N, D25 = 5797.2 N. Weight on the pivot 2998 N; weight moment about h at the ground stage 2698 N m (model's +-0.20) / 2548 N m (synth's +-0.15). Worst transverse moment, drag along the dish axis: 9 m/s 3.37 kN m at el 83 (2.92 at el 12); 25 m/s 7.90 kN m at el 83 (6.35

### MS-04 · material · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/synth.py`:34

**Claim.** F_ring = M_v/(2*R_OUT); per = F_ring/2  # two blades in the loaded plane per stage -> '1.77 kN per blade' at 9 m/s and '5.69 kN' at 25 m/s.

**Problem.** M/(2*R_OUT)/2 = M/1.80 is an ad-hoc couple at the outer radius shared between two blades; it ignores the four other blades that carry sin(a) of the load and it uses R_OUT rather than where the blade actually reacts. It under-predicts the peak blade force by a factor 1.54.

**Correction.** Under a transverse moment M the hub tilts by theta about a transverse axis; the compatible motion at a blade at polar angle a is an AXIAL (along the neck) displacement -theta*r*sin(a) at its inner edge, resisted in-plane. Equal in-plane stiffnesses give F_k = F_max sin(a_k) and M = sum F_k r sin(a_k) = F_max r sum sin^2(a_k) = 3 r F_max for N=6 (sum sin^2(a+phi) = 3 for ANY phi, so the cartwheel is transversely isotropic - their 'two blades in the loaded plane' is consistent with this count, only the arm is wrong). F_max = M/(3 r): with r = mean radius 0.39, F = M/1.17; taking the hub reaction at R_IN = 0.33, F = M/0.99. With the corrected M of MS-03 (7.92 kN m at 25 m/s): 6.77-8.00 kN per blade, against their 5.69 kN. At 9 m/s (3.37 kN m): 2.88-3.40 kN.

- verifier (confirmed): Verified. The blades' stiff (in-plane) directions are radial and axial, so a transverse moment is reacted as AXIAL in-plane forces at the blade inner edges, F_k = F_max sin(a_k), M = F_max r sum sin^2(a_k) = 3 r F_max for N=6; sum sin^2(a_k+phi)=3 for any phi (sum cos 2(a_k+phi)=0), so the cartwheel is transversely isotropic and the file's 'two blades in the loaded plane' count is coincidentally the right count with the wrong arm. The file's M/(2*R_OUT)/2 = M/1.80 versus M/(3r) = M/1.17 (r = mean 0.39) or M/0.99 (r = R_IN 0.33, where the force actually lands on the intermediate ring): 1.54-1.8 → F_max = M/(3r). With the corrected M of MS-03: 25 m/s 6.75 kN (r=0.39) to 7.98 kN (r=0.33), against the file's 5.69 kN; 9 m/s 2.88-3.40 kN against 1.77 kN. Both auditor figures reproduced to <1 %.
- verifier (confirmed): Confirmed that the file under-predicts, though the cleanest proof is one the auditor missed: synth.py:34 sets F_ring = M/(2*R_OUT), which is ALREADY the per-blade force of a couple whose two forces sit at the two blades in the loaded plane; dividing by 2 again is a double count, so the file is half its own model. The auditor's distribution is also right in form - sum sin^2(a_k+phi) = 3 exactly for six blades at any phase (verified numerically for seven phases), so the cartwheel is transversely isotropic - and the reaction arm is R_IN = 0.33 (the hub-side edge), giving M/(3*0.33) = M/0.99. CAVE → Per-blade in-plane (axial) force M/(3*R_IN) = M/0.99. ### MS-05 · material · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/synth.py`:35

**Claim.** '... kN per blade in plane: SF {Pcr/per}' -> SF 6.5 at 9 m/s and 2.0 at 25 m/s against Pcr = 4 pi^2 E I/L^2 = 11.4 kN (line 28).

**Problem.** Category error: Pcr with I = W t^3/12 is the buckling load of the blade in RADIAL compression (buckling in its thin, tangential direction). The blade force produced by the transverse moment is an AXIAL-direction in-plane shear across a 250 mm x 120 mm x 1 mm plate - it cannot buckle the blade as a column. The load that DOES compress the blades radially is the transverse SHEAR through the pivot (the head weight plus the wind force), which the file never computes.

**Correction.** (a) In-plane shear path: sigma_bend = 6(F L/2)/(t W^2) = 6*(6766*0.06)/(0.001*0.0625) = 39.0 MPa and tau = F/(W t) = 27.1 MPa at 25 m/s. Plate shear buckling of the 250x120x1 blade (clamped, a/b = 2.08, k_s ~ 8.1): tau_cr = k_s pi^2 E/(12(1-nu^2)) (t/b)^2 = 101.7 MPa -> V_cr = 25.4 kN, SF 3.8 at 25 m/s and 8.8 at 9 m/s. (b) Column-buckling path: transverse shear V = |W + D sin(el)| combined with D cos(el) = 3745 N at 9 m/s and 8781 N at 25 m/s (el 83); radial force distribution F_k = (2V/N) sin(a_k) gives a peak blade compression of 1248 N and 2927 N -> SF 9.2 and 3.9 against the 11.42 kN column value. (c) Neither check may be read alone: at the design rotation the blade is a circular arc offset e0 = L*theta/8 = 4.65 mm from its chord, so the compression acts on a bent strut - see MS-14 for the same interaction in fact_mount.

### MS-06 · material · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/synth.py`:36

**Claim.** tau9, tau25 = D9*(O['NECK'] + 0.3), D25*(O['NECK'] + 0.3) -> 'elevation torque about the neck: wind 1.6 kN m at 9 m/s, 12.2 at 25' (out/synth.txt:8, memo.md:52).

**Problem.** The full axial drag is given a 2.1 m lever about the elevation axis. But the drag on a dish acts essentially along the dish axis s and is applied on that axis, 1.8 m in front of the neck: its LINE OF ACTION passes through the point N on the elevation axis, so its moment about that axis is exactly zero. The 1.8 m arm belongs only to the in-plane (tangential) component of the aerodynamic force; the normal force earns only the centre-of-pressure offset. The 1.8 and the 0.3 are then double-counted.

**Correction.** With F = F_N s + F_T n applied at 1.8 s + x_cp n, tau_e = (r x F).e = |1.8 F_T - x_cp F_N|. Using C_N = C_D = 1.3 as elsewhere in the file, a flat-plate in-plane coefficient C_T <= 0.10 (the edge-on frontal area of the dish, 4.2 m x 0.27 m sag, at C_D 1.2 gives C_T,eq = 0.098) and x_cp = 0.30-0.42 m (0.14-0.20 of the radius): tau_el = (1.8*0.10 + x_cp*1.3) q A = 0.33-0.42 kN m at 9 m/s and 2.5-3.2 kN m at 25 m/s, against their 1.58 and 12.17 kN m - overstated 3.8-4.8x. Jack force at the worst arm 0.392 m falls from 4.0/31.0 kN to 1.1/8.3 kN. But the sizing does not get easier: with the corrected pivot stiffness (MS-02) the flexure alone demands 3.99 kN m = 10.2 kN of jack force at the ends of travel, so the jack is spring-dominated, not wind-dominated, and the file's whole ordering of the load cases is inverted.

The number is in fact the absolute physical ceiling: 1.3*q*A*2.1 = 1578 / 12 174 N m is identically the file's tau9/tau25, i.e. it puts the entire centre of pressure on the extreme rim of the dish. The file's implicit alternative (a purely horizontal resultant at the dish centre, giving 1.8 D sin el = 10.4 kN m at el 83) is no better, since it requires an in-plane force of D sin(el) - a tangent → tau_el = |1.8 F_T - x_cp F_N|. - verifier (confirmed): The zero-moment identity is exact and decisive: the drag is applied on the dish axis at r = 1.8 s with F = D s, so (1.8 s) x (D s) = 0 and its moment about the elevation axis e is identically zero. synth.py:36's tau = D*(NECK+0.3) = D*2.1 reproduces the file's 1578 and 12 174 N m, and the 2.1 m lever has no equilibrium justification: NECK is the distance along s from the elevation axis to the vertex, i.e. exactly the direction in which the lever vanishes. ### MS-07 · material · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/synth.py`:39

**Claim.** jack_geom(..., off=0.8) and model.py:80: the jack runs from the yoke bracket at e = +0.6 to the crank on the head at e = 1.4, i.e. skewed 0.8 m along the neck axis; only its planar share Lp/L is used, for the torque.

**Problem.** The function correctly discounts the force by Lp/L for the torque, then throws the other component away. The axial share 0.8/L is 0.38-0.57 of the jack force over el 12-83, and it acts at the crank, 0.80 m off the neck axis. That is a transverse moment fed straight into the same flexure blades, on top of MS-03, and it is nowhere in the load list.

**Correction.** Axial component F_e = F_jack*0.8/L: at el 12 L=1.40 -> 0.571 F, el 47 L=1.79 -> 0.446 F, el 83 L=2.11 -> 0.379 F. Parasitic transverse moment = F_e * |crank| = F_e * 0.80 m. At their own jack forces this is 1.43 kN m at 4.0 kN (9 m/s) and 11.07 kN m at 31 kN (25 m/s) - the latter larger than the entire weight-plus-wind moment of MS-03 (7.9 kN m). At the corrected spring-dominated 10.2 kN it is 3.64 kN m. Fix: put the jack anchor at the crank's own e-station (off = 0), or use two jacks straddling the crank plane so their axial components cancel.

- verifier (confirmed): Confirmed by re-running the file's own jack_geom. The jack is skewed off=0.8 m along the neck axis (synth.py:39; anchor at e-station 0.6, model.py:80, crank at e-station 1.4, model.py:78); the function keeps only the planar share Lp/L for the torque and discards the axial share 0.8/L, which acts at a crank tip 0.80 m off the neck axis and therefore feeds a transverse moment 0.64*F/L straight into the same blades. Reproduced: L = 1.400 / 1.793 / 2.109 m at el 12 / 47 / 83, axial share 0.571 / 0.446 / 0.379, parasitic moment 0.457 / 0.357 / 0.303 kN m per kN of jack force. The auditor's magnitud → Parasitic transverse moment = 0.64*F_jack/L. At the corrected spring-driven jack force: 12.6 kN at el 12 -> 5.76 kN m; 21.0 kN at el 83 -> 6.38 kN m (auditor said 3.64 kN m). Compare the corrected weight+wind pivot moment 3.4-7.9 kN m: the discarded component is not a correction term, it is a co-equ
- verifier (confirmed): The geometry check reproduces synth.py:39-42 exactly: L runs 1.4002-2.1089 m over el 12-83, the minimum torque arm is 0.3924 m at el 83, and the axial share 0.8/L there is 0.379 (0.571 at el 12, 0.446 at el 47). The function multiplies the force by Lp/L for the torque and discards the axial component 0.8/L, which acts at the crank tip 0.80 m off the neck axis and therefore feeds a transverse moment straight into the pivot blades - a load that appears nowhere in synth.py's list. Their arithmetic is slightly off: at 31 kN the parasitic moment is F*(0.8/L)*0.8 = 31000*0.379*0.8 = 9.42 kN m at the → Parasitic transverse moment = 0.8*F*0.8/L: 1.22 kN m at the file's 4.0 kN jack, 9.42 kN m at its 31 kN, and 6.38 kN m at the corrected 21.0 kN spring-dominated force (all at el 83, L = 2.109 m).

### MS-09 · material · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/synth.py`:46

**Claim.** '{(m_head + m_cw1)*OFF/1.6:.0f} kg 1.6 m beyond the stalk on the yoke (about the azimuth axis)' -> 267 kg (model.py:63, memo.md:48).

**Problem.** The arithmetic is right ((155+150.6)*1.4/1.6 = 267.8 kg, and the drawn r 0.40 m sphere holds 268 kg of water), but the counterweight is not justified anywhere and it buys nothing structural. It does not reduce the pivot moment (MS-03), the jack force, or the elevation torque by a single newton-metre - it only removes a static overturning moment from the slew ring and the stalk, and that moment is trivial for both.

**Correction.** Without the counterweight the slew ring and stalk carry a static overturning moment of (155+150.6)*9.81*1.4 = 4.20 kN m. In the stalk as drawn (model.py:33, OD 1.0 m, 40 mm wall: A = 0.1206 m^2, I = 0.013922 m^4, Z = 0.02784 m^3) that is 151 kPa - four orders below yield; adding the 25 m/s wind case gives 0.53 MPa. A 1.1 m PCD slew ring (model.py:34) has a tilting-moment rating an order above 4.2 kN m. The counterweight costs 268 kg of water plus ~115 kg of yoke arm as drawn (a 1.3 m solid 120 mm bar) = +383 kg on a 306 kg rotating assembly, i.e. it more than doubles the azimuth inertia, the slew-ring axial load (5.63 kN balanced vs 3.00 kN unbalanced) and the deck load, to delete a 4.2 kN m moment that neither part notices. Recommend deleting it and stating the ring's moment rating instead.

Unbalanced overturning at the ring = 305.6*9.81*1.4 = 4197 N m; the stalk as drawn (r 0.5/0.46, model.py:33) has Z = 0.027843 m^3, so 151 kPa - and the tip rotation it causes is 4197*1.49/(E I) = 2.3 urad, optically invisible. Costs verified: 268 kg of water plus the drawn yoke  → Counterweight arithmetic correct (267.4 kg; sphere 268.1 kg). Moment deleted: 4.20 kN m, = 151 kPa and 2.3 urad in the stalk as drawn. Mass added: 383 kg on a 306 kg assembly. Recommendation to delete it and quote the ring's rating instead is sound but the rating itself remains uncomputed.

### MS-14 · material · CONFIRMED
`stage3/fact_mount/synth.py`:61

**Claim.** '258 MPa = 0.17 sigma_y at el 12 and 83' for the crossed trunnion blades, and separately 'worst blade 906 N in plane vs fixed-fixed buckling 9139 N -> SF 10.1' (line 88).

**Problem.** The bending stress and the compression are checked as if they were independent. They are not: at the end of travel the blade is a circular arc, so the compression acts on a strut with a built-in bow and amplifies the bending it already carries. (The 258 MPa figure and the 2EI/L spring ARE correct for this geometry - the blades cross on the axis, line 46 - unlike the coude case of MS-01.)

**Correction.** Arc offset from the chord e0 = rho(1-cos(theta/2)) ~ L*theta/8 = 0.120*0.30979/8 = 4.65 mm. With P/Pcr = 906/9139 = 0.099, the amplifier 1/(1-P/Pcr) = 1.11 and the extra moment P e0 * 1.11 = 4.7 N m -> sigma = M(t/2)/I = 140 MPa, total 398 MPa = 0.27 sigma_y at 9 m/s. At 25 m/s, P = 1940 N, P/Pcr = 0.21, amplifier 1.27, extra moment 11.4 N m -> 343 MPa, total 602 MPa = 0.40 sigma_y. Still below yield, but 0.17 sigma_y is not the number to publish and the fatigue margin at 0.40 sigma_y with daily reversal is a different conversation.

### MS-17 · minor · CONFIRMED
`fact/fact_core.py`:30

**Claim.** blade() returns [wire(p,u), wire(p,v), wire(p + 50*u, v)] - 'two concurrent + one offset: rank 3'.

**Problem.** The 50 is a bare literal written for the self-test's millimetre coordinates (line 70 uses points at 100, 87). Every stage-3 caller works in METRES, so the third constraint line of every blade is placed 50 m away from the blade. It stays in the blade's plane, so the rank result is unaffected, but it makes the wrench matrix badly scaled and it means the 'blade' the algebra sees is 50 m long - a silent trap for anyone who later adds a finite-extent or stiffness-weighted check.

**Correction.** Pass the offset as a fraction of the blade's own size (e.g. 0.4*L_B). Verified harmless today: with offset 50 m the coude stage gives singular values [86.88, 86.88, 2.449, 1.727, 1.727, 0] -> rank 5, DOF 1, condition ratio s1/s5 = 50.3; with offset 0.05 m, [2.490, 2.490, 2.449, 0.667, 0.667, 0] -> rank 5, DOF 1, ratio 3.7. Same answer, 14x worse conditioning against the fixed 1e-9 rank tolerance in dcm_core.interface_freedom.

Re-running the coude stage through dcm_core.interface_freedom: with offset 50 m the singular values of C @ DELTA are [86.62, 86.62, 2.449, 1.732, 1.732, 0] -> rank 5, DOF 1, s1/s5 = 50.0; with offset 0.05 m they are [2.45, 2.45, 2.449, 0.678, 0.678, 0] -> rank 5, DOF 1, s1/s5 = 3.6. Same topological answer (the offset line stays in → Rank 5 / DOF 1 in both cases; conditioning s1/s5 = 50.0 at offset 50 m vs 3.6 at offset 0.05 m (auditor's 50.3 and 3.7, same to rounding). Harmless today, scale-dependent by construction.

With fact_core.py:30's literal 50 (metres, for every stage-3 caller): singular values [86.883, 86.883, 2.449, 1.727, 1.727, 0.000], rank 5, DOF 1, s1/s5 = 50.3.
