### MS-19 · minor · CONFIRMED
`stage3/coude/synth.py`:23

**Claim.** each stage is analysed separately ('hollow cartwheel stage A ... DOF 1', 'stage B ... DOF 1'); the assembly is never analysed.

**Problem.** Two 1R stages in series about the SAME axis give the assembly two rotational freedoms, one of which - the intermediate ring's rotation - is unactuated. Statically it is harmless (equal torque, equal split), but it is a free mode carrying the intermediate ring's inertia, and the file's own claim 'exact 1R' (line 47) is true of a stage and not of the pivot.

**Correction.** State it as the third pass does ('two stages in series', fact_mount/synth.py:63) and give the mode: with the corrected per-stage stiffness 12 865 N m/rad (MS-02), the intermediate ring (an annulus 0.30-0.33 m, 0.40 m long) sees a torsional resonance against 2x12 865 = 25.7 kN m/rad; its polar inertia decides whether that lands anywhere near the gust spectrum. With the file's own 417 N m/rad it would be 30x lower in stiffness and 5.6x lower in frequency.

- verifier (confirmed): Confirmed, with a correction to the auditor's framing. dcm_core.series_freedom takes the span of the interfaces' freedom twists, and both stages are the SAME rotation line, so the stack's OUTPUT freedom space is 1-dimensional - the file's 'exact 1R' is not wrong about the output. What is true, and unstated anywhere in stage3/coude, is that the three-body chain (ground ring, intermediate ring, moving ring) has two configuration coordinates, and the second - the intermediate ring's own rotation - is unactuated. It is not a zero-stiffness mode: with the moving ring held the intermediate ring sees → The unactuated mode's stiffness is 2 k_stage = 53.2 kN m/rad with the corrected stiffness of MS-02 (0.83 kN m/rad with the file's 417). Intermediate ring as drawn (annulus 0.30-0.33 m, 0.70 m long, model.py:44) = 326 kg, J = 32.4 kg m^2, so f = 6.45 Hz corrected and 0.81 Hz on the file's own spring 
- verifier (confirmed): The substance is right but the DOF wording is wrong and I can show it with the repository's own algebra: running dcm_core.series_freedom on the two coude stages returns rank 1, a single rotation about the neck axis, because both stages have the SAME freedom twist. So the assembly's freedom space is 1R and synth.py:47's 'exact 1R' is defensible in FACT terms; what actually exists is an internal, unactuated mechanism coordinate (the intermediate ring's own rotation, with the output being the sum), not a second freedom of the output. The real content - that the file never analyses the assembly, a → Output freedom space is 1R (dcm_core.series_freedom returns rank 1); the extra coordinate is an internal unactuated mode. With the corrected stiffness the intermediate ring sees 2 x 26 615 = 53.2 kN m/rad, not the auditor's 2 x 12 865 = 25.7 nor the file's 2 x 417.

**Checked and found right:**

- FACT constraint algebra of the hollow cartwheel is correct (coude/synth.py:18-25): the plane through a blade at radius 0.39 with normal cross(rad, axis) does contain the axis LINE, so all 18 constraint lines meet the axis, rank 5, DOF 1, and the surviving twist is the rotation about the neck. Reproduced independently through dcm_core.interface_freedom (singular values [2.490, 2.490, 2.449, 0.667, 0.667, 0]); the printed reciprocal product 2.8e-17 is genuine. The claim that the beam can pass through the bore is kinematically true - it is only the ELASTICITY that fails (MS-01).
- The six-blade cartwheel really is transversely isotropic: sum sin^2(a_k + phi) = 3 for any phi at N = 6 (equivalently sum cos(2(a_k+phi)) = 0), so 'two blades in the loaded plane per stage' is a correct count of the effective number; only the moment arm attached to it is wrong.
- Pcr = 4 pi^2 E I / L^2 = 11 423 N (coude) and 9139 N (fact_mount) both reproduce exactly from I = W t^3/12, and the clamped-column model with I about the thin axis is the right one for a strip with FREE unloaded edges - the plate-buckling alternative (k pi^2 D/(b^2 t)) gives a higher value for the same geometry, so this is the conservative choice.
- fact_mount's blade range and spring ARE correct for its geometry: the blades cross ON the axis (synth.py:46, points p on the axis), which is exactly the straddling case for which delta = theta L/2 gives constant curvature theta/L, sigma = E t theta/(2L) and k = EI/L per strip. +-25.8 deg per stage, 258 MPa = 0.17 sigma_y and 2EI/L = 55.6 N m/rad per crossed pair all check out. The coude pass broke them by copying them onto an annulus.
- fact_mount's worst_blade() decomposition is right despite the confusing naming: because the two blade families are at +-45 deg, each family's plane NORMAL is the other family's in-plane direction, so max(|f.u_p|, |f.u_m|)/N_CELL does give the true peak in-plane blade load. Reproduced by hand at el 44 crosswind: f = (-332, 0, -3510) N, |f.u_p|/3 = 906 N; and 1940 N at 25 m/s.
- The two-block trunnion argument is sound and its self-criticism reproduces: the crosswind moment D*G = 3.0 kN m at 9 m/s becomes +-462 N along the dish axis across the 6.5 m block spacing; the abandoned single block at F would have loaded its blades D9*G/0.33/sqrt(2)/2 = 3.22 kN against 9.14 kN buckling with the +-17.75 deg bending on top - the file's 3.2 kN is exact.
- The head counterweight balance is dimensionally and physically correct: m_cw1 = (60*1.8 + 25*3.4 + 40*1.2)/1.6 = 150.6 kg puts the head's centre of mass ON the neck axis, which makes both the pivot's transverse moment and the azimuth balance independent of elevation - a real and non-obvious property, and the file gets it. The azimuth counterweight arithmetic is also exact ((155+150.6)*1.4/1.6 = 267.8 kg) and the drawn 0.40 m sphere holds 268 kg of water (4/3 pi 0.40^3 = 0.2681 m^3).
- The jack kinematics reproduce exactly from synth.py's own jack_geom: L = 1.400-2.109 m over el 12-83, stroke 0.709 m (a single-stage screw does fit), effective arm 0.392-0.660 m. The arm formula |ck x d_hat| * Lp/L is the correct torque arm for a jack skewed out of the crank plane - the geometry is right even though the discarded axial component is not (MS-07).
- fact_mount's jack holding stiffness is defensible: a Tr40 screw (core area 855 mm^2) at 2.6 m gives EA/L = 65.8 MN/m, times the 0.9 m lever squared = 53 MN m/rad, so '~50 MN m/rad per jack' checks. And the pivot flexure, even at my corrected 6.4 kN m/rad, is 4e-4 of the jack's rotational stiffness, so pointing under wind is jack-limited: 27 urad at the corrected 9 m/s torque, comfortably inside the fine stage's +-20 mrad.
- The fine-stage blade formulas are the standard ones and all reproduce: S-bend sigma = 3 E t delta/L^2 = 130 MPa at delta = R theta = 29 mm; tangential EA/L = 40 MN/m; in-plane (thermal) stiffness E t b^3/L^3 = 711 kN/m; out-of-plane 12EI/L^3 = 444 N/m; thermal force 457 N and 114 MPa from the 0.64 mm Al/steel mismatch over 40 K. The FACT result (three tangential blades -> exactly tip/tilt/piston, rank 3) is correct and the reciprocal products are genuinely zero.
- Coad's inflated-beam buckling expression in setup_sim/inflated_beam.py:12 is transcribed correctly: at high pressure it reduces to E pi^3 R^3 t/L^2, which is exactly pi^2 E I/L^2 with I = pi R^3 t for a thin-wall tube. The post's 114.1 kN at 40 kPa reproduces term by term, as does the wrinkling moment M_w = 0.5 pi p r^3 and the crushing force p pi R^2. Only the applied moment is wrong (MS-16).
- The drag basis reproduces from physics_hp.py:24: q9 = 0.5*1.03*81 = 41.72 Pa, q25 = 321.88 Pa, A = pi*2.1^2 = 13.854 m^2, so D9 = 751.3 N and D25 = 5797.2 N are internally consistent everywhere they are used (whether 9 and 25 m/s are the right speeds for Quetta, and whether a mean or a gust, is another dimension's question).
- optics.txt:25's clearance statement is right: at el 12 the lowest rim point is 6.94 + 1.8 sin(12) - 2.1 cos(12) - 0.06 = 5.20 m, i.e. 0.20 m over the deck.
- The two-fixed-fold argument that motivates the whole side-hung head is geometrically sound and is a mount-structural result, not just an optical one: the dish axis is always perpendicular to the elevation axis, and the elevation axis is always perpendicular to the azimuth axis, so M3 and M4 sit at 45 deg at every elevation with no half-angle drive and no slaved ring - which is what lets the pivot be a single 1R flexure instead of a coupled mechanism.

## Wind analyses

### The user is right, and the reason is structural in the memos, not just in the machines: every wind number in this branch...

The user is right, and the reason is structural in the memos, not just in the machines: every wind number in this branch is a MEAN-ONLY number. `physics_hp.py:24` sets `Q9, Q25 = 0.5*RHO*81, 0.5*RHO*625` and every downstream memo multiplies that by Cd·A. There is no gust factor anywhere in the repository, the words "gust", "peak factor" and "3-second" do not appear in any load path, and it is never stated whether 9 and 25 m/s are means or gusts — an ambiguity worth a factor 2 on every load and every quoted safety factor in stage 3. Cd 1.3 and rho 1.03 are RIGHT (I recompute rho = 1.039 from the barometric formula at 1680 m, and 1.3 is defensible for a disc of sag/D = 0.065 at Re 2.5e6, between the flat-disc 1.17 and Peterka's heliostat 1.4). The drags 751 N and 5797 N reproduce exactly. The error is never the coefficient; it is the absent gust, the absent pitching moment, the absent foundation, the absent stow case, and the absent backlash of the two joints in every design that were bought rather than synthesized.

Recomputing with a peak factor of 2 on q and a CP offset of 0.15 D, the gating element in each machine is NOT the flexure anyone designed: (A) is gated by its own foundation — memo_hp.md section 6 claims 4 t of water on a 1.8 m root gives 36 kN m "which is the 25 m/s demand", but section 5's own demand at 25 m/s is 44.7 kN m and I recompute the restoring moment as 35.3 kN m: the root lifts at 22 m/s mean with no gust and at 15.6 m/s with one. (B) is gated by an azimuth pinion that is one unsized line of prose in synth.py:95 while carrying 2.94 kN m of crosswind torque, and by a ring rail whose rollers lift at 24 m/s with gust. (C) is gated by an optical acceptance nobody computed: I derive from optics.py that f_eff = 26.8 m gives a plate scale of 26.8 mm per mrad, 6.5x the machine it replaces, and the pivot bore r 0.30 first vignettes at 5.1 mrad — a fifth of the +-20 mrad the memos budget, while the elevation jack's nut at its short 0.39 m arm and the slew ring together spend 1.1-3.4 mrad of that 5.1 in backlash alone. (D), the user's fixed spherical bowl on one trunk, is the only design that is not wind-gated in pointing: the element that must be pointed is a 1.1 m2 receiver arm instead of a 13.9 m2 sail, it needs 11-14x less stiffness at its pointed joint, it has no stow case because it is always in its survival attitude, and it has no bearing, jack or slew ring anywhere in the optical path. Its cost is optical, not structural: 1.45x the mirror area from the cosine loss, computed over the repository's own 59 Quetta sun positions.

Two findings invalidate register statements. BLOCKING: memo_hp.md's "the water is the foundation, no anchors into the roof" fails against its own 44.7 kN m by 24 % before any gust. BLOCKING: memo_hp.md section 3a states "the stem alone holds 1.7 mrad at 9 m/s" and "above about 18 m/s the coarse error leaves the fine range" in the same paragraph; these are inconsistent by 2.9x in q, because a U^2 law makes 1.7 mrad at 9 m/s reach 20 mrad at 30.9 m/s. I recomputed the pointing with the repository's own `stem_element` over all 59 positions and 8 wind directions with the deck tendons deleted as section 3 requires, and get 5.04 mrad at 9 m/s mean for the r 0.80 stem — which puts +-20 mrad at exactly 17.9 m/s. The 18 m/s is right and the 1.7 mrad is wrong by 3x; the evidence file screw_checks.txt itself prints 0.38-1.00 mrad, a number that only exists WITH the four deck tendons that section 3 then deletes. MATERIAL: the fine stage is presented as the answer to wind ("a loop on the beam centroid at a few hertz") but its own metering, 1 mL = 62 urad with a 10 mL/s pump, gives 0.62 mrad/s — it saturates in RATE above 0.1 Hz at 1 mrad and is 30x short of the claimed few hertz. It is a drift corrector, not a gust rejecter, which means the coarse mean error is the part the tracker already removes and the gust is the part that matters and nothing in any of these machines removes it.

Finally, the user's geometric complaint is also correct and is the same complaint as the wind one. The third pass's own best insight — two trunnion blocks 6.5 m apart so the crosswind's moment becomes a pair of forces (fact_mount/memo.md:45-47) — is undone by the fourth pass, which concentrates the entire crosswind moment on one slew ring 1.4 m from a side-mounted head. A fork is two trunks. One trunk with branches, with a spherical section as the reflecting surface, is not a stylistic preference: the sphere is the only surface whose focus depends solely on the incoming direction and the centre of curvature (a parallel bundle in direction s focuses at C - (R/2)s for every s), so it is the only geometry in which the MIRROR can be fixed and the receiver moves. The membrane is already a sphere of R 8.2. Design D changes nothing optical except which body moves.

- air density at 1680 m: 1.039 kg/m3 recomputed; repo uses 1.03 (p/p0 = (1-2.25577e-5 z)^5.2559 = 0.8162, T = 277.2 K, rho = 1.225*0.8162*(288.15/277.2). physics_hp.py:24 sets RHO=1.03. Agrees to 0.9 %: NOT an error.)
- drag coefficient of the membrane: Cd 1.3 is defensible; sag/D = 0.065 (sag = 8.2 - sqrt(8.2^2 - 2.1^2) = 0.273 m over D 4.2 m. This is a shallow disc, not a cup: free-stream disc Cd 1.11-1.17 at Re = 9*4.2/1.5e-5 = 2.5e6; ASCE 7 solid circular sign Cf ~1.2; Peterka & Derickson SAND92-7009 mean C_Fx ~1.4 for heliostats near ground. physics_hp.py:24.)
- gust factor applied anywhere in the repository: none (physics_hp.py:24 Q9,Q25 = 0.5*RHO*81, 0.5*RHO*625; every memo multiplies q_mean*Cd*A. No peak factor, no 3-s gust, no statement of whether 9/25 m/s are means or gusts. I apply G_q = 2.0 (velocity gust factor 1.41, 3 s over 10 min, terrain III).)
- aperture drag, 9 m/s: 751 N mean, 1503 N peak (q(9)=41.7 Pa, A = pi*2.1^2 = 13.854 m2, Cd 1.3. Reproduces fact_mount/out/synth.txt:23 exactly (751 N).)
- aperture drag, 25 m/s: 5797 N mean, 11594 N peak (q(25)=321.9 Pa. Reproduces fact_mount/out/synth.txt:25 exactly.)
- aerodynamic pitching moment about a hinge through the dish centre: 473 N m mean / 947 N m peak at 9 m/s; 3652 / 7304 at 25 m/s (e = 0.15 D = 0.63 m, M = q Cd A e. Effective C_M = Cd*e/D = 0.195 mean, 0.39 peak, against Peterka's peak heliostat hinge-moment coefficient 0.25-0.35. The repo's only pitching term is the fine stage's 'drag asymmetry 315 N m at 9 m/s' = 0.10 D, mean only (fact_mount/out/synth.txt:37).)
- pointing sensitivity of the A/B optics: 4.1 mm per mrad at F (a parallel bundle on a sphere R focuses at C - (R/2)s; a rigid rotation of the dish by eps about F moves C by (R/2)eps = 4.10 m/rad. Sun's own image radius (R/2)*4.65 mrad = 19 mm; repo's defocus blur 51 mm (screw_checks.txt).)
- pointing sensitivity of the C optics (plate scale): 26.8 mm per mrad, 6.5x design A/B (coude/optics.py:11 f_eff = F1*m = 4.0*6.69 = 26.77 m. Reproduced: s1 0.65, s2 4.35, m 6.69, r_img 0.138 m, beam r at M3 0.201 (optics.txt 0.20), at M4 0.288 (optics.txt 0.29).)
- C's POINTING ACCEPTANCE, derived from the relay: 5.1 mrad first vignetting; 17.3 mrad total loss (pivot bore r 0.30 (coude/optics.py:49, synth.py:12 R_IN 0.33); beam radius at a pivot station 0.7 m along the neck = r_img + |0.8-0.7|*NA = 0.163 m, clearance 0.137 m; eps = 0.137/26.77. At the 0.5 m station it is 3.25 mrad. M4 (r 0.47, beam 0.29, clear 0.18) gives 6.7 mrad.)
- C's stated fine-stage range vs its actual acceptance: +-20 mrad range against 5.1 mrad acceptance: 3.9x over (fact_mount/memo.md:26 and memo_hp.md:109 both budget +-20 mrad. The fourth pass inherited that budget without recomputing the optics it replaced it with.)
- design A pointing at 9 m/s, memo's stem, tendons deleted: 5.04 mrad mean, 10.08 mrad peak (r 0.80 @ 1.0 bar); 2.58 / 5.16 (r 1.00 @ 0.8 bar) (my recomputation using the repo's own screw_mount.stem_element over all 59 positions x 8 wind directions, bridle contributing exactly zero to rotations about F (its own reciprocity check is 8.9e-16, screw_checks.txt:4), deck tendons deleted per memo_hp.md:87. Worst case summer 17.5 h, el 18, crosswind, stem L 4.88 m. Effective K_min 0.60 / 1.16 MN m/rad.)
- the memo_hp contradiction: '1.7 mrad at 9 m/s' and '18 m/s leaves the fine range' differ by 2.9x in q (memo_hp.md:92 and :118-119. U^2 scaling: 1.7 mrad at 9 gives 20 mrad at 9*sqrt(20/1.7) = 30.9 m/s. My 5.04 mrad puts +-20 mrad at 17.9 m/s, matching the memo's own 18. The 18 is right; the 1.7 is 3x optimistic. screw_checks.txt prints 0.38-1.00 mrad, which only exists WITH the four deck tendons section 3 deletes.)
- design A foundation, BLOCKING: 35.3 kN m restoring against 44.7 kN m demanded (memo_hp.md:176-177 claims 4 t of water on a 1.8 m root footprint = 36 kN m 'which is the 25 m/s demand'. Recomputed 4000*9.81*0.9 = 35.3 kN m. memo_hp.md:160 gives the 25 m/s base moment as 44.7 kN m. Deficit 21 % with no gust.)
- design A overturning wind speed: 22.1 m/s mean with no gust; 15.6 m/s mean with G_q = 2 (22 m/s gust) (fit to the memo's own table: M = 1.85 + 0.0686 U^2 kN m (from 7.4 kN m at 9 and 44.7 at 25); solve for 35.3. Ballast actually needed: 5.1 t mean, 10.1 t peak on a 1.8 m base.)
- design A stem wrinkling: 42.6 m/s mean; 30.1 m/s with gust (same fit against M_w = 0.5 pi p r^3 = 125.7 kN m for r 1.0 @ 0.8 bar. The stem is NOT the first failure; the roof is.)
- design A stow uplift: 7.1-13.4 kN at 25 m/s, 16-30x the membrane's own weight (memo_hp.md:169-170 'vent the rod... the dish lies face-up on the deck'. q_peak(25) = 644 Pa on 13.854 m2 with net uplift coefficient 0.8-1.5 (ASCE 7 rooftop equipment / low-slope roof edge and corner zones reach 1.5-1.8). Membrane 45 kg = 0.44 kN. Nothing in the design holds it.)
- the fine stage as a gust rejecter, MATERIAL: 0.62 mrad/s; saturates in rate above 0.1 Hz at 1 mrad (memo_hp.md:112 '1 mL moves a column 0.10 mm, 62 urad of tilt; a 10 mL/s pump slews 0.62 mrad/s'. To follow amplitude a at frequency f needs 2 pi f a: at 0.5 Hz and 1 mrad, 3.14 mrad/s = 5.1x the pump. memo_hp.md:118 claims 'a loop... at a few hertz': at 3 Hz and 1 mrad that is 18.8 mrad/s, 30x the pump.)
- design B blade trunnion spring: 167 N m/rad; carries 18 rad under the 3.0 kN m wind torque (fact_mount/out/synth.txt:21. The flexure pivot supplies essentially no restraint; the elevation wind load is entirely on the screw jacks. Same in C: 208 N m/rad, 7.6 rad (coude/out/synth.txt:5).)
- design B jack stiffness claim, checked: 48-73 MN m/rad from the bare screw's EA/L; repo claims ~50 MN m/rad (fact_mount/synth.py:125. Tr40 core d 34 mm, A 908 mm2, L 2.5 m: EA/L = 73 MN/m, times the 0.81-1.00 m lever squared. The claim is the screw alone: no nut/thread Hertzian compliance (typically 3-10x the screw's own), no bracket, no crank bending, no gearbox. Still stiff enough — stiffness is not B's problem.)
- backlash, the unbudgeted term in B and C: jack nut 0.22-0.77 mrad; azimuth slew/pinion 0.87-2.62 mrad (Tr40 self-locking nut axial lash 0.1-0.3 mm at B's 0.81-0.90 m crank and at C's 0.39 m crank (coude/out/synth.txt:8 'arm at least 0.39 m'); slew/pinion drive backlash 0.05-0.15 deg. These are 5-50x the 30 urad quoted at fact_mount/synth.py:125, do not scale down with wind speed, and are traversed on every gust reversal. They appear nowhere in the repository.)
- design B azimuth torque on the unsized pinion: 2.94 kN m mean / 5.88 peak at 9 m/s (el 12); 22.7 / 45.4 at 25 m/s (crosswind lever 4.0*cos(el); D*lever. The azimuth drive is one line of prose, fact_mount/synth.py:95 'a pinion on the ring beam', with no size and no stiffness.)
- design B ring-rail uplift, BLOCKING: rollers lift at 34 m/s mean, 24 m/s with gust (machine ~1464 kg = 14.4 kN on a ring of radius 3.61 m (geometry.py:10,13,22) gives 52 kN m restoring; overturning = q Cd A * (10.0 - 5.18) = 28 kN m mean / 56 kN m peak at 25 m/s. The rail must be captive and the deck must take ~10 t of local uplift. Never checked.)
- design B blades, the element that IS sized: buckling at 66 m/s mean, 47 m/s with gust (fit to fact_mount/out/synth.txt:24,26: P = 752 N dead + 1.90 N per (m/s)^2 against 9139 N. The blades are 2-3x past survival and are NOT gating. The sizing is correct and generous.)
- design B first structural stop: jack capacity 50 kN reached at 47 m/s mean, 33 m/s with gust (fact_mount/out/synth.txt:43, 14.3 kN per jack at 25 m/s mean; x2 for gust = 28.6 kN, SF 1.75 against a 50 kN Tr40.)
- design B inflatable posts (setup_sim, r 0.6 fabric): wrinkle at 16.4 m/s mean / 11.6 with gust at 40 kPa; 23.7 / 16.7 at 80 kPa (M_w = 0.5 pi p r^3 (setup_sim/inflated_beam.py:15) reduced for the 3.2 kN axial share of the 45 kN crushing force; base moment = (0.5 D + D*4.0/6.5)*4.52 m = 3.79 kN m at 9 m/s mean. setup_sim/memo.md gives the posts as r 0.6 at 40 kPa in the run, 80 kPa in the design.)
- design C stalk geometry: L = 1.94 m, L/r = 3.9, dish centre 3.48 m above the base at el 59 (coude/optics.py:53-54, neck at env z 6.94, deck z 5.0; head 1.8 m in front along the dish axis. L/r 3.9 means this is a stub, not a beam: shear rigidity kGA + P = 275 + 31 kN is comparable to the bending term and is ignored in the repo.)
- design C stalk base moment: 2.62 kN m mean / 5.23 peak at 9 m/s; 20.2 / 40.4 at 25 m/s (q Cd A * 3.48 m.)
- design C inflatable stalk (setup_sim4, r 0.5), pointing: 18.7 mrad at 9 m/s MEAN, 37.3 peak (EI = Et pi r^3 = 196.3 kN m2 (Et = 500 kN/m, inflated_beam.py:11); theta = M0 L/EI + F L^2/2EI = 11.5 + 7.2 mrad with M0 = 751*1.54 N m and F = 751 N. Against C's 5.1 mrad acceptance this is exceeded at 4.7 m/s mean, 3.3 m/s with gust — below the operating wind.)
- design C inflatable stalk, wrinkling and dynamics: wrinkles at 14.0 m/s mean / 9.9 with gust at 40 kPa; first mode 1.46 Hz (M_w,eff = 0.5 pi p r^3 (1 - N/(p pi r^2)) = 6.30 kN m with 6.2 kN of dead load (head 155 + cw 151 + cw 267 + 60 kg). k_lat = 53.4 kN/m including shear, mass 633 kg. 1.46 Hz is INSIDE the gust band (0.05-2 Hz), so the quasi-static treatment is invalid and resonance adds 30-80 %.)
- design C rigid stalk (1.0 m x 6 mm steel), pointing: 7.9 urad at 9 m/s mean; yield SF 54 (I = pi(0.5^4-0.494^4)/4 = 2.314e-3 m4, EI 463 MN m2. The rigid stalk is the right choice and is not gating — but it abandons the brief's hose-as-frame in the load path.)
- design C elevation jack force: 4.0 kN mean / 8.1 peak at 9 m/s; 31 / 62 kN at 25 m/s (coude/out/synth.txt:8 prints 4.0 / 31.0 kN at the 0.39 m minimum arm. With G_q = 2 the survival demand is 62 kN, over a single-stage Tr40's 50 kN. The short arm is the fourth pass's own choice (coude/synth.py:39-44).)
- design C crosswind concentrated on one bearing: 1.05 kN m mean at 9 m/s, 16.2 kN m peak at 25 m/s about the stalk (D * OFF = D * 1.4 m (coude/optics.py:17). The third pass had split exactly this load into a force pair across 6.5 m of trunnion base (fact_mount/memo.md:45-47, out/synth.txt:23-25). The fourth pass undoes its own best wind move.)
- stow value of el 83: projected area falls to 1.69 m2, 12 % — an 8.2x reduction (A cos(83 deg). Applies to B and C. Neither memo defines a stow case, a stow attitude, a stow speed threshold or a time-to-stow; the jack stroke 0.71-1.16 m at a typical 5 mm/s is about 4 minutes.)
- design D, why a sphere: a parallel bundle in direction s focuses at C - (R/2)s for every s (the focus depends only on the centre of curvature and the sun direction, not on which patch of the sphere the mirror occupies. This is why the mirror can be fixed and the receiver moves; it is the user's 'only geometry that is fixed focus on the target'. The membrane is already a sphere R 8.2 (physics_hp.py:20).)
- design D spherical aberration at this f/D: circle of least confusion ~20 mm at rim half-angle 14.84 deg (longitudinal aberration (R/2)(1/cos th - 1) = 141 mm, transverse at the paraxial plane (R/2)(1/cos th - 1) tan 2th = 81 mm, CoLC ~ TA/4. Against the sun's own 19 mm image, a shallow spherical bowl is nearly aberration-free. Hashemi's 51 mm defocus blur is the same order.)
- design D cosine cost: 1.45x the mirror area: 20.0 m2 instead of 13.9 m2 (aperture radius 2.53 m) (scan of the fixed bowl axis over the repository's own 59 Quetta positions (physics_hp.positions(), el >= 12 deg, three seasons, half-hourly): best axis el 64 deg, az 180 deg, mean cos(sun, axis) = 0.691. This is D's real price, and it is optical, not structural.)
- design D facet pointing under wind: 0.23 mrad at 25 m/s peak; per-facet tolerance 0.6 mrad (a facet b m square: peak moment q_peak Cd b^2 (0.1 b) = 10.5 N m at b 0.5, 83.7 at b 1.0; a scaled bracket tube (d = b/10, L = b/2) gives K = EI/L = 46 / 364 kN m/rad. Scale-free in ratio (M ~ b^3, K ~ b^3) but absolutely small because b is small. Tolerance: facet normal error d moves its contribution 2d(R/2) = 8.2 mm/mrad against a 19 mm sun image, so 0.6 mrad for a 25 % spot growth — 5x LOOSER than the membrane's own 0.5 mrad slope spec.)
- design D receiver arm under wind: 2.02 mrad at 25 m/s PEAK with a bare 200x6 cantilever (head + arm 1.1 m2, Cd 1.2, q_peak(25) = 644 Pa -> 850 N at a 2.0 m mean lever = 1.70 kN m; a 200x6 tube 4.1 m long gives K = EI/L = 840 kN m/rad. It can also be triangulated or railed against the FIXED bowl, which no moving-dish design can do. +-20 mrad would need 79 m/s peak.)
- design D receiver tolerances: 4.9 mrad of arm angle for position; 25 mrad for a relay's aim (position tolerance ~20 mm (the circle of least confusion) at a 4.10 m arm; a relay mirror landing the re-imaged spot in a 0.30 m bore 6 m below tolerates 0.30/(2*6) = 25 mrad. Both LOOSER than C's 5.1 mrad.)
- THE COMPARISON: stiffness needed at the pointed joint (peak, 25 m/s): A 3.80, B 3.80, C 4.77, D 0.35 MN m/rad (M_peak / pointing tolerance. A/B: 46.4 kN m (drag x 4.0 m to F) / 12.2 mrad. C: 24.3 kN m (drag x 2.1 m to the neck) / 5.1 mrad. D: 1.70 kN m (arm+head 1.1 m2 x 2.0 m) / 4.9 mrad. D needs 11-14x less stiffness at its pointed joint, on a member with 13x less wind area.)
- functional pointing criterion for A/B: half power at 0.7 deg = 12.2 mrad (the other fork's env (memory: 'pointing was inert'); the hard geometric aperture is far looser, 0.44 m strip half-width / 4.10 m = 107 mrad.)
- Quetta design wind, unresolved: could not retrieve BCP SP-2007 for Quetta; the neighbouring IS 875-3 band is 33-55 m/s 3-s gust (50 yr, 10 m, terrain II) (WebSearch/WebFetch on BCP SP:07 returned only the paid Dlubal geo-zone tool. IS 875-3's lowest zone, 33 m/s 3-s gust, is q = 561 Pa, 1.74x the repo's 25 m/s survival q of 322 Pa. Whether 25 m/s is a mean or a gust changes every stated safety factor by 2. This must be resolved before any of the SFs in stage 3 mean anything.)

- **A - Hashemi's tube machine: dish on the focal circle, bridle of four wires through F, inflated water stem from a root on the deck, focal tube through the slot (stage3/hashemi_pneumatic/memo_hp.md)** — gating: THE FOUNDATION, not the stem and not the flexures. The 4 t water root on a 1.8 m footprint. Second: the stem's soft bending axis, which is the ONLY rotational stiffness left after memo_hp.md:87 deletes the deck tendons, because the bridle is exactly reciprocal to every rotation about F by construction (its own check: 8.9e-16, screw_checks.txt:4). The stem's soft axis is EI/L with the tip free to translate, and the stem grows to 5.87 m over the year.; operating limit: POINTING: half power (12.2 mrad) at 14.0 m/s mean, 9.9 m/s mean with G_q = 2, for the memo's r 0.80 @ 1.0 bar stem; 19.6 / 13.8 m/s for r 1.00 @ 0.8 bar. +-20 mrad at 17.9 / 12.7 m/s (r 0.80) and 25.1 / 17.7 (r 1.00). STRUCTURE: the root lifts at 22.1 m/s mean and 15.6 m/s with gust — this is the binding limit and it is below the design's own 9 m/s + gust margin claim. The machine is wind-gated at essentially its stated operating wind.; survival: Stem wrinkling at 42.6 m/s mean / 30.1 with gust — NOT the limit. The limit is overturning at 15.6 m/s with gust. Ballast actually needed: 5.1 t mean, 10.1 t peak on a 1.8 m base. STOW is the worst case in the whole branch: 'vent the rod, the dish lies face-up on the deck' gives 7.1-13.4 kN of uplift on a 45 kg membrane at 25 m/s, 16-30x its own weight, with no tie-down specified, plus a vented fabric stem flogging on a roof.; pointing budget: 5.04 mrad mean / 10.08 mrad peak at 9 m/s (my recomputation over 59 positions x 8 wind directions with the repo's own stem_element, tendons deleted; worst summer 17.5 h, el 18, crosswind, L 4.88 m). K_eff 0.60 MN m/rad. Wind wrench about F = drag x 4.0 m = 3.01 kN m mean / 6.01 peak at 9 m/s, 23.2 / 46.4 at 25. The repository's 1.7 mrad is 3x optimistic; its 0.38-1.00 mrad only exists with the four deck tendons the design deletes. And the fine stage cannot recover it: 0.62 mrad/s of pump rate rejects nothing above 0.1 Hz.; verdict: BLOCKING. 'The water is the foundation, no anchors into the roof' (memo_hp.md:176-177) fails against the memo's own 44.7 kN m demand by 21 % with no gust and by 2.5x with one. BLOCKING. memo_hp.md:92 and :118-119 state two mutually inconsistent wind numbers; the 18 m/s is right and the 1.7 mrad is wrong by 3x. MATERIAL. The fine stage is presented as the wind answer and is rate-limited to 0.62 mrad/s, 30x short of the claimed 'few hertz'. RIGHT: rho, Cd, the drag values, the FACT reciprocity of the bridle, and the finding that unilateral lines from below cannot span the torque space.
- **B - the third-pass FACT fork: ring beam on a deck rail, two two-stage cross-blade trunnion blocks at |y| 3.25 m on the axis through F, cradle, water counterweights, screw jacks, diaphragm fine stage (stage3/fact_mount/)** — gating: THE AZIMUTH DRIVE and THE RAIL. The elevation blade pivot is 167 N m/rad and carries nothing (18 rad under the 3.0 kN m wind torque): the load is on the jacks, which are stiff enough. What is not designed is the azimuth pinion — one line of prose at fact_mount/synth.py:95 — carrying 2.94 kN m of crosswind torque at el 12, and the ring rail, whose rollers must take uplift.; operating limit: POINTING: not wind-limited elastically. The jacks give 30 urad mean / 60 urad peak at 9 m/s (the repo's 30 urad checks out) and the fine stage 4 urad. B never approaches +-20 mrad by elasticity at any survivable wind. The real pointing floor is BACKLASH, which does not scale with wind: 0.22-0.37 mrad in the jack nuts plus 0.87-2.62 mrad in the azimuth pinion, traversed on every gust reversal, 1.1-3.0 mrad total. That is 9-25 % of the 12.2 mrad half-power budget, spent by two components nobody sized. STRUCTURE: the rail lifts at 34 m/s mean, 24 m/s with gust.; survival: jack capacity 50 kN reached at 47 m/s mean / 33 with gust; blades buckle at 66 / 47 m/s — 2-3x past survival, correctly and generously sized. The binding structural limit is the ring rail's uplift at 24 m/s with gust: it must be a captive rail and the deck must take ~10 t of local uplift, checked nowhere. As simulated with inflatable posts (r 0.6 fabric), they wrinkle at 16.4 m/s mean / 11.6 with gust at 40 kPa and 23.7 / 16.7 at 80 kPa. NO STOW CASE IS DEFINED AT ALL; el 83 would be worth 8.2x in projected area.; pointing budget: elastic 0.03-0.06 mrad; posts add 0.3 mrad of axis translation at 9 m/s (their own number, out/synth.txt:29); backlash 1.1-3.0 mrad. Total ~1.2-3.1 mrad at 9 m/s, of which 95 % is lash. B is the only design of the four whose wind pointing error is dominated by a term that is independent of wind speed — which is why B is the most robust of the three moving machines and also the one whose accuracy claim (microradians) is furthest from what it will measure.; verdict: The best of the three moving machines and the one whose structural work is right. RIGHT, and the branch's best single idea: putting the two trunnion blocks 6.5 m apart so the crosswind's moment becomes a pair of forces (memo.md:45-47) is exactly the correct wind move, and the memo is honest about the first draft's failure. MATERIAL: the azimuth drive and the rail's uplift are the elements wind sizes first and neither is sized. MATERIAL: '~50 MN m/rad per jack' is the bare screw's EA/L with no nut, bracket, crank or gearbox compliance, and the whole accuracy claim ignores backlash, which is 5-50x the quoted elastic error. MINOR: no stow case.
- **C - the fourth-pass stalk machine: Cassegrain head on a conical style, hollow cartwheel neck flexure, single stalk beside the dish, screw jack, water counterweights, slew ring (stage3/coude/)** — gating: THE OPTICS' OWN ACCEPTANCE, 5.1 mrad, against the backlash of the elevation jack at its short 0.39 m arm and the load-bearing azimuth slew ring. In the simulated (inflatable r 0.5) version the gating element is the stalk itself, which fails below the operating wind.; operating limit: RIGID VERSION: lash-limited from the first reversing gust. 1.1-3.4 mrad of backlash (jack nut 0.26-0.77 mrad at 0.39 m, slew ring 0.87-2.62 mrad) is 22-66 % of a 5.1 mrad budget before any elastic term. Adding the elastic terms (jack 100 urad, stalk 8 urad, pivot spring carries nothing) the budget is exhausted somewhere in the low teens of m/s depending on which slew grade is bought — the design's fate turns entirely on a catalogue number that is not in the repository. INFLATABLE VERSION (setup_sim4, the published sheet 45): out of tolerance at 4.7 m/s mean, 3.3 m/s with gust; wrinkles at 14.0 m/s mean / 9.9 with gust at 40 kPa; first mode 1.46 Hz, inside the gust band, so quasi-static analysis does not apply.; survival: stalk yield SF 54 (not gating); jack demand 62 kN peak at 25 m/s against a 50 kN single-stage Tr40 — over capacity, so ~22 m/s mean with gust. Foundation: 40.4 kN m peak at 25 m/s into a 1.0 m stalk on a domestic deck, needing ~4.1 t of ballast on a 2 m base, not drawn. Stow at el 83 is worth 8.2x but the head is 1.4 m off the azimuth axis at all times, so the slew brake carries the stow moment too.; pointing budget: 5.1 mrad, DERIVED, not inherited: f_eff = 26.8 m gives a plate scale of 26.8 mm/mrad; the beam at a pivot station 0.7 m along the neck is r 0.163 m in an r 0.30 bore, clearance 0.137 m, so first vignetting at 0.137/26.77 = 5.1 mrad (3.25 mrad if the pivot sits at 0.5 m), total loss at 17.3 mrad, M4 at 6.7 mrad. The memos budget +-20 mrad, 3.9x too loose. The fourth pass made the machine 6.5x more pointing-sensitive than the machine it replaced (26.8 vs 4.1 mm/mrad) while making the mount softer and concentrating the crosswind on one bearing — three moves in the wrong direction to buy 3 % of shading.; verdict: MATERIAL, and the register's headline number for this pass is compromised: the 3 % shading gain (optics.txt:16) is bought by a 6.5x increase in pointing sensitivity and a 3.9x reduction in acceptance that no memo states. BLOCKING for the simulated version: the inflatable stalk of setup_sim4 fails its pointing function at 3.3 m/s and wrinkles at 9.9 m/s with gust — the vine-robot frame is a deployment mechanism, not a load path, and sheet 45 should say so. MATERIAL: the fourth pass undoes the third pass's own best wind insight by putting the whole crosswind moment on one slew ring at 1.4 m instead of a force pair across 6.5 m; coude/memo.md:71-72 lists 'wind on the side-mounted head about the stalk' as OPEN, which is honest but means the pass was published with its gating load unanalysed. RIGHT: the counterweight balance holds at every elevation (both arms scale as cos el), the hollow-pivot FACT argument is sound, and the two-fixed-fold geometry is genuinely elegant.
- **D - the user's tree: one trunk, branches forming the scatter pattern, a spherical section cut out of the foliage volume; the bowl FIXED, a receiver or relay moving on the focal sphere R/2** — gating: the receiver arm (1.1 m2) and the facet brackets. Nothing else in the machine has a pointing requirement at all.; operating limit: NONE set by wind on the mirror. The bowl never moves, so it has one load case and is always in it. The arm: 2.02 mrad at 25 m/s PEAK with a bare 200x6 cantilever against a 4.9 mrad position tolerance, and it can be triangulated or railed against the fixed bowl — a datum no moving-dish design has. +-20 mrad would need 79 m/s peak. Facets: 0.23 mrad at 25 m/s peak against a 0.6 mrad per-facet tolerance (5x looser than the membrane's own 0.5 mrad slope spec, because a facet error d moves only its own contribution, 8.2 mm/mrad against a 19 mm sun image).; survival: one load case, permanently stowed. Bowl drag 16.8 kN peak at 25 m/s on 20.0 m2 into a fixed, triangulated, ballastable foundation — an ordinary problem. A bowl whose rim sits at or below the parapet sees 0.3-0.6 of free-stream q, a reduction available to no tracking dish. The arm folds into the bowl, inside the bowl's own wind shadow: no stow actuator, no time-to-stow, no wrong-attitude failure mode, no vented membrane loose on a roof.; pointing budget: 4.9 mrad for the arm's position on the focal sphere (20 mm circle of least confusion at a 4.10 m radius); 25 mrad if the moving element is a relay mirror aiming into a 0.30 m bore 6 m below; 0.6 mrad per facet. Stiffness needed at the pointed joint, peak at 25 m/s: 0.35 MN m/rad, against 3.80 for A and B and 4.77 for C — 11-14x less, on a member with 13x less wind area. That single ratio is the whole argument.; verdict: The user is right on the geometry and right that it is the wind answer. The sphere is the ONLY surface whose focus depends solely on the incoming direction and the centre of curvature (a parallel bundle in direction s focuses at C - (R/2)s for every s), so it is the only geometry in which the mirror can be fixed and the receiver moves. Hashemi's membrane is ALREADY a sphere of R 8.2 (physics_hp.py:20) — D changes nothing optical except which body moves, and at this f/D the sphere's circle of least confusion (~20 mm) is the same order as the sun's own image (19 mm) and smaller than the machine's existing 51 mm defocus blur. It is also the only one of the four that is actually fractal in the brief's sense (one trunk, self-similar branches, the same joint at every scale) and the only one with one trunk rather than a fork or a fork-with-a-branch. HONEST COSTS, computed: 1.45x the mirror area from the cosine loss (mean cos = 0.691 over the repository's own 59 Quetta positions, best fixed axis el 64 deg az 180 deg), i.e. 20.0 m2 and a 2.53 m aperture radius instead of 2.10; and the heat must now leave a MOVING receiver to reach a FIXED underground oven, so either a relay mirror on the focal sphere (0.4-0.7 m radius, 25 mrad aiming tolerance, easy) or a flexible thermal link. D converts a wind problem into an aperture problem and a plumbing problem, both of which are cheaper than the wind problem.

- principle: Wind gates all four machines because the aperture is the sail. The one number that decides every design is the wind moment at the joint that must be pointed, divided by the pointing tolerance: 3.80 MN m/rad for A and B, 4.77 for C, 0.35 for D at the 25 m/s peak. Nothing else in the analysis matters as much as which body the 13.9 m2 is attached to.
- principle: A mean-only load is half a design. The repository's Cd, rho and drag values are all correct, and every safety factor computed from them is optimistic by the peak factor, ~2 on q. Worse, the repository never states whether 9 and 25 m/s are means or gusts, so the same numbers can be read as either — which is a factor 2 of undeclared latitude in every memo in stage 3. Fix the definition first; everything else follows.
- principle: The mean is the part the tracker removes; the gust is the part that matters. Quoting a mean pointing error and then crediting a fine stage with removing it is doubly wrong: the coarse tracker already removes the mean, and the fine stage cannot follow the gust (0.62 mrad/s of pump rate saturates above 0.1 Hz at 1 mrad, against a claimed 'few hertz'). The honest budget is the peak minus the mean, with no loop credit above 0.1 Hz.
- principle: The gating element is never the part you synthesized. In all three moving machines the flexures are stiff, correct and generously sized, and the machine is limited by the four things nobody drew: the foundation, the stow case, the bought bearing's backlash and the rail's uplift. A blade pivot at 167-208 N m/rad carries none of the wind; a screw jack's nut lash of 0.2 mm carries all of the error.
- principle: Backlash does not scale with wind, so it sets the floor. 0.2 mm in a jack nut at a 0.39 m arm is 0.51 mrad; 0.1 deg in a slew drive is 1.75 mrad. These are 5-50x the elastic errors the memos compute, they are traversed on every gust reversal, and they are the reason a microradian flexure claim is meaningless when the same chain contains one bought rotary joint. Either preload the bearing out of the load path or put the sensor beyond it.
- principle: Shorten the moment arm before stiffening the joint. The third pass's correct move was geometric, not material: two trunnion blocks 6.5 m apart turn the crosswind's moment into a pair of forces. The fourth pass reversed it and put the whole moment on one slew ring 1.4 m away. Wind rewards separation and punishes cantilevers, at every scale.
- principle: Pointing sensitivity is an optical choice with a structural bill. Going from the R/2 spherical receiver (4.1 mm/mrad) to an f_eff 26.8 m Cassegrain (26.8 mm/mrad) multiplied the required mount stiffness by 6.5 and cut the acceptance from 12.2 mrad to 5.1, to recover 3 % of shading. Compute the acceptance from the relay's own apertures before adopting a relay; never inherit a +-20 mrad budget across an optics change.
- principle: Inflated fabric is a deployment mechanism, not a load path, at this scale. Et = 500 kN/m at r 0.4-0.6 m and 40-80 kPa gives EI of 0.2-0.8 MN m2 and wrinkling moments of 6-27 kN m; every inflated member in this branch fails between 10 and 24 m/s with a gust factor of 2, and the r 0.5 stalk's first mode at 1.46 Hz sits inside the gust band where quasi-static analysis is invalid. Grow the machine with the hose, then lock it against something rigid.
- principle: A machine with one load case beats a machine with three. Operating, survival and stow are three different geometries for A, B and C, with a transition that must complete before the gust front arrives; the fixed bowl has one, is permanently in it, and can be set below a parapet where it sees 0.3-0.6 of free-stream q. The absence of a stow case is not a small omission — for design A the stow case is the most dangerous state the machine ever occupies.
- principle: Subdivision moves the wind problem to where it is small. A facet's moment goes as b^3 and a scaled bracket's stiffness goes as b^3, so the deflection is scale-free in ratio but absolutely small because b is small and the load path is 0.3-1 m of branch instead of 4 m to a focus and 5 m down a stalk. This is why a trunk with branches is a wind argument and not only an aesthetic one.
- principle: The sphere is the geometry that lets the mirror stand still. A parallel bundle in direction s focuses at C - (R/2)s for every s, so the focus depends on nothing the mirror does; the price is a moving receiver and a cosine loss. Fixing the mirror trades 1.45x the aperture for the entire wind problem, and at this f/D the sphere's aberration (20 mm circle of least confusion) is already smaller than the machine's existing defocus blur (51 mm). This is the trade the branch has never made explicit and should.

### The designer is right on both counts, and the two complaints turn out to be the same complaint.

**Wind is not gating an...

The designer is right on both counts, and the two complaints turn out to be the same complaint.

**Wind is not gating anything in this repository because the repository never let it.** Three independent facts, each verifiable in the files: (1) the simulator's own wind path multiplies wind by a 0.4 "courtyard wall shelter" for a machine that an earlier audit already established is *wholly on the roof*, so its modelled wind never exceeds 5.6 m/s and it never stows (`brief_findings.md:156,183`) — the environment that trains the policy and prices the design is physically incapable of producing a load case; (2) every mount pass sizes on a 25 m/s "survival" wind that is a choice, not a code value, while the project's own brief already records a ~47 m/s 50-year gust (`brief.md:52`) — a factor (47/25)² = 3.53 in load that nobody has ever applied; (3) `RHO=1.03; CD=1.3` at `hashemi_pneumatic/physics_hp.py:24` is the only aerodynamics in the whole stage — no lift, no hinge-moment coefficient, no gust factor, no averaging time, no rooftop factor, no natural frequency.

Applying the missing factors kills the published fourth pass. Its hollow cartwheel elevation pivot reports SF 2.0 at 25 m/s (`coude/out/synth.txt:7`). Recomputing the same expression at 47 m/s with a measured peak drag coefficient gives **SF 0.57**, and with the ASCE 7-16 §29.4.1 rooftop-equipment factor **SF 0.49**: the blades buckle. The screw jack goes from 31 kN to 130 kN. That is blocking, not material.

**The "one trunk" complaint and the wind verdict agree.** The fourth pass hangs a 155 kg head on a neck 1.4 m to one side of a 1.0 m stalk (`coude/memo.md`, `coude/synth.py:31-34`) — the "branch extending backwards on which the dish is" the designer rejects — and that 1.4 m offset *is* the crosswind moment that overloads the pivot (M = 155·9.81·1.4 + D·1.4). Fixing the aesthetic fixes the mechanics.

**Wind-gated architecture, and it is the geometry the designer described.** The governing quantity is not force but force ÷ *structural depth*. The same 47 m/s event delivers 222 kN of stress-resultant into a 0.27 m stalk, 10.7 kN into an 8.5 m deck ring, and 3.3 kN into a recessed bowl. The machine already contains a sphere — `R_SPH = 8.2 m` — and a fixed sphere is the only figure whose focal locus is a *fixed sphere about a fixed centre*, which is exactly the designer's "spherical section cut out of it ... the only geometry that is fixed focus on the target": every facet face terminates on one sphere about C, the branches fill the volume behind that cap, and a two-axis coudé through C keeps the underground target fixed at every sun position. I ray-traced it exactly: at R 8.2 / aperture radius 2.1 (f/D 0.98) the fixed sphere's aberration is 12.9 mm RMS, 26.6 mm max, against the machine's own 138 mm image radius. **The fixed bowl is optically free at this aperture.** It costs mirror area: 4.03× the aperture (56.8 m²) if the crown turns in azimuth only, 18.6× (262 m²) if it never moves at all. It buys an 18× reduction in moving frontal area (0.76 m² of arm and secondary against 13.85 m² of dish) and removes pointing loss entirely — a fixed reflector cannot be mispointed by a gust.

**Porosity is oversold and I have to say so.** Solving Steiros & Hultmark (2018) eqs (2.12b)/(2.15) numerically: C_D falls from 1.333 at β = 0 to 1.224 at β = 0.30 — only 8 % — but a 30 % porous crown needs gross area A/(1−β) to keep 13.85 m² of mirror, so at **constant mirror area a 30 % porous crown carries 1.31× MORE total drag than the solid dish**. Porosity does not buy force. What it buys is real but different: above β ≈ 0.23 the Kármán street is suppressed (Castro 1971), removing lock-in at 0.32–1.68 Hz — precisely the band of the fourth pass's own 0.068 Hz elevation mode; and a crown of separate facets can *feather*, which the solid membrane cannot. Feathering is the only 5× lever in the whole problem: measured peak C_D falls 1.50 → 0.24 from face-on to 15°, and a reconfiguring canopy (Vogel exponent −1) turns the 27× rise from 9 to 47 m/s into 5.2×.

**Inflatables.** M_wrinkle = πpr³/2 is the whole story. An unguyed r = 0.25 m trunk needs 24.7 bar and 617 N/mm of hoop for the tracking dish at 47 m/s — impossible in any fabric. Guyed at a 5 m lever, the same trunk needs 0.62 bar and 15.4 N/mm: **a 40× reduction in required pressure**, because guys convert a moment (capacity ∝ pr³) into an axial force (capacity ∝ pr²). An inflated trunk without guys is not a structure.

- Design air density at Quetta (1680 m), coldest case: 1.055 kg/m3 (ISA 1680 m: T 266.3 K→p 82.4 kPa; rho = p/(R·273.15)) (ISA to 1680 m then rho = p/(Rd·T) at 0 C. The repo uses 1.03 (physics_hp.py:24), 2.4 % light. Storms in Quetta are winter storms: use the densest air.)
- Aperture area: A = pi·2.1^2 = 13.854 m2, D = 4.2 m (physics_hp.py:24 AREA = np.pi*A_DISH**2; agrees.)
- Repo survival load case vs the repo's own recorded design gust: 25 m/s assumed vs 47 m/s recorded; load ratio (47/25)^2 = 3.53 (physics_hp.py:143 'survival: 25 m/s'; brief.md:52 'design gust 47 m/s at lat 28.6 for a 50-yr return (not in code)'. Nothing in stage3 applies the 3.53.)
- Drag coefficient assumed vs measured peak vs code rooftop factor: repo Cd 1.3; Emes 2017 peak Cd 1.50 at alpha 90 (Iu 8 % ABL); ASCE 7-16 29.4.1 GCr 1.77 (physics_hp.py:24 CD=1.3. Emes et al. fig.7-8: mean 1.2, peak 1.50, and peak/(Iu=2%) = 1.28. GCr computed: Af 13.85 m2 on a 12x5 m building, 0.1Bh = 6.0, Bh = 60, GCr = 1.9 - 0.9(13.85-6)/(60-6) = 1.77. Repo is 1.15x low against measurement, 1.36x low against code.)
- Drag on the solid 4.2 m dish, face-on, at each speed (Cd 1.5 / GCr 1.77): 9 m/s 0.89 / 1.05 kN; 25 m/s 6.85 / 8.08 kN; 40 m/s 17.54 / 20.70 kN; 47 m/s 24.21 / 28.57 kN (F = 0.5·1.055·V^2·C·13.854. Repo's own figure at 25 m/s is 5.797 kN (out/synth.txt:7).)
- BLOCKING: fourth-pass hollow cartwheel elevation pivot safety factor: repo 25 m/s: SF 2.0. Recomputed 40 m/s peak Cd: SF 0.77. 47 m/s peak Cd: SF 0.57. 47 m/s ASCE rooftop: SF 0.49 (Same expression as coude/synth.py:33-34, M_v = 155·9.81·1.4 + D·1.4, per-blade = M_v/(2·0.45)/2, against the file's own Pcr 11.4 kN. Reproduces 5.69 kN/blade at the repo's 25 m/s input, so only the load case changes.)
- BLOCKING: the elevation screw jack at the code gust: repo 32 kN at 25 m/s -> 130 kN at 47 m/s (arm 0.39 m) (coude/synth.py:36,44: tau = D·(NECK 1.8 + 0.3), jack = tau/0.39. A 130 kN self-locking screw under a reversing gust load is a different machine.)
- MATERIAL: the elevation-torque lever is wrong by 4.7x: repo uses e = 2.10 m (drag x axial distance to the neck). Measured hinge eccentricity cMy/Cd·D = (0.16/1.50)·4.2 = 0.45 m (coude/synth.py:36. An axial drag has zero moment about a point on its own line of action; the 1.8 m is along the force, not across it. Emes cMHy(peak) <= 0.16 gives tau = 0.40 / 3.07 / 10.85 kNm at 9 / 25 / 47 m/s. The repo is conservative here by 4.7x and un-conservative on speed by 3.53x: net 1.33x un-conservative.)
- MATERIAL: lift is absent from every stage-3 calculation: peak lift coefficient cL up to 0.85 (stow, H/c 1.25); at 47 m/s that is 13.7 kN normal to the wind on 13.85 m2 (Emes 2017 fig.4-5. brief.md:52 did carry CL 1.0; coude/synth.py and fact_mount/synth.py carry no lift term at all. Lift, not drag, sets the hinge moment near stow.)
- BLOCKING: the simulator cannot generate a sizing wind: Hashemi env applies wall_shelter = 0.4 and stows at 16/14 m/s; modelled max over 4000 days = 5.6 m/s, ZERO stows. Unsheltered base model: 20-yr max 14.2-14.7 m/s (brief_findings.md:156,183, citing tandoor_hashemi_env.py / tandoor_polar_env.py:119-120. Even the unsheltered model (base 2.5-6.0 m/s + OU sigma 1.8, clipped at 25) has no extreme-value tail: its 20-year maximum is a third of the design gust. No policy trained on it has ever met a gust.)
- BLOCKING: '9 m/s' and '25 m/s' have no stated averaging time, worth a factor 2.3: 3-s gust / hourly mean = 1 + 3.5·Iu: 1.28 (Iu 8 %), 1.52 (Iu 15 %, suburban roof), 1.70 (Iu 20 %). A 9 m/s MEAN is a 13.7 m/s gust; a 25 m/s MEAN is a 38.1 m/s gust (Standard peak-factor form with g = 3.5. Loads scale with the square: 2.33x at Iu 15 %. Until the averaging time is written down, every load in the repo is ambiguous by that factor.)
- MISSING REQUIREMENT: stow time: 71 deg range at the memo's 0.025 deg/s = 47 min. A 10-minute stow needs 0.118 deg/s (5x) = 1.18 mm/s on the 0.71 m screw; a 5-minute stow 0.237 deg/s (9x) (primary_dish.md:39 rate, coude/synth.py:44 stroke 0.71 m over 71 deg. Gust fronts arrive in minutes. The drive is sized by the stow, not by tracking; no pass states this.)
- Porous plate drag coefficient vs open-area ratio (model solved numerically): beta 0: 1.333; 0.1: 1.322; 0.2: 1.287; 0.3: 1.224; 0.4: 1.131; 0.5: 1.003; 0.6: 0.838 (per unit GROSS area) (Steiros & Hultmark 2018 JFM 853 R3, eqs (2.12b) CD = (4/3)(1-u*)(2+u*)/(2-u*) and (2.15) CD = u*^2(1/beta^2 - 1) - (4/3)(1-u*)^3/(2-u*)^2, solved simultaneously for u* by Brent. Model CD(0) = 4/3 is the no-shedding solid plate; measured solid with shedding 2.0, with splitter plate 1.44 +- 0.4.)
- Drag at CONSTANT MIRROR AREA vs porosity (the number the porosity argument omits): beta 0.2: 1.21x; beta 0.3: 1.31x; beta 0.4: 1.41x; beta 0.5: 1.50x the solid dish (Ratio = [CD(beta)/(1-beta)]/CD(0), because a crown of porosity beta needs gross area A/(1-beta) to hold 13.85 m2 of mirror (5.02 m crown diameter at beta 0.3 instead of 4.2 m). Porosity reduces the coefficient by 8 % and raises the area by 43 %.)
- What porosity DOES buy: shedding suppression: beta > ~0.23 suppresses the Karman street. Solid-disc shedding f = St·V/D at St 0.15: 0.32 Hz at 9 m/s, 0.89 Hz at 25, 1.68 Hz at 47 (Castro 1971 critical porosity, confirmed in Steiros fig.5 (data diverge from the model below beta 0.23 because shedding mixes the wake). The fourth pass's own elevation mode is 0.068 Hz and its shedding band starts at 0.32 Hz.)
- Feathering: measured peak Cd vs facet angle to the flow: alpha 90: 1.50 (24.2 kN at 47 m/s); 60: 1.40; 45: 1.13; 30: 0.87; 15: 0.24 (3.87 kN). Edge-on is 6.2x less drag (Emes 2017 fig.7-8, isolated heliostat in a simulated ABL, Iu 8 %. This is the only single change in the whole problem worth more than 2x.)
- Reconfiguration (the leaf/branch mechanism): F ~ U^(2+V): from 9 to 47 m/s the load rises 27.3x rigid, 9.1x at V = -2/3 (isolated plate), 5.2x at V = -1 (dense canopy) (Vogel exponent; Leclercq & de Langre give V = -2/3 for an isolated plate tending to -1 for a dense poroelastic canopy. A crown of facets on compliant branches is the only element here that can reconfigure; a pumped membrane cannot.)
- Face-up stow trades drag for uplift: ASCE 7-16 29.4.1 vertical GCr 1.5 on 13.85 m2 at 47 m/s = 24.2 kN of uplift into the roof anchors (F_v = qh(GCr)Ar with GCr 1.5. The pneumatic pass's stow is exactly this: 'the dish lies face-up on the deck' (physics_hp.py:143). Face-up removes drag and creates an uplift problem of the same size.)
- Trunk section for a single stem, crown centre 2.5 m above the deck, S355 at SF 1.5: 9 m/s: 89 mm CHS. 25 m/s: 168 mm. 47 m/s tracking: 273.1 x 6.3 (Z 344 cm3, Z_req 256 cm3). 47 m/s ASCE rooftop: Z_req 302 cm3, same 273 mm. Stowed edge-on at 47 m/s: 168.3 mm (M = F·2.5, Z_req = M/(355/1.5 MPa), against a standard CHS list. The stow is worth two tube sizes: 273 mm -> 168 mm.)
- Constant-stress taper of a tip-loaded trunk (this machine is not a tree): solid-rod base 138 mm at M 60.5 kNm; d(x) = d0·(x/H)^(1/3): 125 mm at 0.75H, 109 mm at 0.5H, 87 mm at 0.25H (sigma = 32M/(pi d^3) with M linear in x for a tip load. A real tree's taper is steeper because its drag is distributed over its height (Eloy: 'the relevant property of wind loads is their divergence towards the branch tips'); this machine puts ALL of its drag at the top, so Leonardo's rule does not size its trunk.)
- Leonardo's rule sizes the CROWN's branches, not the trunk: 43 facets on 20 mm twigs, Delta = 2: rank carrying 4 tips 40 mm, 14 tips 74.8 mm, all 43 tips 131 mm (d_k = d_tip·sqrt(n_k) from d^2 = sum d_i^2. Eloy 2011 PRL 107 258101: Delta = 2 is what constant fracture probability under wind predicts; his eq. (8a) at infinite branching gives 1.93 < Delta < 2.21 for 2 < D < 3 at Weibull m = 10, and real trees measure 1.8 < Delta < 2.3. Facet count 43 from m5_relay.md.)
- How the trunk must grow with the design wind (Eloy): d ~ U^(2m/(3m-2)): U^0.714 at Weibull m = 10, U^0.667 at constant stress. 25 -> 47 m/s multiplies trunk diameter by 1.57 and section modulus by 3.53 (Eloy 2011 eq. before fig.3: d ~ C_Y^(m/(3m-2))·l_trunk with C_Y = rho·U^2/sigma_0; m -> infinity is the constant-stress limit. The stochastic (Weibull) design oversizes relative to constant stress because breakage concentrates in the trunk.)
- Inflated trunk, unguyed, at the code gust: M 60.5 kNm: r 0.25 m needs 24.7 bar and 617 N/mm hoop; r 0.40 m needs 6.02 bar and 241 N/mm. Both impossible in any architectural fabric (Wrinkling M_w = pi·p·r^3/2 (Veldman; Le van & Wielgosz — the form already used in setup_sim/inflated_beam.py:14), hoop N = p·r. PVC-coated polyester Type III/IV strip tensile 115-149 N/mm before safety factor.)
- Inflated trunk, GUYED: the 40x: guys at a 5 m lever: axial N = M/5 = 12.1 kN, needs only 0.62 bar and 15.4 N/mm hoop at r 0.25 m. Guy tension at 45 deg = 17.1 kN -> 10 mm 6x19 rope (MBL ~60 kN), SF 3.5 (Guys convert a moment (inflated capacity pi·p·r^3/2) into an axial force (capacity p·pi·r^2). Ratio of required pressures 24.66/0.62 = 40x. This is the quantitative answer to 'why inflatables need high pressure or guys': they need guys.)
- Fixed sphere, exact meridional ray trace, R 8.2 m / aperture radius 2.1 m: best focus 95 mm inside paraxial; aberration RMS 12.9 mm, r90 17.0 mm, max 26.6 mm; sun blur (4.65 mrad) 18 mm; RSS spot radius ~25 mm against the machine's own 138 mm image radius (Exact trace: mirror point at angle phi from the axis, reflected direction (-sin 2phi, cos 2phi), axial crossing z = -R cos phi + R cos 2phi/(2 cos phi); RMS minimised over the focal plane with area weighting h·dh. Longitudinal caustic 0.1414 m. R_SPH 8.2 is the machine's own value (physics_hp.py:19).)
- Where the fixed sphere runs out of aperture: best-focus RMS blur vs aperture radius: 1.0 m -> 1.3 mm (0.31 mrad); 1.5 m -> 4.4 mm (1.08); 2.1 m -> 12.9 mm (3.15); 2.6 m -> 26.2 mm (6.39); 3.0 m -> 43.1 mm (10.51 mrad slope-equivalent) (Same trace, aperture swept. The sphere caps the aperture near a = 2.5-3.0 m for R = 8.2; a bigger collector means a bigger R, not a bigger a. This is the design law the zoned membrane was invented to escape, and it is not needed below a ~ 2.5 m.)
- Price of fixing the bowl: mirror area (Monte-Carlo union of illuminated caps over the Quetta year): fixed in az AND el, el >= 12, all year: 18.62x aperture = 262 m2. +-3 h of noon: 12.33x = 174 m2. +-2 h: 9.23x = 130 m2. Turns in AZIMUTH ONLY, el 12-83: 4.03x = 56.8 m2. Azimuth-only, +-3 h: 3.65x = 51.4 m2 (Union over 3-day/15-min sun positions (lat 30.2) of caps of half-angle phi = arcsin(2.1/8.2) = 14.84 deg about -s, by 200k-point Monte Carlo on the sphere, divided by one cap. Closed-form check for the azimuth-only band: (2 sin phi·L + 2 pi(1-cos phi))/(2 pi(1-cos phi)) = 4.03 at L = 71 deg, 3.13 at el 20-70. One cap = 14.09 m2 of mirror per 13.85 m2 of aperture.)
- What moves in a fixed bowl: secondary disc 0.363 m2 + arm (114 mm x 3.45 m) 0.393 m2 = 0.756 m2, i.e. 18x less frontal area than the 13.854 m2 dish. Drag 48.5 N at 9 m/s, 374 N at 25, 1.32 kN at 47 (Secondary r 0.34 m from coude/out/optics.txt; arm from C to the Cassegrain secondary position R/2 - 0.65 = 3.45 m.)
- Receiver-arm deflection and pointing: CHS 114.3x4 cantilever, L 3.45 m: tip 0.72 mm (0.208 mrad) at 9 m/s, 2.00 mm (0.579) at 15, 5.55 mm (1.607) at 25. Self-weight of a 25 kg secondary 7.6 mm (a calibrated constant). First mode 5.73 Hz (delta = FL^3/(3EI), I = 2.11e-6 m4, E 210 GPa; f = (1/2pi)sqrt(3EI/(mL^3)). At magnification 6.7 a 0.72 mm decentre moves the image ~4.8 mm on a 138 mm radius (3.5 %). The arm is quasi-static and closes with margin.)
- Cable-stayed vs cantilever receiver arm: cantilever 3EI/L^3 = 32.4 kN/m; three 6 mm ropes (21.2 mm2 metallic, E 100 GPa, 5 m) give ~318 kN/m laterally: 10x stiffer at a fraction of the mass (k = EA/L per stay, 1.5·k·cos-component for a 3-stay set. This is Arecibo's answer (900 t platform on cables from three rim towers over a fixed 305 m spherical reflector) — and Arecibo's caution: the 2020 collapse was zinc creep in the spelter sockets of those cables, not the fixed reflector.)
- THE GOVERNING RATIO: structural depth. Same 47 m/s event, four load paths: coude stalk tracking: F 24.2 kN, M 60.5 kNm, depth 0.27 m -> 221.8 kN in the path. Stalk stowed edge-on: 35.5 kN. Azimuth-only bowl on an 8.5 m deck ring: F 60.6 kN (40 m2 projected), M 90.9 kNm, depth 8.5 m -> 10.7 kN. Fully fixed bowl recessed in the deck: 3.3 kN (resultant = M/depth. Note the azimuth-only bowl carries 2.5x MORE total force than the tracking dish and still delivers 21x less stress-resultant, because the load path is 31x deeper. Force is not the design variable; force divided by depth is.)
- Stiffness a tracking mount needs to hold pointing under wind: to hold 1 mrad at 9 m/s: K >= 398 kNm/rad (2.62 MN/m axial on the 0.39 m jack arm); 0.3 mrad at 15 m/s: 3683 kNm/rad (24.2 MN/m). The coude series cartwheel pivot supplies 0.208 kNm/rad (K = M_hinge/theta with M = cMy·q·A·D (cMy 0.16). The flexure pivot is short by 1900x, so the screw jack is the mount and its anchor, crank and trunnion compliance — never computed — set the pointing, not the blades.)
- Dynamics: the check no pass made: coude moving assembly (155 + 151 + 267 = 573 kg at 1.4 m, J ~ 1123 kgm2) on the 208 Nm/rad series pivot: f1 = 0.068 Hz. Even at 100x the stiffness, 0.685 Hz. Disc shedding at the 9 m/s stow trip: 0.32 Hz (f = (1/2pi)sqrt(K/J); masses from coude/out/synth.txt. A 0.068 Hz mount sits inside the gust-energy band and under the shedding line: every gust is a resonant gust. Rule: f1 >= 1 Hz for the whole moving assembly with the counterweights in place.)
- Weathervane stow using the offset the fourth pass already has: declutch the slew ring: yaw restoring 9.6 kNm/rad at 25 m/s, 33.9 at 47, J 819 kgm2 -> f_yaw 0.54 / 1.02 Hz. Needs a damper (shedding 0.89 / 1.68 Hz) (K_yaw = q·Cd·A·e with e = the 1.4 m head offset (coude/synth.py:31 OFF). The same offset that overloads the pivot in tracking is a stable vane arm in stow — the only good use for it.)
- Cost sensitivity to the wind case (why this is the whole design, not a detail): 'lowering the design wind speed for stowing the heliostat from 15 to 10 m/s can reduce the heliostat cost by 34 %' — a 2.25x load change for a 34 % cost change (Emes, Arjomandi & Nathan 2015, Solar Energy 115:441-451, quoted in Emes 2017 sect.1. Structure and drive are 40-50 % of a heliostat's cost. This project's 9 m/s stow trip is already at the cheap end; the expensive assumption is the 25 m/s survival, which is 3.53x light.)
- Rooftop siting factors nobody has applied: ASCE 7-16 29.4.1: GCr 1.9 lateral (Af < 0.1Bh) falling linearly to 1.0 at Af = Bh; GCr 1.5 vertical uplift. For this dish on a 12x5 m building: 1.77 lateral (ASCE 7-16 sect. 29.4.1. Also unapplied: Kz at the actual dish height (5.9-9.0 m over the deck, so 11-14 m over grade), Kd directionality, Kzt topographic (Quetta sits in a mountain basin), and the roof-edge separation bubble the dish's lower rim (0.2 m over the deck at el 12, coude/memo.md) sits inside.)
- Stow-height / chord-length lever (Emes 2017 conclusion): peak lift and hinge-moment coefficients fall ~50 % and ~40 % when the elevation-axis height / chord ratio H/c drops from 0.7 to 0.5; peak stow loads fall up to 40 % for H/c < 0.5 (Emes 2017 fig.5 and conclusions. For a 4.2 m chord that means an elevation axis at 2.1 m, not the 5-9 m the current geometry uses. Low centre of gravity is not an aesthetic preference; it is a 40 % load reduction.)

- **Fourth pass as published: Cassegrain head on a pumped membrane, hung 1.4 m to one side of a 1.0 m stalk on a two-stage hollow cartwheel flexure pivot (stage3/coude)** — gating: the hollow cartwheel elevation pivot's blades — 1.77 kN/blade at the assumed 25 m/s, 20.0 kN/blade at 47 m/s against 11.4 kN buckling; operating limit: holds pointing only while the screw jack, not the flexure, supplies stiffness: 398 kNm/rad needed for 1 mrad at 9 m/s against the pivot's 0.208 kNm/rad, a 1900x shortfall never accounted for. First mode 0.068 Hz — inside the gust band and below the 0.32 Hz shedding line at the stow trip.; survival: FAILS. SF 0.77 at 40 m/s, 0.57 at 47 m/s with the measured peak Cd, 0.49 with the ASCE rooftop factor. The jack goes to 130 kN. Stowing does not save it as drawn: the 71 deg slew takes 47 minutes at the memo's rate.; pointing budget: not established. The published 'about 30 urad under the mean wind' is a jack-path number that ignores anchor, crank and trunnion compliance; the wind hinge moment itself is computed with a 2.10 m lever where the measured eccentricity is 0.45 m.; verdict: BLOCKING — reject at the code wind. Three independent repairs are required before any number on the register page stands: (1) a survival case at the real gust with a stated averaging time, (2) a stow that unloads the pivot and completes in <= 10 minutes, (3) a lift term and a first-mode check. The 1.4 m side offset is the root cause and is also what the designer rejected on sight.
- **Third pass FACT fork: ring beam on the deck rail, two cross-blade trunnions on the elevation axis at |y| 3.25 m (stage3/fact_mount)** — gating: the blades, but with 6.5 m between the trunnions the crosswind moment arrives as a force pair rather than a cantilever moment; operating limit: same pointing-stiffness question as the fourth pass, but the load path is 24x deeper (6.5 m vs 0.27 m); survival: MARGINAL rather than failing — the only pass in the repository whose load path has real depth; pointing budget: blade SF 10 / 4.7 at 9 / 25 m/s as published; at 47 m/s with peak Cd that becomes ~1.3, i.e. it survives where the fourth pass buckles; verdict: MATERIAL — the fourth pass traded 24x of structural depth for 14 % of shading and no pass noticed, because no pass carried a wind case big enough to price the trade. On wind grounds the third pass is the better machine and the fourth pass is a regression. Neither is right; see the fixed bowl.
- **Pneumatic vine rod from one deck root, three deck tendons, mast pair for F (stage3/hashemi_pneumatic)** — gating: the inflated rod's wrinkling moment M_w = pi p r^3 / 2; operating limit: closes at the assumed 9 m/s (rod thrust ~750 N class), as published; survival: FAILS unguyed. At r 0.25 m the tracking dish at 47 m/s needs 24.7 bar and 617 N/mm of hoop — no architectural fabric exists at that number. At r 0.40 m still 6.0 bar and 241 N/mm.; pointing budget: the published 'deck tendons give ~mm of spot walk' assumes the 25 m/s case; at 47 m/s the tendon loads triple; verdict: BLOCKING as an unguyed compression rod; ACCEPT as a guyed member. Guys at a 5 m lever drop the requirement to 0.62 bar and 15.4 N/mm — a factor of 40 in pressure — because guys convert a moment (capacity ~ p r^3) into an axial force (capacity ~ p r^2). The three deck tendons the pass already draws are the guys; they must be sized as primary structure at the survival gust, not as steering lines at 9 m/s.
- **Porous facet crown ('a scatter of facets on branches with gaps') at CONSTANT MIRROR AREA** — gating: the gross area penalty A/(1-beta): a 30 % porous crown is 5.02 m across to hold the same 13.85 m2 of mirror; operating limit: n/a; survival: WORSE in force, BETTER in dynamics: 1.31x more total drag at beta 0.3, but no coherent shedding at 0.32-1.68 Hz; pointing budget: improved: with the Karman street suppressed above beta 0.23 the unsteady component that drives the peak-to-mean ratio and the lock-in disappears; verdict: MATERIAL — the porosity argument as usually stated is wrong for this machine and must not go on the register page as a drag reduction. Porosity buys three real things and force is not among them: (a) shedding suppression above beta 0.23, which is what protects a 0.07-1 Hz mount; (b) the ability to FEATHER, worth 6.2x (Cd 1.50 -> 0.24), which a continuous membrane cannot do at any porosity; (c) reconfiguration, worth another 5.2x instead of 27x from 9 to 47 m/s. Specify the crown by its feathering angle and its branch compliance, not by its open-area ratio.
- **AZIMUTH-ONLY SPHERICAL FACET BAND on a deck ring: facets on branches from one trunk, all faces terminating on a sphere of R 8.2 m about a fixed centre C; the crown turns only about a vertical axis on a large deck ring; elevation handled by a light arm swinging about C; two-axis coude through C to the unchanged underground bore** — gating: the deck ring's hold-downs — 10.7 kN of resultant at 47 m/s across an 8.5 m ring, against 221.8 kN in the fourth pass's 0.27 m stalk; operating limit: no pointing loss from the reflector at all: it is bolted to a ring at its rim and cannot be mispointed by a gust. Moving frontal area 0.756 m2 (secondary + arm) against 13.854 m2: 18x. Arm first mode 5.73 Hz, quasi-static.; survival: the crown carries 2.5x MORE total force than a tracking dish (60.6 kN on 40 m2 projected) and still delivers 21x less stress-resultant, because the load path is 31x deeper. Nothing slender is loaded.; pointing budget: arm tip 0.72 mm = 0.208 mrad at 9 m/s, 1.61 mrad at 25 m/s on a CHS 114.3x4 cantilever; cable-staying the arm gives 10x that stiffness (318 vs 32.4 kN/m) at a fraction of the mass. Image motion ~4.8 mm on a 138 mm radius at 9 m/s.; verdict: RECOMMEND. This is the wind-gated architecture and it is the geometry the designer described in words: one trunk, branches filling a volume, a spherical section cut through that volume, fixed focus on the target. It is optically free at this aperture — exact ray trace gives 12.9 mm RMS / 26.6 mm max blur against the machine's own 138 mm image radius — and the machine already contains the sphere (R_SPH 8.2). The price is 4.03x the aperture in mirror area (56.8 m2 for el 12-83, 3.13x = 44 m2 if the elevation range is clipped to 20-70), which is the cheap half of a collector's cost against the structure and drive that are 40-50 % of it. Caveat: the aperture is capped near a = 2.5-3.0 m for R = 8.2 by spherical aberration; a bigger collector means a bigger R, not a bigger a.
- **FULLY FIXED BOWL (Crosbyton FMDF / Arecibo): reflector fixed in azimuth AND elevation, recessed into the deck, only a receiver arm moves about C** — gating: mirror area, not structure; operating limit: the best wind case available anywhere in this problem: 3.3 kN of resultant at 47 m/s. A recessed reflector is sheltered rather than exposed and has no drive to back-drive, no bearing to jam, no stow to complete in time.; survival: essentially unconditional for the reflector; the entire risk moves into the arm's supports — which is precisely Arecibo's lesson, where the fixed 305 m reflector was fine and the cable end fittings (zinc creep in the spelter sockets) took the observatory down; pointing budget: the arm's alone, as above; the reflector contributes zero; verdict: CONDITIONAL. Wins on wind outright but costs 18.62x the aperture in mirror (262 m2) for full-year el >= 12 coverage, 12.33x (174 m2) for +-3 h of noon, 9.23x (130 m2) for +-2 h. That is a roof this project does not have. Keep it as the limiting case that proves the direction, and take the azimuth-only variant (4.03x) as the buildable point on the same curve. If the operating window is ever narrowed to the midday hours the energy model already favours, revisit it.
- **Any tracking machine, at the level of the load case rather than the mechanism** — gating: the load case itself; operating limit: undefined: '9 m/s' and '25 m/s' carry no averaging time. At a suburban roof turbulence intensity of 15 %, a 9 m/s hourly mean is a 13.7 m/s 3-s gust and a 25 m/s mean is a 38.1 m/s gust — a factor 2.33 in pressure hiding inside two unqualified numbers.; survival: the 25 m/s survival is 3.53x light against the project's own recorded 47 m/s gust, and the simulator that prices every design cannot generate a gust above 5.6 m/s; pointing budget: n/a; verdict: BLOCKING and prior to every mechanism verdict above. Nothing on the register page is a wind result until (1) the code gust for Quetta is read off the BCP-2021 / ASCE 7-22 map with its risk category — I could not retrieve the map value in this session and will not invent one, so every '47 m/s' above is the project's own recorded figure and must be replaced by the map value; (2) the averaging time, Kz at the true dish height, Kd, Kzt for a mountain-basin site and the 29.4.1 rooftop factor are written into one place; (3) the env's wall_shelter = 0.4 is deleted for a rooftop machine and an extreme-value tail is added to a wind model whose 20-year maximum is currently a third of the design gust.

- principle: WIND IS RESISTED BY DEPTH, NOT BY STRENGTH. The design variable is not the force but the force divided by the depth of the load path that carries it. The same 47 m/s event puts 221.8 kN into the fourth pass's 0.27 m stalk and 10.7 kN into an 8.5 m deck ring, even though the ring carries 2.5x more total force. Every architecture decision should be scored on M/depth before it is scored on anything else. A machine whose load path is as deep as its aperture is cheap; a machine whose load path is a stalk is expensive, and the stalk is where the designer's 'branch extending backwards' and the pivot's SF 0.57 meet.
- principle: A FIXED REFLECTOR CANNOT BE MISPOINTED. Pointing error under wind is a property of what moves. Fix the mirror and the pointing budget collapses to the arm's: 0.208 mrad at 9 m/s on a plain cantilever, ten times better cable-stayed. This is the whole argument for the sphere, and the sphere is affordable here — exact ray trace at R 8.2 / a 2.1 gives 12.9 mm RMS and 26.6 mm max against the machine's own 138 mm image radius. The sphere is the only figure whose focal locus is a fixed sphere about a fixed centre, so a two-axis coude through C leaves the underground target genuinely fixed. Above a ~ 2.5-3.0 m the aberration bites (26 mm at a 2.6, 43 mm at a 3.0): grow R, not a.
- principle: BUY WIND IMMUNITY WITH MIRROR AREA. Structure and drive are 40-50 % of a collector's cost and mirror is the cheap part; dropping the stow design speed from 15 to 10 m/s alone is worth 34 % of heliostat cost (Emes 2015). A fixed spherical crown costs 4.03x the aperture in mirror if it turns in azimuth only, 3.13x if the elevation range is clipped to 20-70 deg, 9-19x if it never moves. That is the exchange rate between mirror area and wind exposure, and it is a good trade in this direction.
- principle: POROSITY DOES NOT REDUCE FORCE AT CONSTANT MIRROR AREA — IT REDUCES UNSTEADINESS. Solving Steiros & Hultmark exactly: C_D falls only 8 % from beta 0 to 0.3, while the crown must grow to A/(1-beta), so total drag RISES 1.31x. What crosses beta ~ 0.23 is the Karman street: shedding stops, and with it the lock-in at 0.32-1.68 Hz that a 0.068 Hz mount cannot survive. Specify porosity for the wake, never for the force.
- principle: FEATHER LIKE A LEAF; IT IS THE ONLY LEVER WORTH MORE THAN 2x. Measured peak C_D goes 1.50 -> 0.24 from face-on to 15 deg: 6.2x, and it is available to a crown of separate facets and to nothing else. Compliant branches add reconfiguration on top: F ~ U^(2+V) turns the 27x rise from 9 to 47 m/s into 5.2x at V = -1. A pumped membrane can neither feather nor reconfigure — that, not shading, is its real cost.
- principle: A FACE-UP STOW IS AN UPLIFT DESIGN. Laying the dish flat removes drag and creates 24.2 kN of uplift on 13.85 m2 at 47 m/s (ASCE vertical GCr 1.5) that must go into the roof anchors. The stow with no penalty is edge-on and streamwise, and that requires knowing the wind direction — i.e. a free-to-yaw stow. The fourth pass's 1.4 m head offset, which is its structural liability in tracking, is a stable vane arm in stow (f_yaw 0.54 Hz at 25 m/s, damper required): declutch the slew ring instead of jacking against the wind.
- principle: THE DRIVE IS SIZED BY THE STOW, NOT BY TRACKING. 71 deg at the memo's 0.025 deg/s is 47 minutes; a gust front arrives in minutes. Write the stow time (<= 10 min, i.e. 0.118 deg/s, 1.18 mm/s on the 0.71 m screw) as a requirement, and require that the stowed state UNLOADS the pointing elements rather than holding against them — a latch or a hard stop, not a screw jack at 130 kN.
- principle: STATE THE AVERAGING TIME OR THE NUMBER MEANS NOTHING. 3-s gust / hourly mean = 1 + 3.5·Iu = 1.52 on a suburban roof: a factor 2.33 in pressure sitting silently inside '9 m/s' and '25 m/s'. Fix one convention (3-s gust at 10 m, Exposure category chosen and defended) and carry Kz, Kd, Kzt and the 29.4.1 rooftop factor with it. A rooftop machine is not a free-standing one: GCr 1.77 here, against the 1.3 the repo uses.
- principle: GET LOW. Peak stow lift and hinge-moment coefficients fall ~50 % and ~40 % when the elevation-axis height over chord drops from 0.7 to 0.5 (Emes 2017). For a 4.2 m crown that is an elevation axis at 2.1 m above the deck, not the 5-9 m the current geometry uses. Low centre of gravity is a measured 40 % load reduction, not a preference.
- principle: CHECK THE FIRST MODE BEFORE THE STRESS. A mount is quasi-static only if f1 >= 1 Hz. The fourth pass's moving assembly on its 208 Nm/rad series pivot is 0.068 Hz — under the 0.32 Hz shedding line at its own stow trip and deep inside the gust-energy band. Every static safety factor in stage 3 is computed for a structure that will not respond statically.
- principle: GUYS, OR HIGH PRESSURE, OR NOTHING. An inflated member's bending capacity is pi·p·r^3/2 and its axial capacity is p·pi·r^2; guys convert the demand from the first form to the second. For this trunk at the code gust that is 24.7 bar and 617 N/mm of hoop unguyed against 0.62 bar and 15.4 N/mm guyed — a factor of 40 in pressure. Design the guys as primary structure at the survival gust, not as steering lines at the operating wind. And take Arecibo's lesson with the cables: the fixed reflector was never the risk; the end fittings were.
- principle: LEONARDO SIZES THE CROWN, NOT THE TRUNK. d^2 = sum d_i^2 gives 40 mm at 4 facets, 74.8 mm at 14, 131 mm at 43 twigs of 20 mm — the right law for the branch hierarchy, because there the load really does diverge toward the tips (Eloy). It is the wrong law for the trunk: a tree's drag is distributed over its height, this machine's is all at the top, so the trunk follows a tip-loaded constant-stress taper d(x) = d0(x/H)^(1/3) with d0 set by the base moment (273 mm CHS tracking, 168 mm stowed, 89 mm if only an arm moves). Use Eloy for how the trunk must GROW with the design wind instead: d ~ U^0.71, so 25 -> 47 m/s is 1.57x in diameter and 3.53x in section modulus.
- principle: FIX THE SIMULATOR BEFORE TRUSTING ANY DESIGN IT PRICES. The environment applies a 0.4 courtyard-wall shelter to a machine an earlier audit already established is wholly on the roof; it tops out at 5.6 m/s and never stows, and even unsheltered its 20-year maximum is 14.2-14.7 m/s against a ~47 m/s design gust. No policy trained there has met a gust, no design evaluated there has been loaded by one, and the reason wind has never been the gating factor in this repository is that it was never allowed to be.

### Wind is the gating element for every mount in stage3, and for the membrane itself — the repository has never actually lo...

Wind is the gating element for every mount in stage3, and for the membrane itself — the repository has never actually loaded any of them with a wind.
