The single error that propagates through every file is at `stage3/hashemi_pneumatic/physics_hp.py:24` (`RHO = 1.03; Q9, Q25 = 0.5*RHO*81, 0.5*RHO*625; CD = 1.3`) and its clone at `stage3/flower/physics_flower.py:15-17`: the force is computed as Cd x q(MEAN speed). The design load on a bluff body in the atmospheric boundary layer is Cd x q(GUST). Peterka & Derickson's own method (the report the brief names) multiplies the mean-velocity pressure by the squared gust factor R^2, R = 1 + ~3.33 Iu; they measured R = 1.6, R^2 = 2.56 at their solar-site turbulence Iu = 18% (quoted with page cites in Pfahl 2011 p.5). On a 5 m roof deck in the Quetta roofscape (z0 ~ 0.3 m, dish centre 6-9 m) Iu = 0.31, R = 2.01, R^2 = 4.06. Every force and every moment in stage3 is therefore low by a factor of about 4 at the operating winds, and the "survival" case (25 m/s treated as a mean, 5.80 kN) is low by 2.3-2.9x against a code-class 3-s gust of 36-40 m/s, which gives 13.5-16.6 kN. Recomputing the repository's own load paths with the corrected wind: the coude cartwheel's blades go from SF 2.0 to SF 0.81 (they buckle) and its Tr40 jack needs 90 kN against its 50 kN rating; the fact_mount blades go from SF 4.7 to SF 0.46; the vine rod's inflated hose wrinkles at 3.5x its capacity if even a fifth of the transverse load reaches its tip at a 9 m/s MEAN; the 4.2 m flower's stem collapses at the survival gust and its pointing is 47 mrad, not the 11.7 it prints. Second, three load components that size real dish mounts are simply absent: LIFT (never computed anywhere; the peak lift at a 12 m/s mean, 2.50 kN, exceeds the 1.52 kN head weight, and the dish is nearly face-up at summer noon, which is both the highest-DNI hour and the classic peak-lift/peak-hinge-moment attitude); the PITCHING (hinge) MOMENT from the centre-of-pressure offset, which `physics_hp.py:139` under-counts 2.6x by taking e = 0.1 D on the MEAN force (peak c_MHy = 0.34 on mean q against the repo's implied 0.13, and P&D measured peak/mean hinge ratios of 10-12); and the ROOFTOP amplification — ASCE 7-16 29.4.1 gives GCr = 1.72 for this Af on q_h, so the code treats a 13.9 m2 object on a 5 m roof as a 1.7 Cd body in the gust pressure, not a 1.3 Cd body in the mean. Third, and this is the finding that outranks all the mount work: for a PUMPED MEMBRANE the wind gates the OPTICS before it gates the structure. The symmetric pressure mode is held by the enclosed air (a 40 m/s gust moves the vertex 4.4 mm and the focus 64 mm), but the ANTISYMMETRIC mode moves no volume, so the gas spring does nothing and only the membrane tension resists it; solving T grad^2 w = -dp(r/a)cos(th) gives a beam error dp*a/(2T), and with the antisymmetric pressure amplitude taken self-consistently from the same peak hinge moment that loads the mount (dp = 1.385 V^2 Pa), a single 4.2 m membrane at 20 kN/m tension is already 5.9 mrad off at a 9 m/s mean and passes 0.5 mrad at 2.6 m/s. No mount stiffness can recover that. The one lever that works is the zoning already in the other fork: the error scales as a_zone^2, so 7 zones buy a factor 6.9 and 19 zones a factor 19 — 19 zones at 20 kN/m hold 0.5 mrad to 11.5 m/s. Fourth, the gust dynamics: with the 4.2 m aperture acting as its own low-pass (Vickery admittance), 17% of the along-wind force variance sits in 0.1-2 Hz, 82% below 0.1 Hz and 0.4% above 2 Hz — so the mount does not need to be fast, it needs to be STIFF, and because theta = M_peak/K with K = (2*pi*f_n)^2 J, the pointing budget IS a frequency requirement: with J = 840 kg m2 about the elevation axis, +-20 mrad needs f_n >= 1.1 Hz, +-2 mrad 3.5 Hz, +-0.2 mrad 11.1 Hz at a 9 m/s mean (1.5 / 4.7 / 14.8 Hz at 12 m/s). A mount whose first mode lands at 0.5-1.5 Hz is not merely soft, it is resonant: at zeta = 0.02 the resonant variance exceeds the background at f_n = 0.5 Hz (1.63x the quasi-static response), and vortex shedding off a 0.5-0.68 m stalk sweeps 1-5 Hz across the operating range, so any 1-4 Hz mount locks in somewhere between 3 and 12 m/s. Finally the climate: Quetta is not a calm site. From the 1973-2020 synoptic record at station 41660, 13.0% of daylight hours carry a mean above 9.1 m/s and 33% above 6 m/s, worst in March-June (30.6% above 9 m/s in May) — exactly the high-DNI season. So a design whose pointing tolerance breaks at a 9 m/s mean loses ~13% of daylight hours to stow, and one that breaks at 3 m/s loses ~70%. That is the number the design tool should be pricing, and it prices none of it. One thing the repository gets right and should keep: rho = 1.03 kg/m3 is correct for 1680 m (I compute 1.001 at 15 C, 0.936 hot, 1.056 cold) and it is a real 16% discount on sea level; the mean Cd = 1.3 is also defensible for a cap this shallow (sag/D = 0.065, essentially a disc, Hoerner 1.17). The coefficient is right; the velocity it multiplies is wrong.

- Air density at Quetta, 1680 m: 1.001 kg/m3 at 15 C (p = 82.7 kPa); 0.936 at 35 C; 1.056 at 0 C (p = 101325(1 - 2.25577e-5 z)^5.25588, rho = p/(287.05 T). The repo's 1.03 (physics_hp.py:24) is right for cool air, 10% high for a hot operating afternoon and 2.5% low for a winter gale. Keep it; it is a 16% real discount on sea level.)
- Turbulence intensity at the dish (6-9 m above ground, city roofscape): Iu = 0.31 (z0 = 0.30 m, z = 8 m); 0.33 at z0 = 0.50 (Iu = 1/ln(z/z0), the log-law value with sigma_u = 2.5 u*. Compare Peterka & Derickson's wind-tunnel Iu = 0.18 for an open solar site.)
- Peterka gust factor R and R^2 (the multiplier the repo is missing): R = 2.01, R^2 = 4.06 at this site (P&D: R = 1.6, R^2 = 2.56 at Iu = 0.18) (R = 1 + 3.33 Iu, the constant fitted to P&D's own pair (R = 1.6 at Iu = 0.18). Peak load = mean-velocity-pressure load x R^2. Independent check: the von Karman/Vickery spectral route gives sigma_F/F_mean = 0.588 and F_peak/F_mean = 1 + 3.5 sigma = 3.06, i.e. the same load to within 25%.)
- Reference geometry: A = 13.85 m2, D = 4.2 m, sag = 0.273 m, sag/D = 0.065, f/D = 0.95 (A = pi a^2 with a = 2.1; sag = R - sqrt(R^2 - a^2) with R = 8.2. The cap is so shallow that flat-disc aerodynamics apply; the concave/convex split is small, not the parachute case.)
- Mean drag coefficient (the repo's 1.3 is sound): concave-to-wind 1.2-1.4, convex 1.0-1.1, edge-on ~0.1 (Circular disc normal to flow Cd = 1.17 (Hoerner); open hemisphere 1.42; at sag/D = 0.065 the dish is 6% of the way to a hemisphere. CFD literature: dish Cd is maximum at 0 deg elevation and falls with elevation, lift peaks near 60 deg; convex-to-wind cuts drag by up to 0.50 in coefficient (22.5% mean reduction at beta = 0).)
- Peak force at a 9 m/s MEAN wind: 3.05 kN (repo: 0.751 kN, physics_hp.py:24 and coude/synth.py:31) (F = q(9) x 1.3 x A x R^2 = 41.7 x 1.3 x 13.85 x 4.06. Equivalently the gust speed is R x 9 = 18.1 m/s and F = Cd q(18.1) A. Ratio to the repo's number: 4.06.)
- Peak force at a 12 m/s MEAN wind: 5.42 kN (Same, q(12) = 74.2 Pa. Note this alone exceeds the repo's stated 25 m/s 'survival' load of 5.80 kN.)
- Peak force at a 15 m/s MEAN (stow-decision wind): 8.47 kN (q(15) = 115.9 Pa x 1.3 x 13.85 x 4.06.)
- Survival force, 36.1 m/s 3-s gust (BCP-class 130 km/h): 13.54 kN free-field; 13.17 kN by the ASCE rooftop route (q = 0.613 Ke V^2 = 0.613 x 0.941 x 36.1^2 = 752 Pa; F = 752 x 1.3 x 13.85. Rooftop route: q_h = 0.613 Kz Kd Ke V^2 = 553 Pa with Kz = 0.865 (Exp C, 5 m), Kd = 0.85; F = q_h x GCr 1.72 x A. The two routes agree to 3%.)
- Survival force, 40 m/s 3-s gust (recommended design value): 16.62 kN (q = 0.613 x 0.941 x 1600 = 923 Pa, x 1.3 x 13.85. This is 2.87x the repo's 5.80 kN 'survival' number.)
- ASCE 7-16 29.4.1 rooftop-equipment gust coefficient for this dish: GCr = 1.72 lateral (1.86 on a wider building), 1.5 uplift (GCr = 1.9 for Af < 0.1 B h, reduced linearly to 1.0 at B h. With B = 10 m, h = 5 m: 0.1 B h = 5.0 m2 < Af = 13.85 m2, so GCr = 1.9 - 0.9 (13.85 - 5)/(50 - 5) = 1.72. The code's own view of a 13.9 m2 object on a 5 m roof is a 1.7 coefficient on the GUST pressure.)
- Altitude and exposure factors, ASCE 7-16: Ke = 0.941 at 1680 m; Kz = 0.865 (Exp C at 5 m), 0.955 (Exp C at 8 m), 0.700 (Exp B, floored at 9.1 m) (Ke = exp(-0.0000362 z); Kz = 2.01 (z/zg)^(2/alpha) with (alpha, zg) = (9.5, 274.32) for C and (7.0, 365.76) for B.)
- Peak hinge (pitching) moment coefficient about the dish's own elevation axis: c_MHy = 0.34 on the mean q (bracket 0.20-0.34); 10.07 N m per (m/s)^2 of mean wind (Half-disc gust model: one half at (1 + 2 Iu)^2 q, the other at (1 - 2 Iu)^2 q, acting at the half-disc centroid ybar = 4R/3pi = 0.891 m. M = q Cd (A/2)(2.59 - 0.15)(0.891). c_MHy = M/(q A D). Independent support: P&D measured peak/mean hinge ratios of 10 (stow) and 12 (upright, frontal), Pfahl 9; published peak c_MHy for heliostats run 0.1-0.35 depending on Iu and H/c.)
- Effective centre-of-pressure offset at the peak: e = 1.09 m = 0.259 D (repo assumes 0.1 D on the MEAN force, physics_hp.py:139) (e = M_peak/F_mean. The repo's 315 N m at 9 m/s becomes 816 N m: it is low by 2.59x, and that is before the force itself is corrected.)
- Peak lift, never computed anywhere in stage3: 1.41 kN at a 9 m/s mean, 2.50 kN at 12 m/s, 7.67 kN at a 40 m/s gust; 17.37 N per (m/s)^2 (Mean CL ~ 0.6 at 30-60 deg tilt (dish lift peaks near 60 deg elevation per the CFD literature), x R^2 = 2.43 peak. The head weighs 155 kg = 1.52 kN, so the peak lift at a 12 m/s mean already unloads the mount and reverses every unilateral tendon.)
- The dish's attitude is the sun's, and the worst attitudes are the best hours: aperture tilt from horizontal: 6.8 deg at summer noon, 30.6 deg at equinox noon, 53.6 deg at winter noon, 78.7 deg at winter 08h (tilt = 90 - solar elevation at lat 30.2. Summer noon is the near-horizontal 'stow-like' attitude where P&D's peak/mean hinge ratio is ~10 and lift dominates; winter noon sits in the 30-60 deg peak-lift band. The repo's single Cd 'averaged over attitude' (flower/physics_flower.py:17) erases exactly this.)
- Stow orientation loads at a 40 m/s 3-s gust: face-on 16.62 kN; convex-to-wind 13.43 kN; edge-on drag 1.28 kN but ~11.5 kN peak lift normal to the aperture; face-up on the deck 13.9 kN uplift (q40 = 923 Pa x A x (1.3 / 1.05 / 0.10); edge-on peak CL ~ 0.9; face-up by ASCE 7-16 29.4.1 uplift, q_h = 0.613 x 0.865 x 0.85 x 0.941 x 1600 = 678 Pa x GCr 1.5 x 13.85. Edge-on cuts the DRAG by 13x but not the normal force: only venting the membrane does that.)
- The repo's face-up deck stow needs 11-14 kN of tie-down: 11.3 kN at a 36 m/s gust, 13.9 kN at 40 m/s, against a 441 N head weight (physics_hp.py:143 says 'vent the rod and the plenum -> the dish lies face-up on the deck'. Face-up on a roof is the worst uplift case in the code, and it is inside the roof's separation bubble where local suctions run higher still. The stow that is meant to remove the load is the one that maximises it.)
- Gust energy in the 0.1-2 Hz band: 17.3% of the along-wind force variance (56.1% in 0.01-0.1 Hz, 26.1% below 0.01 Hz, 0.4% above 2 Hz) (von Karman S_u(n) with sigma_u = 2.74 m/s, Lu = 60 m, U = 9 m/s, times the Vickery aerodynamic admittance 1/(1 + (2 n sqrt(A)/U)^(4/3)). Without the size filter the 0.1-2 Hz share would be 19.5%: the 4.2 m aperture is its own low-pass, and there is essentially nothing above 2 Hz to chase.)
- Head inertia about the elevation axis: J = 840 kg m2 (dish 66, secondary+style 281, frame 10, M3+neck 97, counterweight 387) (Disc about a diameter (1/4) m a^2 for the dish; point masses at their radii from coude/synth.py:30 and its counterweight line. The 151 kg water counterweight contributes 46% of J: balancing the head for gravity costs pointing bandwidth.)
- Natural frequency the mount needs, at a 9 m/s mean: +-20 mrad: f_n >= 1.1 Hz; +-2 mrad: 3.5 Hz; +-0.2 mrad: 11.1 Hz (theta = M_peak/K with M_peak = 816 N m and K = (2 pi f_n)^2 J, J = 840 kg m2 -> f_n = (1/2pi) sqrt(M_peak/(J theta)). The pointing budget is a frequency requirement, not a stiffness opinion.)
- Natural frequency the mount needs, at a 12 m/s mean: +-20 mrad: 1.5 Hz; +-2 mrad: 4.7 Hz; +-0.2 mrad: 14.8 Hz (Same formula with M_peak = 1450 N m.)
- Resonant amplification if the first mode lands in the gust band: f_n = 0.5 Hz, zeta = 0.02: resonant/background variance 1.65, total response 1.63x the quasi-static; f_n = 1.0 Hz 1.33x; f_n = 2 Hz 1.14x; f_n = 3.5 Hz 1.06x (sigma_res^2 = (pi/4 zeta) n1 S_F(n1) against the background integral. A 1.1 Hz mount does not merely meet the +-20 mrad static requirement, it sits in the band and amplifies: the real floor is f_n above ~3 Hz whatever the tolerance.)
- Vortex-shedding lock-in on the stalk (never checked in the repo): f_shed = 1.0-4.8 Hz over U = 3-12 m/s for stalk diameters 0.5-0.68 m (f = St U/d, St = 0.2. Any mount with a first mode in 1-4 Hz locks in somewhere inside the operating range; an inflated tube has zeta ~ 0.01-0.03 and no aerodynamic damping to speak of at these speeds.)
- Membrane: symmetric wind mode is held by the enclosed air: 40 m/s gust moves the vertex 4.4 mm and the focus 64 mm; a 9 m/s peak moves the focus 12 mm (Plenum volume ~ pi a^2 sag = 3.79 m3; gas spring dp = 1.4 p_abs dV/V with p_abs = 82.7 kPa; w_centre = (dV/V) V/(pi a^2/2); df/f = -dsag/sag. This mode is fine and is NOT the problem.)
- Membrane: antisymmetric wind mode is held by tension alone — the gating optical error: beam error = dp a/(2T); antisymmetric pressure amplitude dp = 1.385 V^2 Pa (112 Pa at a 9 m/s mean, 0.66 of q_peak) (Solving T grad^2 w = -dp (r/a) cos(th) gives w = (dp/8T)(a r - r^3/a) cos(th), max surface slope dp a/(4T) at the rim, beam error twice that. dp is derived self-consistently from the same peak hinge moment: M = dp pi a^3/4, so dp = 4 M/(pi a^3). This mode displaces no volume, so the gas spring contributes nothing.)
- Beam error of a single unzoned 4.2 m membrane: at T = 20 kN/m (plenum 4.9 kPa): 0.65 mrad at 3 m/s, 2.62 at 6, 5.89 at 9, 10.5 at 12; at T = 10 kN/m double those (theta = dp a/(2T) with dp = 1.385 V^2. The 0.5 mrad spec of the zoned membrane is broken at a 2.6 m/s mean and 2 mrad at 5.3 m/s. Tension needed for 0.5 mrad at 9 m/s is 355 kN/m, beyond any coated fabric.)
- Zoning is the lever that fixes it (beam error scales as a_zone^2): at T = 20 kN/m and a 9 m/s mean: 1 zone 5.89 mrad, 7 zones 0.87 mrad, 19 zones 0.30 mrad (Within a zone the antisymmetric pressure amplitude scales as a_z/a and the slope as dp_local a_z/(4T), so theta ~ a_z^2. 19 zones at 20 kN/m hold 0.5 mrad to an 11.5 m/s mean; 7 zones to 6.8 m/s; 1 zone to 2.6 m/s.)
- Quetta operating wind climate (station 41660, 1973-2020 synoptic obs, daylight 09-16 local): mean 4.82 m/s; 33.4% of daylight hours above 6 m/s, 13.0% above 9.1, 2.9% above 12, 1.0% above 15 (My own analysis of 141,962 wind observations from the Meteostat bulk hourly file for WMO 41660, converted km/h -> m/s. The 2021-2025 hourly (model-infilled) subset is calmer: mean 3.61, 3.5% above 9.1 m/s. Take the pair as a bracket.)
- Seasonality of the operating wind (the loss falls in the best months): % of daylight hours above 9 m/s by month: Jan 8.6, Mar 18.8, Apr 20.7, May 30.6, Jun 21.9, Sep 5.5, Dec 6.0 (Same record. March-June carry 19-31% of daylight hours above 9 m/s — the high-DNI, long-day months. A stow threshold near 9 m/s costs most where the yield is.)
- NASA POWER / MERRA-2 climatology at 30.2 N, 67.0 E (independent cross-check): annual mean WS10M 2.95 m/s (May peak 3.92, Aug min 2.17); WS10M_MAX annual 15.92 m/s; WS50M annual 3.78 (NASA POWER climatology API, parameters WS10M, WS10M_MAX, WS50M, WS50M_MAX. Reanalysis smooths terrain and gusts, so treat the maxima as a floor, not a design value.)
- Extreme-value fit to the station annual maxima (with a data-quality caveat): Gumbel V50 = 34.1 m/s with values above 30 m/s excluded, 27.8 m/s with values above 25 excluded, 66.5 m/s unfiltered (Method-of-moments Gumbel on 46 annual maxima 1973-2020. The unfiltered fit is not usable: the record contains repeated exact knot values (40, 50, 60, 70, 96 kt) and 82 observations above 30 m/s in 48 years, a flat tail no valley station produces. The record is good for the operating climate and unreliable for the survival wind.)
- Recommended design survival wind (flagged as unresolved on the code side): 40 m/s 3-s gust at 10 m, open country, giving q = 923 Pa at 1680 m (Bounds the BCP SP:07 map's 120-130 km/h band (33.3-36.1 m/s) that Dlubal's BCP service exposes, with margin for the map I could not open and for dust-storm downbursts, which are convective and not represented in a synoptic mean record. BCP-2021 adopts ASCE 7-16 but Pakistan's own 3-s gust hazard maps are still listed as future work, so this number needs the actual BCP SP:07 map sheet before it is quoted as code-compliant.)
- The coefficients the design tool should carry, per (m/s)^2 of MEAN wind: F_peak 37.63 N (repo uses 9.28); M_hinge 10.07 N m (repo uses 3.90); F_lift 17.37 N (repo uses 0) (F = 0.5 rho Cd A R^2; M = 0.5 rho Cd (A/2)((1+2Iu)^2 - (1-2Iu)^2) ybar; L = 0.5 rho CL A R^2. Three constants; they replace every wind number in stage3.)
- One trunk under the survival wind (the user's geometry, sized): M_base = 39.9 kN m at a 2.4 m arm, 49.9 at 3.0 m, 66.5 at 4.0 m; an inflated trunk needs r = 0.54 m at 1 bar or 0.43 m at 2 bar for the 3 m arm (M = F(40 m/s gust) x arm; wrinkling capacity M_w = pi p r^3, so r = (M/(pi p))^(1/3). Hoop tension p r = 54 kN/m at 1 bar and 86 kN/m at 2 bar, against the flower's assumed 60 kN/m fabric: the survival wind, not the optics, sets the trunk diameter and the fabric grade.)
- One trunk under the operating wind (pointing): free-cantilever tip rotation at a 9 m/s mean: 323 mrad at r = 0.30 m, 96 mrad at 0.45, 20 mrad at r = 0.60 m with Et = 1 MN/m (theta = F_peak h^2/(2 EI), EI = Et pi r^3, h = 3 m. A single inflated trunk cannot point the dish by itself at any plausible radius; it needs either stays (which reintroduce ground anchors) or a stiff root joint with the trunk carrying only compression.)
- Spot walk from mount translation under the corrected wind: 98 mm at the dish and 360 mm at F2 at a 9 m/s mean; 175 / 639 mm at 12 m/s (repo prints 24 / 88 mm) (physics_hp.py's own lateral stiffness of 31 kN/m, driven by the peak force 3.05 kN instead of the mean 751 N. M4 has radius 1.0 m: a 360 mm walk of the spot is a loss of the receiver, not a tolerance.)
- Tr40 screw-jack stiffness reality check: 16.2 MN m/rad per jack at 1.7 m free length, not the 50 assumed at fact_mount/synth.py:125 (EA/L with a 34 mm core (A = 908 mm2), E = 200 GPa: 107 MN/m at 1.7 m, about a 0.39 m lever = 16.2 MN m/rad, before nut, bearing, bracket and anchor compliance, which typically halve it. The claim is 3-6x optimistic.)

- **coude, fourth pass (stage3/coude/synth.py, the hollow cartwheel elevation pivot on a style, offset stalk, Tr40 screw jack)** — gating: the six 0.25 x 0.001 x 0.120 m blades of the hollow cartwheel, in-plane buckling at Pcr = 11.4 kN, and the elevation screw jack rated 50 kN; operating limit: blade SF falls from the printed 6.45 to 3.21 at a 9 m/s mean and to 2.11 at 12 m/s (blade force 5.40 kN); the jack needs 16.4 kN at 9 m/s and 29.2 kN at 12 m/s, so the printed 'wind 1.6 kN m at 9 m/s' (synth.py:44) is the mean, not the load. Usable operating ceiling with SF 2 on buckling: a mean of about 12 m/s, provided the jack is real.; survival: FAILS. At the 36.1 m/s gust: M_v = 21.1 kN m, blade force 11.71 kN against Pcr 11.40 kN, SF 0.97; jack force 72.9 kN against a 50 kN rating. At 40 m/s: blade 14.11 kN, SF 0.81; jack 89.5 kN. The design has no stow that removes this, and stage3/README.md:51 claims it 'stays deployed in the survival wind'.; pointing budget: not established. The cartwheel's own torsional rate is 208 N m/rad about the freedom axis (synth.py output), i.e. the pivot is deliberately soft and the pointing is entirely the jack's. At the realistic 16.2 MN m/rad per jack rather than the assumed 50, the elevation torque of 6.40 kN m at a 9 m/s mean gives 0.20 mrad at the pivot alone, but the series chain — stalk, slew ring, deck, foundation — is never summed anywhere in stage3, and one of those will dominate. f_n must be above 3 Hz to stay out of the gust band and above 11 Hz for a 0.2 mrad budget at J = 840 kg m2.; verdict: BLOCKING. The register claim 'checked over 59 Quetta sun positions at 9 and 25 m/s; stays deployed in the survival wind' (stage3/README.md:51) is false twice over: 25 m/s applied as a mean is not the survival wind, and at the real one the cartwheel blades buckle and the jack is 80% over rating. Fix by (a) thickening the blades — Pcr goes as t^3, so 1.0 -> 1.4 mm buys 2.7x and restores SF 2.2 at 40 m/s; (b) sizing the jack for 90 kN or adding a survival lock that takes the elevation torque off the screw; (c) declaring and designing an edge-on-plus-vent stow, which is the only orientation that removes the load.
- **fact_mount, third pass (stage3/fact_mount/synth.py, crossed-blade FACT blocks on the elevation axis through F, blocks 6.5 m apart)** — gating: worst blade in-plane force against Pcr = 9.14 kN fixed-fixed; operating limit: the printed SF 10.1 at 9 m/s becomes 2.49 with the corrected wind, and 1.40 at a 12 m/s mean. Operating ceiling with SF 2: a mean of about 10 m/s.; survival: FAILS. Blade force 16.33 kN at a 36.1 m/s gust and 20.04 kN at 40 m/s against Pcr 9.14 kN: SF 0.56 and 0.46.; pointing budget: the claim at synth.py:125, '30 urad under the 9 m/s mean; gusts inside the fine stage's +-20 mrad', rests on 50 MN m/rad per jack. My recomputation of a Tr40 screw at 1.7 m free length gives 16.2 MN m/rad before nut and bracket compliance, and the load is 4.06x larger, so the honest figure is about 0.38 mrad — still inside the fine stage's range, so the conclusion survives even though the number is 12x out. The crosswind axis translation, printed as 1.1 mm and 0.3 mrad, becomes 4.5 mm and 1.2 mrad.; verdict: BLOCKING for survival, MATERIAL for operation. There is also an internal tell the pass should have caught: its own 25 m/s line prints 1940 N against 906 N at 9 m/s, a ratio of 2.14 where the wind alone would give (25/9)^2 = 7.7. The reason is that the cradle weight W/2 dominates the resolved blade force, so the safety factors quoted at 'wind' load cases are really gravity numbers with a wind perturbation. The two-block architecture that turns the crosswind moment into a force pair across 6.5 m is right and should be kept — it is the one structural idea in stage3 that scales the wind moment down instead of up — but the blades must be sized on 20 kN, not 1.9 kN.
- **hashemi_pneumatic, vine rod (stage3/hashemi_pneumatic/physics_hp.py: an inflated rod from a deck root pushing the dish's back ring, three deck tendons, three tip tendons)** — gating: the inflated hose's wrinkling moment, pi p r^3 = 982 N m at r = 0.25 m and 0.2 bar; and the face-up deck stow; operating limit: about 4-5 m/s mean, and only if the tendons take essentially all of the transverse load. If a fifth of the transverse load reaches the rod tip, the rod wrinkles at 3.5x capacity at a 9 m/s mean (M = 3.40 kN m against 982 N m) and is already at 0.9x capacity at the repo's own mean-wind force. A vine rod is a compression member; it has almost no bending capacity, so this architecture is only viable with a tendon set that is provably in tension in every wind direction at every sun position — which the LP checks only for the mean force at 9 m/s.; survival: FAILS as written. physics_hp.py:143 vents and lies the dish face-up on the deck, which is the maximum-uplift stow: 11.3 kN at a 36 m/s gust and 13.9 kN at 40 m/s by ASCE 7-16 29.4.1, against a 441 N head weight, and that is before the roof-edge separation bubble the dish would be sitting in.; pointing budget: the printed 0.8 mrad at 9 m/s (physics_hp.py:140) becomes 1.97 mrad with the corrected hinge moment, 3.49 mrad at 12 m/s and 15.2 mrad at 25 m/s. K_rot = 415 kN m/rad gives f_n = 3.54 Hz at J = 840, which is just above the gust band, so the quasi-static estimate holds. This mount holds +-2 mrad up to a 9.1 m/s mean, +-20 mrad up to 28.7 m/s, and +-0.2 mrad only up to 2.9 m/s. The translation is worse than the rotation: 98 mm of spot walk at the dish and 360 mm at F2 at a 9 m/s mean, against M4's 1.0 m radius.; verdict: BLOCKING. The rod is fine as a pure strut and hopeless in bending, so the whole design rests on tendon tension margins that were never checked at the real load; and the stow chosen to shed the load maximises it. If this line is continued, the fixes are: keep the rod in pure compression by construction, prove tendon tension over all wind directions at the peak load, and stow face-DOWN or edge-on with the membrane vented and tie-downs rated at 14 kN.
- **flower, D = 4.2 m single head (stage3/flower/physics_flower.py, one inflated stem, three ground stay cables)** — gating: the stem's wrinkling and collapse moments, and the stay-cable pointing; operating limit: about 3 m/s mean if 2 mrad is wanted. The printed 11.7 mrad at a 9 m/s mean is 47.5 mrad at the peak — outside the fine stage's +-20 mrad range entirely. 2 mrad is reached at a mean of 1.8 m/s.; survival: FAILS. Base moment 39.4 kN m at a 40 m/s gust against a collapse moment of 25.2 kN m. Even the wrinkling safety factor at the operating wind falls from the printed 7.1 to 1.75.; pointing budget: not achievable at this scale with stays. Note also that the hose is already over-stressed in the repo's own output at its working pressure: hoop 68 kN/m against a 60 kN/m fabric.; verdict: BLOCKING at D = 4.2 m. The 4.2 m flower is the one scale the wind rules out.
- **flower, bouquet (D = 2.1 m x 4 or D = 1.4 m x 9)** — gating: still the stem, but with margin; and at these scales the membrane figure rather than the stem; operating limit: D = 2.1: 12.2 mrad at the peak of a 9 m/s mean, inside the +-20 mrad range; 2 mrad at a 3.6 m/s mean. D = 1.4: 6.1 mrad at the peak, 2 mrad at 5.2 m/s.; survival: D = 2.1 stands at a 40 m/s gust (5.53 kN m against a 5.8 kN m collapse moment, SF 1.05 — thin but standing); D = 1.4 stands with SF 1.39. Wrinkling SF at the corrected operating load: 2.85 and 3.77 against the printed 11.6 and 15.3.; pointing budget: the bouquet is the only variant that stays inside +-20 mrad at a 9 m/s mean without changing anything else.; verdict: SUPPORTED, with a correction that helps the case. The memo's scale law is wrong in the right direction: flower/physics_flower.py:101 and memo_flower.md:33 claim 'the safety factor is scale-free', but the code's own outputs give wind moment ~ D^2.8, wrinkling ~ D^2.1, hence SF ~ D^-0.70 — a 1.6x gain in safety factor per halving of diameter — and pointing ~ D^1.96 rather than the claimed D^1, a 3.9x gain per halving. Small flowers win by considerably more than the memo credits. The scale-free claim fails because the stem height does not scale with D in the model (hv/D = 0.56, 0.63, 0.79 at D = 4.2, 2.1, 1.4) and the hose radius is set by the beam, not by D (r ~ D^0.7).
- **the one-trunk flower the designer is asking for (a single stem, branches forming the parabolic scatter, a spherical volumetric section cut out of the foliage)** — gating: the survival wind at the single root: everything the machine has, in one moment, at one section; operating limit: a bare inflated trunk cannot point the dish at any plausible radius: free-cantilever tip rotation at a 9 m/s mean is 323 mrad at r = 0.30 m, 96 mrad at 0.45 m, and still 20 mrad at r = 0.60 m with a doubled fabric modulus (Et = 1 MN/m). One trunk therefore means the trunk carries compression and the pointing comes from something else — stays to the deck, a stiff root joint, or a fine stage at the head. That is a real design constraint the single-trunk idea has to answer, not an objection to it.; survival: the base moment is 39.9 kN m at a 2.4 m arm, 49.9 kN m at 3.0 m and 66.5 kN m at 4.0 m for a face-on 40 m/s gust. Turning the convex back to the wind only takes it to 40.3 kN m at 3 m, and edge-on only to 34.5 kN m because the peak lift normal to the aperture (11.5 kN) replaces the drag. Sizing by pi p r^3: at a 3 m arm the trunk needs r = 0.68 m at 0.5 bar, 0.54 m at 1 bar, 0.43 m at 2 bar, 0.34 m at 4 bar, with hoop tensions of 34, 54, 86 and 136 kN/m — the last two beyond the 60 kN/m fabric assumed in the flower model.; pointing budget: with a fine stage at the head the trunk only has to hold the head inside the fine stage's +-20 mrad capture range, which r = 0.60 m at Et = 1 MN/m just does at 9 m/s and fails at 12 m/s (35.9 mrad). Stays or a guyed collar at mid-height would fix it at any radius.; verdict: VIABLE, and the wind actually sizes it cleanly: a single trunk of about 1.0-1.1 m diameter at 1 bar, or 0.85 m at 2 bar with a stronger fabric, carries the 4.2 m head through a 40 m/s gust at a 3 m arm. That is the number the one-trunk geometry should be designed around, and it is not absurd — it is roughly a quarter of the dish diameter, which is what a tree trunk to crown ratio looks like. Two further consequences the wind imposes on the single-trunk form and which the branch-and-scatter geometry can exploit: (1) branches are cantilevers in the same gust, so each branch must be sized on the antisymmetric pressure over its own patch, and this is exactly the zoning that also fixes the membrane's optical error (error ~ a_zone^2); a trunk with 7-19 branch-borne zones is both the structurally and the optically correct answer. (2) The spherical volumetric section is what makes the fixed focus possible, and the wind's threat to it is not the mount but the antisymmetric membrane mode: hold the zones at 20 kN/m or more and the cut stays spherical to 0.3-0.9 mrad at a 9 m/s mean.
- **the pumped membrane primary itself (the other fork's optics, but the wind case belongs here)** — gating: the antisymmetric pressure mode, resisted by membrane tension alone because it displaces no plenum volume; operating limit: an unzoned 4.2 m membrane at 20 kN/m passes 0.5 mrad at a 2.6 m/s mean and 2 mrad at 5.3 m/s. This is below any wind the mount designs were worrying about, so the membrane, not the mount, sets the operating limit.; survival: the symmetric mode is safe (a 40 m/s gust moves the focus 64 mm, held by the enclosed air), but at 40 m/s the local peak pressure is of the order of the forming pressure for any tension below about 40 kN/m: the mirror inverts. Venting the plenum before the survival wind is therefore mandatory, not optional, and that in turn forces a stow orientation that does not rely on the membrane holding shape.; pointing budget: beam error = dp a/(2T) with dp = 1.385 V^2 Pa. Required tension for 0.5 mrad at a 9 m/s mean is 355 kN/m (plenum 87 kPa), beyond any coated fabric. Required for 2 mrad at 9 m/s is 88.8 kN/m (21.7 kPa), above the 60 kN/m fabric the flower assumes.; verdict: BLOCKING for the unzoned membrane, SOLVED by zoning. The error scales as the square of the zone radius, so 7 zones buy 6.9x and 19 zones buy 19x: 19 zones at 20 kN/m hold 0.5 mrad to an 11.5 m/s mean and 2 mrad to 23 m/s. This is the strongest independent argument for the zoned design already in the other fork, and it should be stated as a wind requirement, not just an optical one: n_zones is set by the wind, and the zone radius follows from a_zone = sqrt(2 T theta_budget a / dp).

- principle: The design load on a dish is the coefficient times the GUST velocity pressure, never the mean. Peterka & Derickson's whole method is mean load times R^2 with R = 1 + ~3.33 Iu; at Iu = 0.31 on a Quetta roof that is a factor of 4.06 on every force and every moment in stage3. The repository's Cd = 1.3 is a good mean coefficient for a cap this shallow; the velocity it multiplies is the error.
- principle: Quote loads at a stated averaging time or not at all. '9 m/s' and '25 m/s' appear throughout stage3 with no statement of whether they are 3-second gusts, 10-minute means, or hourly means, and the whole factor-of-four sits in that ambiguity. Every wind number should carry its averaging time, its height, and its terrain.
- principle: Drag times an arm is not the moment. A dish carries a pitching moment about its own elevation axis from the centre-of-pressure offset even when it is perfectly centred, because a gust never covers the aperture uniformly. Peak c_MHy is 0.2-0.34 on the mean velocity pressure, and Peterka measured peak-to-mean hinge ratios of 10 to 12. physics_hp.py:139 uses e = 0.1 D on the MEAN force and is 2.6x low before the force correction and 10x low after it.
- principle: Lift is not a second-order effect on a dish that tracks the sun. The aperture is 7 degrees from horizontal at summer noon and 31 to 54 degrees at equinox and winter noon — the peak-lift and peak-hinge-moment attitudes sit on the highest-value hours of the year. The peak lift at a 12 m/s mean, 2.50 kN, exceeds the 1.52 kN head weight, which reverses every unilateral tendon and unloads every bearing. Nothing in stage3 computes lift.
- principle: A roof is not open country. ASCE 7-16 29.4.1 treats a 13.9 m2 object on a 5 m roof with GCr = 1.72 on the gust pressure, and a face-up object with 1.5 uplift, because rooftop separation raises the local peaks well above free-field. Any design that stows on the deck must be checked against the uplift provision, and 'lie flat and let go' is the worst available stow, not the safest.
- principle: The pointing budget is a natural-frequency requirement. Because theta = M_peak/K and K = (2 pi f_n)^2 J, a tolerance and an inertia determine a minimum f_n: at J = 840 kg m2 and a 9 m/s mean, +-20 mrad needs 1.1 Hz, +-2 mrad 3.5 Hz and +-0.2 mrad 11.1 Hz. Stating a stiffness in kN m/rad without stating J and the peak moment says nothing.
- principle: But 1.1 Hz is not enough for +-20 mrad, because 1.1 Hz is inside the gust band. 17% of the force variance lies in 0.1-2 Hz, and at zeta = 0.02 a mode at 0.5 Hz amplifies the quasi-static response by 1.63x and one at 1 Hz by 1.33x. Put the first mode above 3 Hz whatever the tolerance, and check vortex-shedding lock-in on the stalk (f = 0.2 U/d sweeps 1-5 Hz over the operating range for a 0.5-0.68 m stem).
- principle: Quote the SERIES stiffness, never the stiffness of the part just designed. Every pass in stage3 reports the compliance of its own new element — 415 kN m/rad of tendon, 50 MN m/rad of jack, 2.5 GN m/rad of locked column — and never sums the chain through the stalk, the slew ring, the deck and the foundation. The softest link, which is usually the foundation or the ring rail, sets the pointing.
- principle: Check the actuator's stiffness, not its catalogue force. A Tr40 screw at 1.7 m free length is 16.2 MN m/rad about a 0.39 m lever from EA/L alone, not the 50 MN m/rad assumed at fact_mount/synth.py:125, and nut, bearing and bracket compliance typically halve that again.
- principle: For a pumped membrane, the wind gates the optics before it gates the structure. The symmetric pressure mode is carried by the enclosed air and is harmless; the antisymmetric mode displaces no volume, so the gas spring does nothing and only the membrane tension resists it, giving a beam error dp a/(2T). Design the plenum pressure against the wind, not just against gravity and the desired sag.
- principle: Zoning is the wind lever, not just the optical one. The membrane's wind slope error scales as the square of the ZONE radius, so the number of zones is set by the wind: theta = dp a_zone^2/(2 T a), and a_zone = sqrt(2 T theta_budget a/dp). 19 zones at 20 kN/m hold 0.5 mrad to an 11.5 m/s mean where one zone breaks at 2.6 m/s. This is the same fractal argument the brief makes for the stem and skin, arriving from the wind side.
- principle: Smaller heads win, by more than the memo claims. flower/physics_flower.py:101's 'the safety factor is scale-free' is contradicted by its own outputs: wind moment goes as D^2.8, wrinkling as D^2.1, so SF goes as D^-0.70, and pointing goes as D^1.96 rather than the claimed D^1. Halving the module diameter buys 1.6x on structural safety and 3.9x on pointing.
- principle: The stow policy is an energy decision and belongs in the design tool, not in a footnote. At Quetta 33% of daylight hours carry a mean above 6 m/s and 13% above 9 m/s, concentrated in March to June — the high-DNI months. A design whose tolerance breaks at 9 m/s pays 13% of daylight; one that breaks at 3 m/s pays 70%. Wind availability should be an argument of the cost model beside efficiency and shading.
- principle: State the survival wind before designing the mount, and design the stow with it. 'Survival' in stage3 is 25 m/s applied as a mean, giving 5.80 kN. The code-class value is a 36-40 m/s 3-second gust giving 13.5-16.6 kN, and the only orientation that removes the load is edge-on with the membrane vented — face-up maximises uplift and convex-to-wind only takes 20% off.
- principle: One trunk is a wind statement, not only an aesthetic one. A single stem concentrates the whole survival moment into one section: 50 kN m at a 3 m arm, sizing the trunk at about 1.0 m diameter at 1 bar. That is the honest price of the flower geometry, and it is affordable — but it must be paid at the root, and the pointing must come from stays, a stiff root joint or a head-mounted fine stage, because a bare inflated cantilever rotates 96 to 323 mrad at a 9 m/s mean.
- principle: Reanalysis and synoptic records answer different questions. MERRA-2 and the station record agree on the operating climate (annual mean 3-5 m/s at 10 m) and neither is usable for the survival wind: the reanalysis smooths gusts and terrain, and the station record's tail is contaminated by knot-quantisation artefacts. Take the operating limit from the observed distribution and the survival wind from the code.

- source: Peterka, J.A. and Derickson, R.G. (1992), 'Wind load design methods for ground-based heliostats and parabolic dish collectors', SAND92-7009, Sandia National Laboratories / Colorado State University. The primary source named in the brief. osti.gov was unreachable from this environment (ECONNREFUSED on both the purl and the biblio record), so it is used at second hand through Pfahl 2011 and Emes 2017, which cite it by page. What is established: loads are defined for mean and peak; the peak route multiplies the mean-velocity-pressure load by R^2 with R = 1.6 at Iu = 18% (p.5-6); coefficients are referenced to the mean speed at the elevation-axis height; measured peak/mean hinge-moment ratios are 10 (stow) and 12 (upright, frontal wind) (p.18); My = Fx H + MHy (eq. 6); the correlations are valid only for square heliostats (p.13); wind force on a dish is nearly all along the dish axis. https://www.osti.gov/biblio/7105290
- source: Pfahl, A., Buselmeier, M., Zaschke, M. (2011), 'Wind loads on heliostats and photovoltaic trackers of various aspect ratios', Solar Energy 85(9) 2185-2201, author PDF at https://elib.dlr.de/70778/ — fetched and text-extracted. Source of the R = 1.6 / R^2 = 2.56 gust-factor statement, the peak/mean hinge ratios, the coefficient definitions (eqs. 11-17, coordinate system after P&D p.11), the load-case set, and the caution that the gust-factor route fails for components sensitive to wind direction or to non-uniform pressure (which is exactly the hinge moment).
- source: Emes, M.J., Yu, J., Jafari, A., Ghanadi, F., Arjomandi, M. (2017/2018), 'Experimental Investigation of the Wind Loads on Heliostats', Asia-Pacific Solar Research Conference, https://apvi.org.au/solar-research-conference/wp-content/uploads/2018/01/Matthew-Emes-Experimental-Investigation-of-the-Wind-Loads-on-Heliostats.pdf — fetched and text-extracted. Source of the coefficient definitions cD = D/(0.5 rho U^2 A), cL, cMHy = MHy/(0.5 rho U^2 A c), cMy = My/(0.5 rho U^2 A H) following Peterka & Derickson; peak drag rises 1.28x when Iu goes from 2% to 8% while mean drag rises 5%; worst operating case is beta = 0 (dish square to the wind); stow peak lift and hinge coefficients scale inversely with chord and rise linearly with Lxu/c and with stow height.
- source: Emes, Jafari, Pfahl, Coventry, Arjomandi (2021), 'A review of static and dynamic heliostat wind loads', Solar Energy — abstract-level only (ScienceDirect blocked). Confirms that Peterka & Derickson measured in a simulated ABL at Iu = 18% and reported maximum load coefficients for an open-country model heliostat.
- source: ASCE 7-16 Section 29.4.1, 'Rooftop Structures and Equipment for Buildings': Fh = qh (GCr) Af with GCr = 1.9 for Af < 0.1 B h reducing linearly to 1.0 at B h, and GCr = 1.5 for uplift on Ar reducing to 1.0 from 0.1 B L to B L; qh at mean roof height. Confirmed via UpCodes and Meca Enterprises summaries. This is the code's own treatment of a 13.9 m2 object on a 5 m roof and it is the independent check on the dish aerodynamics.
- source: Building Code of Pakistan SP-2007 wind provisions, via Dlubal's BCP SP:07 geo-zone service, https://www.dlubal.com/en/load-zones-for-snow-wind-earthquake/wind-bcp-sp.html — the map itself could not be read (the value is computed client-side and the site refused navigation); the page's reference table exposes basic wind velocities of 120 and 130 km/h. UNRESOLVED: the Quetta map value needs the actual BCP SP:07 sheet before any number here is called code-compliant.
- source: Building Code of Pakistan 2021 (BCP-2021), Chapter 16, adopts ASCE 7-16 for design loads (codes.iccsafe.org/content/PKBC2021P1, 403 to automated fetch). Fawad Najam's summary of BCP-2021 states that design wind speeds were updated and that 'the development of wind design provisions and 3-sec gust wind hazard maps for Pakistan in accordance with ASCE 7-22 and IBC 2021' remains a future target — i.e. Pakistan does not yet have its own 3-s gust hazard map, which is why the survival wind here is given as an engineering recommendation with a bracket, not as a code citation. https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/building-code-pakistan-bcp-2021-fawad-najam
- source: Quetta observed wind climate: Meteostat bulk hourly record for WMO station 41660, https://bulk.meteostat.net/v2/hourly/41660.csv.gz — 143,980 rows 1944-2025, 141,962 with wind speed. All climate statistics above are my own computation on this file (scripts in the scratchpad). Caveat recorded: no gust column (wpgt is empty for the whole record), the pre-2021 data are 8x/day synoptic obs quantised to whole knots, the 2021+ data are hourly and model-infilled and systematically calmer, and the tail above ~25 m/s is contaminated by repeated exact-knot artefacts and is not usable for extreme-value work.
- source: NASA POWER climatology API at 30.2 N, 67.0 E (WS10M, WS10M_MAX, WS50M, WS50M_MAX), https://power.larc.nasa.gov/api/temporal/climatology/point — MERRA-2 based, used only as an independent cross-check on the mean wind climate.
- source: Dish-specific aerodynamic trends: the CFD/wind-tunnel literature on parabolic dish concentrators (Energies 2026 19(1) 182 on pulsating wind loads on a dish system; Renewable Energy 2025 on enclosing a dish with a membrane) reports that the dish drag coefficient is maximum at 0 deg elevation and falls as elevation rises, that lift peaks near 60 deg elevation, and that turning the convex back to the wind reduces drag by up to 0.50 in coefficient (22.5% mean reduction at beta = 0). Used qualitatively for the stow-orientation ranking; the numeric coefficients above come from the disc/plate values and the Peterka gust-factor route, not from these papers.
- source: Hoerner, S.F., Fluid-Dynamic Drag: circular disc normal to the flow Cd = 1.17; open hemisphere concave to the flow 1.42, convex 0.38. Used to bound the mean Cd of a cap with sag/D = 0.065.

### The three analyses agree on the one thing that matters and get five things wrong, and all three miss the strongest evide...

The three analyses agree on the one thing that matters and get five things wrong, and all three miss the strongest evidence in the repository — which is the repository's own.

WHAT STANDS. (1) Every static load path in stage3 multiplies Cd·A by the velocity pressure of an unqualified speed. `hashemi_pneumatic/physics_hp.py:24` sets `Q9, Q25 = 0.5*RHO*81, 0.5*RHO*625` and `coude/synth.py:31`, `fact_mount/synth.py:26` and `flower/physics_flower.py:16` all inherit it. Nowhere in stage3 does "9 m/s" or "25 m/s" carry an averaging time, a height or a terrain, and that ambiguity alone is worth a factor 2.4-4.2 on every quoted safety factor. (2) `rho = 1.03` and `Cd = 1.3` are both defensible and should be kept; I recompute rho = 1.0548 (0 C) / 0.9999 (15 C) / 0.9350 (35 C) at 82.70 kPa, so 1.03 is right for cool air and should become 1.055 for a winter survival case. (3) The coude cartwheel buckles at any code-class gust: I reproduce the repo's own SF 6.46 / 2.01 exactly and get SF 1.02 / 0.85 / 0.63 at ASCE-rooftop 36.1 / 40 / 47 m/s 3-s gusts. (4) memo_hp.md's foundation claim fails against the memo's own demand: 4 t on a 1.8 m footprint is 4000·9.81·0.9 = 35.3 kN m against the 44.7 kN m at `memo_hp.md:160`, a 21 % deficit before any gust — and the register (`build_page.py:143`) publishes "25 m/s: SF 2.8 ... stays deployed", which is the STEM's factor, not the foundation's. (5) Lift is absent from every stage3 script though `brief.md:52` carried CL 1.0; peak lift passes the coude head's 1.52 kN weight at a 9.1-10.9 m/s mean. (6) The face-up deck stow (`physics_hp.py:143`) is the maximum-uplift case: 13.6 kN at a 40 m/s gust on a 0.44 kN membrane. (7) The flower's "safety factor is scale-free" (`physics_flower.py:101`) is contradicted by its own printed output: M ~ D^2.83, M_w ~ D^2.12, SF ~ D^-0.71, pointing ~ D^1.96.

WHAT ALL THREE MISSED, AND IT INVERTS THEIR HEADLINE. The claim that the repository has never loaded a design with a wind is false for the published fourth pass. `setup_sim/setup_sim4.py:199-215` carries an attitude-dependent drag (cd = 0.25 + (1.3-0.25)cos²α), a pitching-moment coefficient (cm 0.12, M = q A D cm sin2α) and a gust model (`--gust` 0.35, two sinusoids at 4.0 and 1.3 s, a 1.35 velocity / 1.82 pressure peak factor) — and it HAS BEEN RUN, at 9 and 25 m/s: `setup_sim/out/wind9.log`, `wind25.log`, `setup4w9_anim.json`, `setup4w25_anim.json`. The results are damning. At a 9 m/s mean: stalk lean max 1.04 deg = 18.2 mrad, and residual dish pointing WITH the fine stage running 3.66 mrad mean — 18x memo_hp.md:118's claimed "of order 0.2 mrad in a 9 m/s wind". At 25 m/s: stalk lean max 21.37 deg, coarse pointing max 30.9 deg, residual 102.8 mrad; the machine loses the sun. Register sheet 45 (`build_page.py:277`) publishes `setup4_anim.json`, the CALM run. The correct finding is not "wind was never applied" but "wind was applied, the machine failed, and the failing run is not on the register."

FIVE CORRECTIONS THE SYNTHESISER MUST APPLY. (a) Analysis 1's ASCE altitude factor is wrong: Ke = exp(-3.62e-5·z) takes z in FEET (ASCE 7-16 Eq. 26.9-1). At 1680 m = 5512 ft, Ke = 0.819, not 0.941. Its own arithmetic should have caught it — 2·0.613·0.941 implies rho = 1.154 kg/m3, impossible at 1680 m, while 0.819 gives 1.004, exactly the barometric value it computed itself. Every analysis-1 ASCE force is 15 % high. (b) Analysis 1 uses M_w = π p r³ for WRINKLING; the repository's own convention (`setup_sim/inflated_beam.py:14-15`, Veldman / Le van & Wielgosz for wrinkling, Comer & Levy for collapse) is π p r³/2, and π p r³ is COLLAPSE. Its inflated capacities are 2x generous and its trunk radii 1.26x small: "r 0.54 m at 1 bar" should be 0.68 m. (c) Analysis 1's fact_mount survival verdict is wrong by 5x. Running the repo's own `worst_blade()` with the corrected load gives 3994 N at a 40 m/s ASCE gust (SF 2.29) and 5228 N at 47 m/s (SF 1.75), not its 20.04 kN and SF 0.46. The two-block force-pair architecture does exactly what `fact_mount/memo.md:45-47` says; the third pass's blades are NOT gating. What gates it is the ring rail's uplift (overturn/restoring 0.80 at 40 m/s, 1.11 at 47, on 2146 kg and r 3.61 m from `geometry.py`) and the unsized azimuth pinion at `synth.py:95`. (d) The elevation-jack failure in analyses 1 and 3 is an artefact of a lever the repo got wrong and analysis 3 itself identified and then used anyway. `coude/synth.py:36` sets tau = D·(NECK+0.3) = D·2.1 m, but the drag on a dish is nearly all normal to the aperture and the dish axis MEETS the neck axis, so an axial force has zero moment about it. The real elevation torque is c_MHy·q·A·D + C_T·q·A·1.8: at a 40 m/s ASCE gust, 10.4-16.1 kN m, i.e. 27-41 kN at the 0.39 m arm. The 50 kN Tr40 is adequate. Neither "90 kN" nor "130 kN" survives. (e) Both dynamics claims are wrong. Analysis 3's f1 = 0.068 Hz uses the 208 N m/rad blade spring, which is the pivot's FREEDOM direction and is held by the jack, not by the blades; the elevation mode on the jack is 18.3-22.4 Hz bare, 9-11 Hz with a 4x compliance derate, and the repo's own hashemi modes are 7.1-13.3 Hz (`out/screw_checks.txt:12-19`). Analysis 1's premise that these mounts land at 0.5-1.5 Hz is unsupported. The honest statement is that the soft link is the slew ring, the deck and the foundation, none of which exists in the files.

THREE MORE MATERIAL CORRECTIONS. Analysis 1's gust multiplier 4.06 is a POINT-gust factor with no aerodynamic admittance; the von Karman + Vickery route over a 4.2 m aperture gives sigma_F/F = 0.402 (Iu 0.208) to 0.601 (Iu 0.311) and peak/mean on the LOAD of 2.37-3.04. Use G_load = 3.0 on a mean-hourly wind, bracket 2.4-4.2. Analysis 2's "the memo's 1.7 mrad is wrong by 3x" is overstated: the bridle holds translation (`screw_checks.txt:2-3`, rank 3 from four lines through F), so the stem's stiffness about F is the propped-cantilever 4EI/L, not EI/L, giving 1.79 mrad at 9 m/s for the memo's own r 1.0 at 0.8 bar at the mean stem length — the memo's number. The worst stem length gives 2.3-2.8 mrad, a 1.4-1.6x correction. And the memo's "18 m/s" is recovered exactly by putting a load gust factor of 3 on that 1.79 mrad (±20 mrad at 17.4 m/s): the two numbers are not inconsistent by 2.9x, they carry different load definitions, which is the real finding. Analysis 1's membrane result is not new and its lever is not corroborated: `brief_findings.md:158` already records a fitted 2-D FvK law in the env, sigma = 0.88e-3(q/15)^0.6 rad doubled on reflection (~3.6 mrad beam at 9 m/s against analysis 1's 5.9), with an explicit comment that it is "not q a/2T" — precisely the form analysis 1 derives. The mechanism (the antisymmetric mode moves no plenum volume, so the gas spring does nothing) is real and the magnitudes agree at 9 m/s, but the theta ~ a_zone² zoning lever rests entirely on the tension form the repository's fitted law rejects.

TWO UNRESOLVED INPUTS. The Quetta code speed: I failed as all three did. Dlubal's BCP SP:07 page exposes only 120 and 130 km/h (33.3-36.1 m/s) as basic velocities and will not give the Quetta value; ICC returns 403; BCP-2021 adopts ASCE 7-22 but Pakistan's own 3-s gust maps are still future work. Critically, the "47 m/s" analysis 3 builds its SF 0.57/0.49 on is flagged in the repository itself as "IS 875 Part 3, DELHI ... an estimate from memory" (`brief_findings.md:176`) and "(not in code)" (`brief.md:52`). The defensible interim is 33.3-36.1 m/s (q_h 481-531 Pa on this roof), with 40 m/s as an engineering margin. The Quetta operating climate: analysis 1's "13.0 % of daylight hours above 9.1 m/s" comes from a knot-quantised synoptic record whose tail it itself calls unusable, and its own alternatives (2021-25 hourly 3.5 %; NASA POWER WS10M mean 2.95 m/s) plus a published Quetta study (monthly means 1.2-2.5 m/s) bracket the annual mean at 1.2-4.8 m/s. Treat 13 % as an upper bound; the stow-loss economics hang on a number that is unsettled by a factor of 4.

THE NUMBER THE BRIEF ASKED FOR — fine-stage range consumption. The setup_sim4 inflatable stalk (r 0.50, Et 500 kN/m, L 1.49 m, EI 196 kN m2) spends 13.0 mrad of the fine stage's ±20 mrad at a 9 m/s mean with NO gust (65 %), 39.1 mrad with G 3.0 (195 %), 23.2 mrad at a 12 m/s mean with no gust (116 %); the repo's own sim measures 18.2 mrad of lean at 9 m/s with its 0.35 gust, 91 % of the range. The rigid 1.0 m x 6 mm steel stalk spends 15.8 microradians at the same load, 0.08 %. The stalk material is the entire fine-stage budget. And a stalk lean is not correctable at all: the fine stage sits between the neck bar and the head, ABOVE M4, so a lean of theta tilts M4 with it and deviates the down-going beam by 2·theta into a fixed underground bore — 36 mrad at the sim's own 9 m/s lean. Nothing in stage3 computes it.

- Air density at Quetta, 1680 m (all three analyses right; use the cold value for survival): p 82.70 kPa; rho 1.0548 (0 C), 0.9999 (15 C), 0.9664 (25 C), 0.9350 (35 C) kg/m3 (p = 101325(1-2.25577e-5 z)^5.25588, rho = p/(287.05 T). physics_hp.py:24 RHO 1.03 is right for cool air, 10 % high for a hot operating afternoon, 2.4 % low for a winter gale. Analysis 1's 1.001 and analysis 3's 1.055 are the same calculation at different T; analysis 2's 1.039 used a lapsed T of 277.2 K. All three are correct; none is an error in the repo.)
- BLOCKING CORRECTION to analysis 1: ASCE 7-16 altitude factor Ke takes elevation in FEET: Ke = exp(-3.62e-5 x 5512 ft) = 0.819, NOT 0.941 (ASCE 7-16 Eq. 26.9-1, z_g in ft; 1680 m = 5512 ft. Self-check: 0.613 Ke is half the density, so Ke 0.819 implies rho 1.0042 kg/m3 (exactly the barometric value) and Ke 0.941 implies 1.1537 kg/m3, which is impossible at 1680 m. Every analysis-1 ASCE velocity pressure and force is 15 % high.)
- Mean-wind loads exactly as stage3 computes them (reproduced, no dispute): q 41.7 / 74.2 / 115.9 / 321.9 Pa and F 751 / 1336 / 2087 / 5797 N at 9 / 12 / 15 / 25 m/s (F = 0.5 x 1.03 x V^2 x 1.3 x 13.854. Reproduces coude/out/synth.txt:6-7 and fact_mount/out/synth.txt:23,25 to the printed digit. The coefficient is not the error; the velocity definition is.)
- MATERIAL CORRECTION to analysis 1: the gust multiplier on the load, with aerodynamic admittance: peak/mean on the LOAD = 2.37 (Iu 0.208) to 3.04 (Iu 0.311); recommend G_load = 3.0, bracket 2.4-4.2. NOT 4.06 (von Karman S_u(n) with Lu 60 m times the Vickery admittance 1/(1+(2 n sqrt(A)/U)^(4/3)) gives sigma_F/F = 0.402 / 0.601; peak = 1 + 3.4 sigma. Analysis 1's R^2 = (1+3.33 Iu)^2 = 4.23 at Iu 0.311 is the POINT-gust factor and ignores the 4.2 m aperture's own low-pass, which analysis 1 computes elsewhere (its 3.06) and then does not use. Every '4.06' in analysis 1 should be 3.0.)
- Turbulence intensity at the dish, two independent routes agreeing: Iu = 0.208 (ASCE Exp C at 8 m) to 0.311 (ASCE Exp B at 8 m); log law 1/ln(z/z0) = 0.305 at z0 0.30 (Iz = c(10/z)^(1/6), c = 0.20 (C) / 0.30 (B). Analysis 1's log-law 0.31 and ASCE Exp B agree to 2 %: the suburban-roof value is sound. P&D's wind-tunnel site was Iu 0.18.)
- Survival velocity pressure, ASCE route done with Ke in feet: free-field q = 557 / 654 / 803 / 1109 Pa at 33.3 / 36.1 / 40 / 47 m/s 3-s gust; q_h on this roof (Exp C at 8 m, Kd 0.85) = 379 / 445 / 546 / 754... i.e. 531 Pa at 36.1 and 652 Pa at 40 (q = 0.613 Ke V^2 free-field; q_h = 0.613 Kz Kd Ke V^2 with Kz 0.955 (Exp C, 8 m), Kd 0.85, Ke 0.819. Analysis 3 omitted Kz and Kd entirely, which is why its numbers run 1.29x above mine.)
- ASCE 7-16 29.4.1 rooftop lateral force on the 13.854 m2 dish: 11.66 kN at 36.1 m/s, 15.81 kN at 40 m/s, 21.83 kN at 47 m/s (GCr 1.75, Exp C at 8 m) (F = q_h (GCr) Af. GCr = 1.9 for Af < 0.1Bh reducing linearly to 1.0 at Bh; analysis 1 got 1.72 (B 10, h 5) and analysis 3 got 1.77 (B 12, h 5) — both defensible, take 1.75. Analysis 1's 13.17 kN at 36.1 and analysis 3's 28.57 kN at 47 bracket the truth from opposite sides for opposite reasons (Ke in metres vs no Kz/Kd).)
- BLOCKING CORRECTION to analysis 1: inflated wrinkling moment is pi p r^3 / 2, not pi p r^3: analysis 1's inflated capacities are 2x generous; its trunk radii are 2^(1/3) = 1.26x small (r 0.54 m at 1 bar should be 0.68 m) (setup_sim/inflated_beam.py:14-15 and flower/physics_flower.py:26-27, citing Veldman and Le van & Wielgosz for M_w = pi P r^3/2 and Comer & Levy / McFarland for collapse M_c = pi P r^3. Analysis 1 used the COLLAPSE formula as the wrinkling capacity throughout (its 'pi p r^3 = 982 N m at r 0.25, 0.2 bar' is 491 N m of wrinkling capacity). Analyses 2 and 3 use the repo's convention correctly.)
- coude cartwheel, the repo's own load cases reproduced exactly: 9 m/s mean: M_v 3.18 kNm, 1.77 kN per blade, SF 6.46 (printed 6.5). 25 m/s mean: M_v 10.24 kNm, 5.69 kN per blade, SF 2.01 (printed 2.0) (coude/synth.py:33-35 with I_b = 0.25 x 0.001^3/12, Pcr = 4 pi^2 E I / L_b^2 = 11.42 kN (printed 11.4), M_v = 155 x 9.81 x 1.4 + D x 1.4, per-blade = M_v/(2 x 0.45)/2. No dispute with the repo's arithmetic.)
- coude cartwheel with a real gust factor and at the code gust — the load case is the whole finding: SF 3.89 at a 9 m/s mean x G3; 2.66 at 12 m/s; 1.89 at 15 m/s; 0.78 at 25 m/s x G3. ASCE rooftop: SF 1.18 / 1.02 / 0.85 / 0.63 at 33.3 / 36.1 / 40 / 47 m/s 3-s gust (Same expression, only the drag changed. Blade force 9.71 / 11.20 / 13.48 / 18.16 kN against Pcr 11.42 kN. Analysis 1's 11.71 kN and SF 0.97 at 36.1 m/s land within 5 % of mine despite its Ke error, because it used free-field Cd 1.3 where I use the ASCE rooftop GCr and the two errors partly cancel. The verdict — the blades buckle at any code-class gust — is robust across all three routes.)
- BLOCKING CORRECTION to analyses 1 and 3: the elevation jack is NOT overloaded; the repo's lever is geometrically wrong: correct elevation torque 10.4-16.1 kNm at a 40 m/s ASCE gust -> 27-41 kN at the 0.39 m arm, inside the 50 kN Tr40. NOT 85-130 kN (coude/synth.py:36 sets tau = D(NECK+0.3) = D x 2.1 m. The drag on a dish is nearly all normal to the aperture and the dish axis MEETS the neck axis (optics.py:16, neck 1.8 m behind the vertex ON the axis), so an axial force has zero moment about it. The real torque is c_MHy q A D + C_T q A x 1.8 with c_MHy 0.16-0.25 (Emes peak; the repo's own setup_sim4.py:42 uses cm 0.12) and C_T 0.10-0.15. Analysis 3 states the lever error correctly in its numbers list and then quotes 130 kN from that same wrong lever in its per-design verdict.)
- BLOCKING CORRECTION to analysis 1: fact_mount blades are not overloaded at survival: worst blade 3994 N at a 40 m/s ASCE gust (SF 2.29) and 5228 N at 47 m/s (SF 1.75), against analysis 1's 20.04 kN and SF 0.46 (I ran the repository's own worst_blade() (fact_mount/synth.py:71-84) with geometry.py's members and the corrected drag: the crosswind moment enters as D x G/(2 Y_T) = D x 4.0/6.5 across the two blocks, and the gravity term W/2 = 3.19 kN dominates at low wind (gravity-only per-blade 751 N, which is why the repo's 9 and 25 m/s numbers are only 2.14x apart). Reproduces the printed 906 N / 1940 N and Pcr 9139 N exactly. The 6.5 m force-pair architecture does what fact_mount/memo.md:45-47 claims.)
- fact_mount ring rail uplift — the element that DOES gate the third pass: overturning/restoring = 0.29 at a 25 m/s mean, 0.80 at a 40 m/s gust, 1.11 at 47 m/s: the rollers lift (Machine 650 (cradle) + 414 (two 250x8 posts, 4.34 m) + 1082 (ring beam at r 3.61) = 2146 kg = 21.0 kN on a ring of r 3.61 m -> 76.0 kN m restoring; the dish centre orbits F at 4 m so its worst height is z 9.03 (el 12), 3.85 m over the rail at z 5.18. Analysis 2 got the right conclusion with a 4.82 m lever that is too high. The rail must be captive and the deck must take the uplift; checked nowhere.)
- THE EVIDENCE ALL THREE MISSED: setup_sim4 has a wind model with gusts and a hinge moment, and it has been run: cd = 0.25 + (1.3-0.25)cos^2(alpha); cm 0.12 with M = q A D cm sin 2 alpha; gust 0.35 as two sinusoids at 4.0 and 1.3 s (velocity peak factor 1.35, pressure 1.82) (setup_sim/setup_sim4.py:40-42 (the --wind/--gust/--cd/--cm arguments) and :199-215 (wind_now and dish_load). Outputs out/wind9.log, out/wind25.log, out/setup4w9_anim.json, out/setup4w25_anim.json exist. Analysis 3's headline ('the repository has never actually loaded any of them with a wind') and analysis 1's equivalent are false for the published fourth pass.)
- What the repository's own 9 m/s wind run measures — 18x worse than the memo claims: stalk lean max 1.04 deg = 18.2 mrad; residual dish pointing WITH the fine stage 3.66 mrad mean, 12.6 mrad max; dish force max 0.55 kN (setup_sim/out/wind9.log final summary. memo_hp.md:118 claims the residual is 'of order 0.2 mrad in a 9 m/s wind'. The 18x gap is the empirical confirmation of analysis 2's rate-limit finding (memo_hp.md:112: 1 mL = 62 urad, 10 mL/s = 0.62 mrad/s, against gusts at 0.25 and 0.77 Hz), which analysis 2 argued from first principles without noticing the repo had already measured it.)
- What the repository's own 25 m/s wind run measures: stalk lean max 21.37 deg = 373 mrad; coarse pointing max 30.87 deg; residual dish pointing 102.8 mrad; dish force max 6.60 kN (setup_sim/out/wind25.log. The machine loses the sun entirely. Register sheet 45 (build_page.py:277) publishes setup4_anim.json, the run with --wind 0.)
- THE NUMBER THE BRIEF ASKED FOR: fine-stage range consumption by the inflatable stalk: 13.0 mrad (65 % of +-20) at a 9 m/s mean with NO gust; 39.1 mrad (195 %) at 9 m/s x G3; 23.2 mrad (116 %) at a 12 m/s mean with no gust; 100.5 mrad at 25 m/s (theta = M0 L/EI + F L^2/(2EI) with EI = Et pi r^3 = 196.3 kN m2 (r 0.50, Et 500 kN/m from inflated_beam.py:11), L = Z_NECK - 0.45 = 1.49 m (setup_sim4.py:53), M0 = F x 1.54 m (neck 1.94 m over the deck, dish centre 3.48 m at el 59). Cross-checked against the sim's own 18.2 mrad max lean at 9 m/s with its 0.35 gust: same order, and the sim is the better number because its Cd follows attitude.)
- Inflated stalk capacity, with the repo's own wrinkling formula and the axial derate: M_w 7.85 kNm at 40 kPa and 15.71 at 80 kPa; less the 8.67 kN dead load through the crushing force -> M_w,eff 5.69 / 13.54 kNm = a face-on drag of 1.63 / 3.89 kN = 13.3 / 20.5 m/s instantaneous = a 7.7 / 11.8 m/s MEAN at G 3.0 (M_w = pi p r^3/2, crush = p pi r^2 = 31.4 / 62.8 kN, M_w,eff = M_w(1 - N/crush); dead = (155 head + 260 cw1 + 409 cw2 + 60) x 9.81 from production4.log's balance line. The sim's 40 kPa run is at SF ~3 on wrinkling at 9 m/s and far over it at 25, which is exactly what its 21.4 deg lean shows.)
- Rigid 1.0 m x 6 mm steel stalk, for contrast — the stalk material IS the fine-stage budget: 15.8 urad at a 9 m/s mean x G3 (0.08 % of the +-20 mrad range); 111 urad at a 40 m/s ASCE gust; base moment 55.0 kNm (I = pi(0.5^4 - 0.494^4)/4 = 2.314e-3 m4, EI 486 MN m2, same theta formula and lever. Analysis 2's 7.9 urad used a 9 m/s mean with no gust. Either way the rigid stalk is three orders below the inflatable one: the vine-robot frame is a deployment mechanism, not a load path, and sheet 45 should say so.)
- MATERIAL CORRECTION to analysis 2: the hashemi stem's stiffness about F is 4EI/L, not EI/L: r 1.0 at 0.8 bar: K = 1680 / 1288 / 1070 kN m/rad at L 3.74 / 4.88 / 5.87 m -> 1.79 / 2.33 / 2.81 mrad at a 9 m/s mean (screw_checks.txt:2-3: the bridle's four wires through F give rank 3 — translations held, rotations about F free. A cantilever with the tip restrained in translation has rotational stiffness 4EI/L at that end, not EI/L. memo_hp.md:92's 1.7 mrad is the mean-stem-length value and is RIGHT; the worst-length value is 2.3-2.8 mrad. The honest correction is 1.4-1.6x, not analysis 2's 3x.)
- MATERIAL: the memo's 1.7 mrad and its 18 m/s are reconcilable, and the reconciliation is a gust factor of 3: +-20 mrad is reached at a 30.1 m/s mean with no gust and at 17.4 m/s with G_load 3.0 — memo_hp.md:119's 'about 18 m/s' (theta(V) = 1.79 x (V/9)^2 mrad, K = 4EI/L at L 3.74. Analysis 2 is right that the two numbers carry different load definitions and wrong that one of them is a 3x error. The repository's coarse LIMIT already has a gust in it; its coarse POINTING does not. That is the disclosure to demand, not a correction.)
- BLOCKING, and it stands: the hashemi_pneumatic foundation fails against the memo's own demand: restoring 35.3 kNm against a demand of 44.7 kNm — 21 % short with no gust, 2.5x short at G 3.0 (memo_hp.md:176-177 claims 4 t of water on the 1.8 m root footprint is '36 kN m of restoring moment, which is the 25 m/s demand'. 4000 x 9.81 x 0.9 = 35.32 kN m. memo_hp.md:160's own 25 m/s stem base moment is 44.7 kN m. Analysis 2 found this; it is the strongest finding in all three analyses because it is purely internal and needs no external wind case. The register plate build_page.py:143 publishes '25 m/s: SF 2.8 ... stays deployed', which is the STEM's factor against M_w 126 kN m, not the foundation's.)
- Face-up deck stow: the stow chosen to shed the load maximises it: 5.3 kN at a 25 m/s gust, 11.0 at 36.1, 13.6 at 40, 18.7 at 47 — against a 45 kg (0.44 kN) membrane with no tie-down anywhere in the files (physics_hp.py:143 and memo_hp.md:169-170: 'vent the rod and the plenum -> the dish lies face-up on the deck'. ASCE 7-16 29.4.1 uplift GCr 1.5 on q_h (Exp C, 8 m, Kd 0.85, Ke 0.819). All three analyses found this; analysis 1's 13.9 kN at 40 m/s is 2 % from mine despite its Ke error, because it used a lower Kz.)
- Lift: absent from stage3, present in the brief: peak lift (CL 0.6-0.85, G 3.0) = 1.04-1.47 kN at 9 m/s, 1.85-2.62 at 12, 8.03-11.37 at 25; it passes the coude head's 1.52 kN weight at a mean of 9.1-10.9 m/s (L = 0.5 rho CL A V^2 x G. brief.md:52 DID carry lift (0.67 kN at 9 m/s, 5.2 at 25, CL 1.0); coude/synth.py and fact_mount/synth.py carry no lift term. Analysis 1's 'never computed anywhere' is wrong for the brief and right for stage3; analysis 3 states it correctly.)
- MATERIAL CORRECTION to analysis 2: the coude acceptance bracket, and the code's own NA inconsistency: first vignetting at 4.8 mrad (bore at M3), 5.3 mrad (0.15 m along), 7.2 mrad (at the intermediate image) of COARSE pointing error (Plate scale f_eff = 26.77 m (optics.py:11). The beam converges into the image at NA = (r_beam_m3 - r_img)/L3 = (0.201-0.138)/0.8 = 0.0793, but optics.py:21 takes the post-M3 divergence as r_beam_m3/L3 = 0.2516 — a 1:1 relay cannot change NA, so r_m4 = 0.29 is 1.56x conservative on the radius and 3.2x on the NA. Analysis 2's 5.1 mrad lands in the right bracket by an arithmetic route built on the code's own inconsistency.)
- MATERIAL CORRECTION to analysis 2: the +-20 mrad fine range is not '3.9x over' the acceptance: the fine stage carries dish + style + secondary + M3 as ONE optical unit, so a correction steers the exit beam instead of walking the image through the bore (coude/optics.py:51 and coude/memo.md:46. The 5-7 mrad acceptance constrains the UNCORRECTED coarse error only. What IS uncomputed is the fine stage's own beam walk: tipping the head 20 mrad about the vertex swings M3, 1.8 m behind it, by 36 mm, which is relayed to F2 against a 138 mm image radius.)
- BLOCKING CORRECTION to analysis 3: the coude elevation mode is not 0.068 Hz: 18.3-22.4 Hz on the jack bare; 9.1-11.2 Hz with a 4x derate for nut, bracket and trunnion compliance; J_el = 994 kg m2 (Analysis 3 used the 208 N m/rad blade spring — that is the pivot's FREEDOM direction, held by the screw jack, and analysis 3 says so itself two lines earlier. Tr40 core A 908 mm2, EA/L = 86-130 MN/m over the 1.40-2.11 m jack, times the 0.39 m arm squared = 13.1-19.7 MN m/rad. J from coude/synth.py:30,45 masses at their levers. The repo's own hashemi modes are 7.1-13.3 Hz (screw_checks.txt:12-19) and its stated coarse mode 10 Hz (memo_hp.md:165). Analysis 1's 'mounts land at 0.5-1.5 Hz and resonate' is unsupported for any design in stage3.)
- Backlash: unverified but arithmetically sound, and absent from the repository: jack nut 0.1-0.3 mm at the 0.39 m arm = 0.26-0.77 mrad; slew ring 0.05-0.15 deg = 0.87-2.62 mrad; total 1.1-3.4 mrad against a 4.8-7.2 mrad optical acceptance (Analysis 2's finding, arithmetic confirmed. It does not scale with wind, it is traversed on every gust reversal, and it is 20-60 % of the coude's whole acceptance budget. Nothing in stage3 carries a lash term; coude/synth.py:47 lists the slew ring and the jack as 'not compliant' without a number.)
- MATERIAL: the flower's scale law, confirmed from its own printed output: wind moment ~ D^2.83, wrinkling ~ D^2.12, so SF ~ D^-0.71 and pointing ~ D^1.96 — not 'scale-free' (flower/out/checks.txt:9-11, 19-21, 29-31: M9 1779 / 251 / 92 N m and M_w 12611 / 2900 / 1407 N m at D 4.2 / 2.1 / 1.4; SF 7.1 / 11.6 / 15.3; pointing 11.7 / 3.0 / 1.5 mrad. log ratios over D 4.2->2.1 give the exponents above. physics_flower.py:101's 'the safety factor is scale-free' is contradicted by lines 9-11 of its own output file. Analysis 1's correction is right; halving D buys 1.6x on SF and 3.9x on pointing.)
- Flower D 4.2 at the survival gust, and its own printed over-stress: base moment 37.5 kNm at a 40 m/s ASCE gust against a collapse moment of 25.2 kNm: it goes down. And the repo's own printed hoop at the design point is 68 kN/m against a 60 kN/m fabric (F x hv with hv 2.37 m; M_c = pi p r^3 = 25223 N m at r 0.3424, p 2 bar (flower/out/checks.txt:10). The hoop over-stress is printed in the repository's own output line and needs no external load case. Analysis 1's 39.4 kNm was 5 % high from its Ke error; the verdict is the same.)
- MATERIAL: analysis 1's membrane finding is not new and its tension scaling is not corroborated: the env already carries a fitted 2-D FvK law: sigma = 0.88e-3 (q/15)^0.6 rad, doubled on reflection -> ~3.6 mrad of beam error at 9 m/s, against analysis 1's 5.9 mrad (brief_findings.md:158, citing tutorials/tandoor_rl_env.py:699-703 and tandoor_polar_env.py:539-549, with the explicit code comment 'not q a/2T' — the exact form analysis 1 derives. The two agree to a factor 1.6 in magnitude at 9 m/s, which corroborates the MECHANISM. They disagree completely on tension scaling, and the theta ~ a_zone^2 zoning lever rests entirely on the tension form the repository's fitted law rejects. Keep the mechanism; do not put the zoning lever on the register as established.)
- UNRESOLVED, and analysis 3 built its headline on a self-flagged memory estimate: the '47 m/s 50-yr gust' is marked '(not in code)' at brief.md:52 and 'IS 875 Part 3, DELHI ... an estimate from memory' at brief_findings.md:176 (Delhi, not Quetta, and unsourced. Analysis 3's SF 0.57 / 0.49 verdicts on the coude and its '3.53x' load factor all rest on it. Defensible interim: BCP SP:07's exposed basic velocities of 120 and 130 km/h = 33.3-36.1 m/s (q_h 445-531 Pa on this roof), with 40 m/s as engineering margin. I could not retrieve the Quetta map value either: Dlubal computes it client-side, ICC returns 403, and BCP-2021's own 3-s gust maps are listed as future work.)
- UNRESOLVED: the Quetta operating climate, which is what the stow economics price: annual mean brackets 1.2-4.8 m/s across four independent sources; treat analysis 1's '13.0 % of daylight hours above 9.1 m/s' as an upper bound (Analysis 1's 4.82 m/s comes from knot-quantised 8x/day synoptic obs whose tail it itself calls contaminated and unusable; its own 2021-25 hourly subset gives 3.61 m/s and 3.5 % above 9.1; NASA POWER WS10M gives 2.95; a published Quetta frequency-distribution study reports monthly means of 1.2-2.5 m/s. A factor of 4 in the mean is a factor of 20 in the fraction of hours above a threshold. The stow-loss argument (13 % of daylight vs 70 %) is the right argument on an unsettled number.)
- MINOR, conservative direction: coude/synth.py:33 adds a bending moment and a torque as scalars: RSS instead of sum gives 2.37 vs 3.18 kNm at 9 m/s (25 % conservative) and 22.24 vs 24.26 kNm at a 40 m/s gust (8 %) (M_v = m g OFF + D OFF: the head's weight at 1.4 m along the neck bends the neck about a horizontal axis, the crosswind at 1.4 m twists it about the vertical. Analysis 2 caught this. Also synth.py:33's own comment concedes the weight term is 'balanced by the yoke's counterweight in service, taken here unbalanced' — at survival it is only 8-9 % of M_v, so it does not change the verdict.)
- MINOR: analysis 1 misattributes a register quote: 'Checked over 59 Quetta sun positions at 9 and 25 m/s; stays deployed in the survival wind' (stage3/README.md:51) belongs to hashemi_pneumatic, not to the coude fourth pass (README.md:44-51 is the 'Earlier: the pneumatic mount' section. Analysis 1 files the quote under its coude verdict. The coude's own register claims are at build_page.py:291 and :300 ('blade SF 6.5 / 2.0 at 9 / 25 m/s', '4 / 31 kN at 9 / 25 m/s'), and those numbers are arithmetically correct — what is wrong is that 25 m/s is called a survival wind.)

- **coude, fourth pass as published (stage3/coude: Cassegrain head 1.4 m to one side of a stalk, two-stage hollow cartwheel elevation pivot, screw jack, slew ring)** — gating: the six 0.25 x 0.001 x 0.120 m blades of the hollow cartwheel in in-plane buckling (Pcr 11.42 kN), loaded by the crosswind's moment about the vertical at the 1.4 m head offset. NOT the jack, and NOT the flexure's own spring.; operating limit: SF 6.46 at the repo's 9 m/s mean (reproduced exactly), 3.89 with a real gust factor of 3, 2.66 at a 12 m/s mean, 1.89 at 15 m/s. Usable operating ceiling with SF 2 on buckling: a mean of about 14 m/s. The optical acceptance is the tighter constraint: the beam first vignettes in the r 0.33 m pivot bore at 4.8-7.2 mrad of UNCORRECTED coarse error (f_eff 26.77 m, beam radius 0.138-0.201 m along the neck), and 1.1-3.4 mrad of that is spent by jack-nut and slew-ring backlash that appears nowhere in the repository and does not scale down with wind speed.; survival: FAILS on the blades, at any code-class gust, in every routing. SF 1.18 / 1.02 / 0.85 / 0.63 at ASCE-rooftop 33.3 / 36.1 / 40 / 47 m/s 3-s gusts; SF 0.78 at a 25 m/s mean with G 3.0. The jack does NOT fail: the corrected elevation torque is 10.4-16.1 kN m at a 40 m/s gust, i.e. 27-41 kN at the 0.39 m arm, inside the 50 kN Tr40 — the 90 kN and 130 kN of analyses 1 and 3 are artefacts of coude/synth.py:36's wrong lever. No stow removes the blade load, because the head stays 1.4 m off the azimuth axis at every attitude and the moment is the head's weight plus the crosswind about the vertical.; pointing budget: not established, and the repository does not contain the softest link. The elevation path on the jack is 13.1-19.7 MN m/rad (Tr40 core, EA/L, 0.39 m arm) giving 2.1-3.2 mrad at the corrected 10.4 kN m survival torque and a first mode of 18-22 Hz bare, 9-11 Hz with a 4x compliance derate — so this mount is quasi-static and analysis 3's 0.068 Hz is wrong by 250x. What is missing is the slew ring, the stalk root, the deck and the foundation, none of which is summed anywhere. Separately, the fine stage carries dish + style + secondary + M3 as one unit (optics.py:51), so its +-20 mrad range is NOT '3.9x over' the acceptance as analysis 2 claims — but its own beam walk is uncomputed: 20 mrad about the vertex swings M3, 1.8 m behind it, by 36 mm into the relay.; verdict: BLOCKING on the survival case, MATERIAL on the load definition, and the register's own numbers are arithmetically correct. Sheets 46 and 48 (build_page.py:291, :300) publish 'blade SF 6.5 / 2.0 at 9 / 25 m/s' and '4 / 31 kN at 9 / 25 m/s'; I reproduce both to the digit. What is wrong is calling 25 m/s a survival wind and quoting a speed with no averaging time. Three repairs, in order: (1) declare the averaging time, height and terrain for every speed in stage3 and restate 9 and 25 m/s as means with G_load 3.0; (2) thicken the blades — Pcr goes as t^3, so 1.0 -> 1.4 mm buys 2.7x and restores SF 2.3 at a 40 m/s gust — or add a survival lock that takes the moment off the flexure; (3) fix synth.py:36's elevation lever, which is currently 4-6x conservative and hides how small the real jack demand is.
- **fact_mount, third pass (stage3/fact_mount: ring beam on the deck rail, two two-stage cross-blade trunnion blocks on the elevation axis through F at |y| 3.25 m, 6.5 m apart)** — gating: the RING RAIL's uplift and the unsized azimuth pinion — NOT the blades. This reverses analysis 1's verdict and confirms the third pass's own memo.; operating limit: blades: SF 10.09 at a 9 m/s mean (reproduced), 7.53 with G 3.0, 2.12 at a 25 m/s mean with G 3.0. Not wind-limited in the operating range. The real operating floor is backlash in the two bought joints (the ring bearing and the jack nuts), which fact_mount/synth.py:125's '30 urad' ignores entirely and which is 5-50x the elastic error it quotes.; survival: blades STAND: worst blade 3994 N at a 40 m/s ASCE rooftop gust (SF 2.29) and 5228 N at 47 m/s (SF 1.75), from the repository's own worst_blade() routine with the corrected drag. Analysis 1's 16.33 / 20.04 kN and SF 0.56 / 0.46 are wrong by a factor of 5. What FAILS is the rail: overturning/restoring is 0.80 at a 40 m/s gust and 1.11 at 47 m/s, on 2146 kg (cradle 650 + posts 414 + ring beam 1082) giving 76.0 kN m of restoring about r 3.61 m against the drag at the dish centre's worst height z 9.03, a 3.85 m lever over the rail at z 5.18. The rail must be captive and the deck must take the uplift. Checked nowhere.; pointing budget: the '~50 MN m/rad per jack' at synth.py:125 is optimistic — a Tr40 at the 2.06-3.22 m jack length gives 56-88 MN/m bare, times the 0.81-1.00 m lever, so about 45-73 MN m/rad from the screw alone, halved again by nut, bracket and anchor compliance. The conclusion survives the correction: with a corrected wind the elastic error is still fractions of a milliradian, well inside the fine stage's +-20 mrad. Analysis 2's judgement here is right and its arithmetic close; analysis 1's 12x claim is against a lever it did not use.; verdict: THE BEST STRUCTURE IN STAGE3, and analysis 1's BLOCKING verdict on it must be withdrawn. Putting the two trunnion blocks 6.5 m apart so the crosswind's moment about F arrives as a force pair (memo.md:45-47) is the one architectural move in the branch that scales the wind moment DOWN, and it survives the corrected load with margin — the branch's own register claim (SF 10 / 4.7) is right and extends to SF 2.29 at a 40 m/s gust. The MATERIAL faults are at the two ends nobody drew: the azimuth drive is one line of prose at synth.py:95 while carrying the crosswind torque, and the ring rail lifts before anything else in the machine. The fourth pass traded this 6.5 m force pair for a 1.4 m cantilever on one slew ring to recover 3 % of shading; on wind grounds that is a regression, and analysis 3 is right to say so.
- **hashemi_pneumatic (stage3/hashemi_pneumatic: dish on the focal circle, four-wire bridle through F, inflated water stem from a deck root, no deck tendons)** — gating: THE FOUNDATION, and it fails against the memo's own number with no external wind case needed.; operating limit: pointing: 1.79 mrad at a 9 m/s mean for the memo's own r 1.0 at 0.8 bar at the mean stem length, 2.33-2.81 mrad at the worst lengths (K = 4EI/L, the propped cantilever, because the bridle holds translation: screw_checks.txt:2-3 gives rank 3 from four lines through F). memo_hp.md:92's 1.7 mrad is therefore RIGHT for the mean length, and analysis 2's '3x optimistic' is overstated — the honest correction is 1.4-1.6x. +-20 mrad is reached at a 30.1 m/s mean with no gust and at 17.4 m/s with G_load 3.0, which is exactly memo_hp.md:119's 'about 18 m/s': the memo's LIMIT already carries a gust factor of ~3 that its POINTING number does not. Structurally the machine is capped far lower: the root lifts.; survival: FAILS at the root, before the stem. memo_hp.md:176-177: 4 t of water on the 1.8 m root footprint is 4000 x 9.81 x 0.9 = 35.3 kN m of restoring moment, claimed to be 'the 25 m/s demand'; memo_hp.md:160's own 25 m/s demand is 44.7 kN m. Deficit 21 % with no gust, 2.5x with G 3.0. The stem itself is fine (M_w 126 kN m at r 1.0, 0.8 bar).
