And the stow is the worst case in the branch: physics_hp.py:143 vents and lays the dish face-up on the deck, which by ASCE 7-16 29.4.1 uplift (GCr 1.5) is 5.3 kN at a 25 m/s gust, 13.6 at 40 and 18.7 at 47, against a 45 kg (0.44 kN) membrane with no tie-down specified anywhere.; pointing budget: the fine stage cannot recover the gust, and the repository has now measured this itself. memo_hp.md:112's own metering (1 mL = 62 urad, a 10 mL/s pump = 0.62 mrad/s) against :118's 'a loop at a few hertz' is a 30x rate shortfall at 1 mrad and 3 Hz; and setup_sim/out/wind9.log, running the fourth pass's fine stage against gusts at 0.25 and 0.77 Hz, measures a residual of 3.66 mrad mean at 9 m/s against the memo's claimed 'order 0.2 mrad'. Analysis 2 argued this correctly from first principles without noticing the empirical confirmation two directories away.; verdict: BLOCKING, on analysis 2's finding, which is the single strongest result in all three analyses because it is purely internal and needs no external load case. The register plate (build_page.py:143) publishes '25 m/s: SF 2.8 ... stays deployed' — that safety factor is the STEM's against its wrinkling moment, and the sentence reads as if it covered the machine. It does not cover the foundation, which is 21 % short of the memo's own demand, and it does not cover the stow, which maximises the load it was chosen to shed. Fixes: 5.1 t of ballast for the mean case and 10 t for a gusted one on the same footprint (or a wider one), and a stow that is edge-on with the membrane vented, not face-up.
- **setup_sim4, the published inflatable-stalk machine (stage3/setup_sim/setup_sim4.py, register sheet 45): one r 0.50 m fabric stalk grown like an everting vine, carrying the yoke, the hollow neck and the head** — gating: the stalk's own bending compliance, and it is the fine stage's entire range budget. Wrinkling is the second limit.; operating limit: about a 9 m/s mean at the design's 80 kPa, and below the operating wind at the simulation's 40 kPa. Capacity: M_w = pi p r^3/2 = 7.85 kN m at 40 kPa and 15.71 at 80 kPa, derated by the 8.67 kN dead load (head 155 + counterweights 260 and 409 + 60 kg, from production4.log's own balance line) through the crushing force pi p r^2 to 5.69 and 13.54 kN m — a face-on drag of 1.63 / 3.89 kN at the dish centre's 3.48 m lever, i.e. 13.3 / 20.5 m/s instantaneous or a 7.7 / 11.8 m/s MEAN at G 3.0.; survival: FAILS, and the repository has already published the run that shows it failing. setup_sim/out/wind25.log: stalk lean max 21.37 deg = 373 mrad, coarse pointing max 30.87 deg, residual dish pointing 102.76 mrad, dish force max 6.60 kN. The machine loses the sun and the stalk is far past wrinkling. And this is at a 25 m/s MEAN with a 1.35 velocity gust factor — well below any code gust.; pointing budget: THE NUMBER THE BRIEF ASKED FOR. With EI = Et pi r^3 = 196 kN m2 and L 1.49 m, the stalk spends 13.0 mrad of the fine stage's +-20 mrad at a 9 m/s mean with NO gust (65 % of the range), 39.1 mrad with G 3.0 (195 %), 23.2 mrad at a 12 m/s mean with no gust (116 %), 100.5 mrad at 25 m/s. The repository's OWN simulation measures 18.2 mrad of lean at 9 m/s with its 0.35 gust — 91 % of the range — and a residual dish pointing of 3.66 mrad. For contrast the rigid 1.0 m x 6 mm steel stalk spends 15.8 microradians at the same load, 0.08 %. And worse: a stalk lean is not correctable at all, because the fine stage sits between the neck bar and the head, ABOVE M4 on the yoke, so a lean of theta tilts M4 with it and deviates the beam going down the stalk by 2 theta into a fixed underground bore — 36 mrad at the sim's own 9 m/s lean. Nothing in stage3 computes that.; verdict: BLOCKING, and this is the finding all three analyses missed by asserting that the repository never applied a wind. It did: setup_sim4.py:40-42 and :199-215 carry an attitude-dependent Cd (1.3 face-on, 0.25 edge-on), a hinge-moment coefficient (cm 0.12, sin 2 alpha) and a two-sinusoid gust model, and out/wind9.log and out/wind25.log are the runs. Register sheet 45 (build_page.py:277) publishes setup4_anim.json, the --wind 0 run, and quotes its microradian residuals. The honest register entry is: the inflatable stalk holds the head to 91 % of the fine stage's range at a 9 m/s mean and loses the sun at 25 m/s; the vine hose is a deployment mechanism, not a load path, and the machine must lock onto something rigid once grown. That sentence is already supported by the repository's own evidence and needs no new analysis.
- **flower, D 4.2 m single head (stage3/flower), and the bouquet variants** — gating: the stem's collapse moment at D 4.2, and the fabric hoop stress, which is already over-strength in the repo's own printed output; operating limit: D 4.2 is out of any sensible pointing budget: 11.7 mrad on three ground stays at a 9 m/s mean as printed, ~35 mrad with a real gust factor. D 2.1 and D 1.4 print 3.0 and 1.5 mrad, i.e. 9 and 4.5 mrad gusted, and are the only variants inside a +-20 mrad fine range.; survival: D 4.2 FAILS: base moment 37.5 kN m at a 40 m/s ASCE rooftop gust and 41.2 kN m at a 25 m/s mean with G 3.0, against a collapse moment of 25.2 kN m. It stands only at the repo's own un-gusted 25 m/s (13.7 kN m). Analysis 1's 39.4 kN m at 40 m/s is 5 % high from its Ke error; the verdict is unchanged.; pointing budget: not achievable at D 4.2 with stays. And the D 4.2 hose is already over-stressed at its own design point: flower/out/checks.txt:10 prints hoop 68 kN/m against the 60 kN/m fabric declared at physics_flower.py:20. That is the repository stating its own violation and needs no external load case.; verdict: BLOCKING at D 4.2, SUPPORTED for the bouquet, with a correction that helps the case. The memo's 'the safety factor is scale-free' (physics_flower.py:101, memo_flower.md:33) is contradicted by its own printed outputs: over D 4.2 -> 2.1 the wind moment goes as D^2.83 and the wrinkling moment as D^2.12, so SF goes as D^-0.71, and pointing as D^1.96 rather than the claimed D^1. Halving the module diameter buys 1.6x on structural safety and 3.9x on pointing. Analysis 1's correction here is right, verifiable line-by-line from checks.txt, and stronger than the memo credits — small heads win by more than the branch claims. The scale law fails because the stem height does not scale with D in the model (hv/D 0.56, 0.63, 0.79) and the hose radius is set by the beam, not by D.
- **the pumped membrane primary itself (the other fork's optics; the wind case is contested between analysis 1 and the env)** — gating: the membrane's antisymmetric wind mode — real, but already modelled, and the disagreement is about the scaling law, not the existence; operating limit: the two routes agree in magnitude and disagree completely on tension. The env's fitted 2-D FvK law (brief_findings.md:158, tandoor_rl_env.py:699-703) gives sigma = 0.88e-3 (q/15)^0.6 rad, doubled on reflection: ~1.8 mrad of slope and ~3.6 mrad of beam error at 9 m/s. Analysis 1's pure-tension solution theta = dp a/(2T) with dp = 1.385 V^2 gives 5.89 mrad of beam error at 9 m/s at T 20 kN/m. A factor 1.6 apart at 9 m/s — good agreement for two independent derivations, and both cross 0.5 mrad somewhere between 1.9 and 3.4 m/s.; survival: venting the plenum before a survival wind is mandatory in both models, and that forces a stow orientation that does not rely on the membrane holding shape — which in turn condemns the face-up deck stow of physics_hp.py:143 twice over.; pointing budget: the mechanism analysis 1 identifies is real and is the right way to think about it: the symmetric pressure mode is carried by the enclosed air (a gas spring), the antisymmetric mode displaces no volume, so the gas spring contributes nothing and only the membrane's own stiffness resists. That belongs on the register. What does NOT belong there yet is the tension scaling.; verdict: MATERIAL, and analysis 1's claim of novelty must be withdrawn. The repository already carries a wind-membrane-figure law and its code comment explicitly rejects the q a/2T form ('not q a/2T') that analysis 1 derives — so the finding is a corroboration of an existing model, not a discovery, and the two must be reconciled before either is quoted. The consequence matters: the whole zoning lever (beam error ~ a_zone^2, '19 zones at 20 kN/m hold 0.5 mrad to 11.5 m/s') follows only from the tension form, and the fitted FvK law is tension-independent as coded. Do not put 'n_zones is set by the wind' on the register as established. Do put on it that the antisymmetric mode is unheld by the plenum and that the membrane, not the mount, sets the low-wind optical limit — that much both models agree on.

- principle: State the averaging time, the height and the terrain, or the number means nothing. This is the one principle all three analyses share and it is correct: 'wind 9 m/s' and 'wind 25 m/s' appear throughout stage3 with no load definition, and the ambiguity is worth a factor 2.4-4.2 on every safety factor in the branch. Fix the definition once, in one place, and restate every existing number against it; most of the arithmetic in stage3 is right and only its input is undeclared.
- principle: Use a load gust factor, not a point gust factor. A 4.2 m aperture is its own low-pass: the von Karman spectrum through the Vickery admittance gives sigma_F/F = 0.40 (Iu 0.208) to 0.60 (Iu 0.311) and a peak/mean on the LOAD of 2.4-3.0, where the point-gust (1+3.33 Iu)^2 gives 2.9-4.2. Carry G_load = 3.0 on a mean-hourly wind for this dish on this roof and say so; 4.06 is 35 % conservative and the conservatism is invisible to the reader.
- principle: Before disputing a repository's number, run the repository's own routine. Two of the three analyses' blocking verdicts dissolve when their load is fed through the code they are criticising: fact_mount's worst_blade() gives 3994 N at a 40 m/s gust (SF 2.29), not the 20.04 kN and SF 0.46 asserted, and coude's jack demand collapses from 130 kN to 27-41 kN once the geometrically wrong lever is fixed rather than propagated. An independent recomputation that does not reproduce the target's printed numbers first is not independent, it is a different calculation.
- principle: Check the units of a code factor against a physical quantity you already know. ASCE 7-16's Ke takes elevation in feet; read in metres it gives 0.941 at Quetta, which implies an air density of 1.154 kg/m3 at 1680 m — impossible, and the analysis that made the error had already computed 1.001 kg/m3 four lines earlier. Any code coefficient that reduces to a density, a pressure or a speed should be back-substituted before it is used.
- principle: Take the capacity formula from the file you are auditing. The branch is consistent throughout on M_wrinkle = pi p r^3/2 and M_collapse = pi p r^3 (inflated_beam.py:14-15, physics_flower.py:26-27, citing Veldman, Le van & Wielgosz, Comer & Levy). An audit that substitutes the collapse formula for wrinkling doubles every inflated capacity and shrinks every trunk radius by 26 %, in the direction that flatters the design.
- principle: Compute the moment with the geometry, not with the nearest available length. coude/synth.py:36 multiplies the drag by 2.1 m because 2.1 m is the distance from the dish to the neck — but that distance lies ALONG the drag, and the dish axis meets the neck axis, so an axial force has zero moment about it. A dish's elevation torque comes from the hinge-moment coefficient and the in-plane force component, nothing else. The error was 4-6x conservative here; the same slip in the other direction is what un-conservative design looks like.
- principle: A flexure's spring is not the mount's stiffness in its own freedom direction. The cartwheel's 208 N m/rad and the FACT trunnion's 167 N m/rad are what those pivots deliberately do NOT resist; the actuator holds that axis. Computing a first mode from the flexure spring gives 0.068 Hz where the jack gives 18-22 Hz — a 250x error that then drives a whole principle about resonance and vortex lock-in. Ask what carries the load before computing what it does under it.
- principle: Quote the series stiffness or say the chain is unsummed. Every pass in stage3 reports the compliance of the element it just designed — 415 kN m/rad of tendon, 50 MN m/rad of jack, 90 MN m/rad of locked stage — and none sums through the stalk, the slew ring, the deck and the foundation. The right conclusion for the fourth pass is not a number, it is 'the softest link is a bought bearing and a foundation that are not in the files'; write that instead of a stiffness that omits them.
- principle: Backlash sets the floor and does not scale with wind. 0.1-0.3 mm in a Tr40 nut at the coude's 0.39 m arm is 0.26-0.77 mrad, and 0.05-0.15 deg in a slew ring is 0.87-2.62 mrad. Against a 4.8-7.2 mrad optical acceptance that is 20-60 % of the whole budget, spent by two components that no pass sized, and traversed on every gust reversal. A microradian flexure claim in a chain containing one bought rotary joint is a claim about the wrong element.
- principle: Compute the acceptance from the relay's own apertures before adopting the relay. The fourth pass's f_eff of 26.77 m makes the machine 6.5x more pointing-sensitive than the one it replaced and caps the coarse error at 4.8-7.2 mrad in the r 0.33 m pivot bore, a number the optics file never prints and every memo silently replaces with an inherited +-20 mrad. Never carry a pointing budget across an optics change; re-derive it.
- principle: The stow is a load case, not a footnote, and 'lie flat and let go' is the worst one available. Face-up on a roof is the maximum-uplift orientation — 13.6 kN at a 40 m/s gust on a 0.44 kN membrane by ASCE 29.4.1 — and it is inside the roof's separation bubble where local suctions run higher still. Edge-on with the membrane vented is the only orientation that removes the load, and it needs a tie-down rated at the survival gust and a stow time short enough to beat a gust front.
- principle: Lift is not second order on a dish that tracks the sun, and it is already in the brief. brief.md:52 carried CL 1.0 and the whole of stage3 dropped it. Peak lift passes the coude head's own 1.52 kN weight at a mean of 9-11 m/s, which reverses every unilateral tendon and unloads every bearing at exactly the hours the machine is meant to work.
- principle: Search the repository for the evidence before declaring it absent. All three analyses opened with the claim that no design here has ever been loaded with a wind. setup_sim4.py has an attitude-dependent Cd, a hinge-moment coefficient and a gust model; out/wind9.log and out/wind25.log are the runs; and they show the published machine holding 91 % of its fine-stage range at a 9 m/s mean and losing the sun at 25. 'It was never checked' and 'it was checked, it failed, and the failure is not on the register' are different findings, and only the second one is true.
- principle: Publish the failing run beside the clean one. Register sheet 45 shows the calm simulation and quotes its microradian residual; the two wind runs sit unpublished in the same directory with a 3.66 mrad residual and a 21 degree lean. A register that shows only the case the machine passes is not a record, and the branch's own habit elsewhere — fact_mount/memo.md keeping the first draft's overloaded blades beside the correction — is the standard to hold it to.
- principle: Distinguish a design choice from a code value, and an estimate from memory from either. The '47 m/s 50-year gust' that carries one analysis's whole verdict is flagged in the repository itself as an IS 875 estimate for DELHI recalled from memory and 'not in code'. Four independent attempts have now failed to retrieve the BCP SP:07 value for Quetta; the defensible interim is the 33.3-36.1 m/s band the code's own service exposes, with 40 m/s as declared engineering margin. Until the map sheet is read, no safety factor in stage3 is code-compliant and none should be described as such.
- principle: Price the stow policy on a wind climate you can defend. The argument is right — a threshold that trips at a 9 m/s mean costs a share of the highest-DNI hours, and that share belongs in the design tool beside efficiency and shading — but the number is unsettled by a factor of four across the four sources available (annual mean 1.2 to 4.8 m/s), and the fraction of hours above a threshold moves by twenty. Put the sensitivity in the cost model, not the point estimate.

- source: ASCE 7-16: Eq. 26.9-1 Ke = e^(-0.0000362 z_g) with z_g in FEET (the source of analysis 1's 15 % error); Eq. 26.10-1 Kz = 2.01(z/zg)^(2/alpha) with (alpha, zg) = (9.5, 274.32) Exp C and (7.0, 365.76) Exp B; Table 26.11-1 Iz = c(10/z)^(1/6); Section 29.4.1 rooftop structures and equipment, GCr = 1.9 for Af < 0.1 B h reducing linearly to 1.0 at B h, with 1.5 for uplift. Used as the independent check on the repo's Cd x q(mean) route.
- source: Peterka & Derickson, SAND92-7009 (1992), the report the brief names and which none of us could fetch (osti.gov refused). Used only at second hand through Emes 2017 for the coefficient definitions cD = D/(0.5 rho U^2 A) and cMHy = MHy/(0.5 rho U^2 A c), the mean/peak load split, and the R = 1 + ~3.33 Iu gust-factor route (R 1.6 at their Iu 0.18). https://www.osti.gov/biblio/7105290
- source: Emes, Yu, Jafari, Ghanadi & Arjomandi (2017), 'Experimental Investigation of the Wind Loads on Heliostats', APSRC — peak cD 1.50 at alpha 90 in an Iu 8 % ABL, peak cMHy <= 0.16, peak cL <= 0.85, and the H/c stow trend. Source of the c_MHy 0.16 I use in the corrected elevation torque; the repository's own setup_sim4.py:42 independently uses cm 0.12. https://apvi.org.au/solar-research-conference/wp-content/uploads/2018/01/Matthew-Emes-Experimental-Investigation-of-the-Wind-Loads-on-Heliostats.pdf
- source: Building Code of Pakistan: BCP SP:07 basic wind velocities are exposed by Dlubal's geo-zone page only as 120 and 130 km/h with no Quetta value (the map is computed client-side); https://www.dlubal.com/en/load-zones-for-snow-wind-earthquake/wind-bcp-sp.html. BCP-2021 chapter 16 adopts ASCE 7 but ICC returns 403 to automated fetch; Pakistan's own 3-s gust hazard maps are described as future work. UNRESOLVED after four independent attempts (three analyses plus mine): no code speed for Quetta should be quoted as code-compliant.
